This is a genuinely CR-specific piece of the height pattern, and \emph{what makes it so is the handover and not a rate}: a rate the two lengths share cancels from their ratio, while the epoch at which the plasma is handed to the expanding leg is fixed by the construction and is not a parameter. So the signature is not an artefact of laying the standard radiation-governed $\rs$ on a rate it was not accumulated on, the calculation belonging to neither framework this paper warns against (\S\ref{sec:intro}). 

One thing about this signature is settled and one is not, and it is essential to keep them apart. 

Settled: the effect in $r_D$ is genuinely CR-specific and does not dissolve under the density that would ordinarily rescale it---$r_D\propto\omega_b^{-0.31}$, so a percent-level $\omega_b$ (fixed by primordial deuterium and the peak-height ratio) shifts it by well under a percent (receipt: \texttt{damping\_reabsorption.py}\rcpt{P15_damping_reabsorption}). \emph{And what the signature does to the observed high-$\ell$ power is measured, with a verdict that is
neither of the two a reader expects.} Scaling the diffusion length by the instrument's own measured ratio
multiplies the damping by a Gaussian in $\ell$, and a tilt is a power law --- so over a restricted high-$\ell$
range the one is well approximated by the other. \emph{Refitting amplitude and tilt therefore absorbs the
signature almost entirely in shape}, and what it costs is a shift in the inferred tilt. \emph{Both scale with
$r^{2}-1$, and at the adjudicated ratio that factor is small}: with both lengths carried to the visibility
peak, $r=0.9918$, the signature costs $1.70$ in $\chi^{2}$ over the $185$ full-range bins---under a hundredth
per bin---and a tilt of $+0.0034$ absorbs seven tenths of it. \emph{Terminating the diffusion integral at a
sharp recombination cut instead}, $r=1.0231$, \emph{gives $12.6$ and a tilt of $-0.0093$}, and a ratio of $1.082$ gives $160$ and $-0.034$ on the same base and the same damping scale\rcpt{P15_the_signature_collapses_at_the_adjudicated_ratio_and_the_endpoint_sets_its_sign}. \emph{So the
hundredfold collapse is the configuration moving and not the fit improving}. \emph{So the signature
does not vanish under a refit --- it moves out of the spectrum's shape and into an inferred parameter.}
\emph{What a refit buys in shape it pays for in parameters}, and that direction is the robust part.

\emph{And how local that approximation is can be stated exactly, which settles what a joint fit would
actually meet.} The signature's own logarithmic slope is
$\dd\ln(\text{ratio})/\dd\ln\ell=-2(r^{2}-1)(\ell/\ell_{D})^{2}$ --- \emph{not a constant, but growing as
$\ell^{2}$}: across the likelihood's own $\ell=30$--$2508$ it runs from $-0.0002$ to $-1.31$, a factor of
seven thousand, which is exactly
$(\ell_{\max}/\ell_{\min})^{2}$\rcpt{P15_the_tilt_is_the_wrong_absorber}. \emph{So a single tilt is a
one-parameter fit to a function whose slope varies by that factor over the data it is scored on}, and the
displacement quoted above is the signature's true slope at $\ell=381$ and at no other multipole.
\emph{The consequence is the useful part}: a joint fit does not encounter a shifted tilt but a residual
whose \emph{shape} is a Gaussian in $\ell$, and no tilt removes a Gaussian --- so what such a fit weighs
is the shape residual, which is the quantity already bounded from above. \emph{And a displacement in
$n_s$ is not by itself a tension here}, the tilt being inherited boundary data rather than predicted: a
different value of an inherited input is a different input. \emph{What could be a tension is a constraint
on $n_s$ that does not run through the damping tail} --- the low- and mid-$\ell$ shape, the polarisation
spectra, lensing --- which is what a joint fit means and is where the question properly sits.

\emph{Two bounds travel with that and neither can be left to a reader.} \emph{The shape residual is an upper
bound}: freeing only the named degeneracy is the least absorption a fuller refit could achieve, so a wash is a
wash \emph{a fortiori}. \emph{The tilt displacement is not a bound}, because more free parameters would share
it --- the baryon density moves the diffusion length itself. \emph{And the magnitude is not robust to a factor
of three}: imposing the ratio on the measured spectrum gives the figures above, while applying it inside the
instrument's own source --- where it also reaches the Doppler term through a derivative --- gives $0.92$ in $\chi^{2}$ per
bin. \emph{The larger comes from the more faithful route, so the wash is stated at the weaker bound} and
closing that factor is named here as work not done.

\emph{The joint fit returns the residual this reading
predicts.} Fitting amplitude and tilt together against the signature-carrying spectrum, on
\texttt{plik\_lite}'s own bandpowers and covariance, the residual left after the fit is the analytic
Gaussian-minus-power-law shape to a correlation of $1.000000$ --- \emph{not merely non-zero, but the
predicted form}. \emph{The residual's form is the finding and its size is the configuration's}: at the adjudicated ratio it is under a hundredth in $\chi^{2}$ per bin, and $1.50$ per bin at a ratio of $1.0926$ on the same window.
\emph{And the displacement is not one number}: fitted over $\ell=100$--$1760$ and over
$\ell=1400$--$2508$ it differs by a factor of $6.3$ across windows of the same data, so a
displacement is the value of nothing without the window it was fitted over. \emph{And the tilt takes a fixed share of it rather than a variable one}: seven tenths at the adjudicated ratio and seven tenths at $1.0926$, so what the refit can and cannot do is a
property of the shapes and not of the size\rcpt{P15_the_joint_fit_leaves_the_gaussian_and_the_bound_was_chi2_not_sigma}.

\emph{Not} settled, and unchanged by that run: whether a displacement of that size in the inferred
tilt is a \emph{tension}. The tilt is inherited boundary data here, not predicted, so a different
value of it is a different input; what would make it a tension is a constraint on $n_s$ that does
not run through the damping tail, which this fit does not supply. The observable is $\theta_D/\theta_*=r_D/\rs$, and translating it into a spectrum requires the full acoustic transfer, which is run (\S\ref{sec:refit-bound}) and does not return high-$\ell$ peaks tracking flat $\Lambda$CDM's. A leading-order pass that simply \emph{assumes} the high-$\ell$ peaks equal $\Lambda$CDM's and applies the extra damping produces a large high-$\ell$ deficit (receipt: \texttt{full\_transfer\_verdict.py}\rcpt{P15_full_transfer_verdict})---but that assumption may not be imported for free: the same handover that fixes $r_D$ also governs the high-$\ell$ driving envelope, so CR's high-$\ell$ spectrum rests on short-wavelength collapse-phase driving rather than on the boost an expanding radiation era supplies.

\emph{And the observable ratio is read at one epoch on both lengths, which is what fixes it.} The two-arm transfer measures the raw diffusion length at the visibility peak, and the sound horizon is carried to the same place, because a ratio of integrals taken to different epochs is not a ratio of anything (\S\ref{sec:diffusion-scale}). \emph{The endpoint is therefore not a free choice}, and the difference between terminating the two lengths together and terminating them apart is larger than anything the cosmological parameters do to the signature.

\emph{That driving is computed below} (\S\ref{sec:envelope}), and the calculation removes the licence the shortcut lacked: the envelope is derived on the collapse leg rather than imported. 

Diffusion on the leaf rate therefore constrains, but does not by itself determine, the high-$\ell$ shape; the net effect is computed in \S\ref{sec:envelope-consequence}; whether it is a tension, a wash, or a distinctive but consistent feature turns on a parameter refit and on the early integrated Sachs--Wolfe term. \emph{Two confrontations are to be distinguished and both are run.} The spectrum computed on the background the distances fix is scored as it stands, one amplitude fitted so that what is compared is shape, and sits at $2.57$ times the control's distance from the data. \emph{Refitting the spectrum's own parameters to the microwave data}---the comparison the standard model is usually judged by---\emph{leaves it at $1.57$ times}: freedom closes about a third of the gap and leaves the rest (\S\ref{sec:refit-bound}). The control reproduces the sky essentially perfectly, which is what makes the comparison readable at all; and the verdict it returns is that the spectrum derived here is disfavoured against the standard model on this data. We state it as it stands. The effect the fit is run on is separately established: a real, computed, non-reabsorbable
CR-specific diffusion-scale signature, under a per cent at the adjudicated endpoint and fixed by the
handover rather than by a rate (\S\ref{sec:diffusion-scale}), its observable consequence derived rather than entangled
(\S\ref{sec:envelope-consequence}). \emph{What the fit does not reach is the join}: it weighs the
five-parameter spectrum construction, and what carries that construction back to the geometry is the transfer
work of this section, which is where the open end lies. The structural point that stands is the \emph{mechanism of phase}: where inflation freezes the phase dynamically, CR fixes it geometrically on the single null layer; how the amplitudes are driven is what the transfer of \S\ref{sec:refit-bound} measures.

\subsection{The collapse-phase driving envelope}\label{sec:envelope}
The driving named above can be carried out in closed form, and doing so removes the entanglement the previous
subsection had to leave standing. On the radiation-dominated collapse leg the potential obeys
$\Psi''+(4/\eta)\Psi'+(k^{2}/3)\Psi=0$, whose regular solution is elementary,
$\Psi=3\Psi_{i}(\sin x-x\cos x)/x^{3}$ with $x=k\eta/\sqrt3$, and which is \emph{even} in
$x$\rcpt{C1_closure_of_the_driving}---so the contracting leg carries the same closed form pointwise, a
sharper statement than the Fourier-magnitude equality of \S\ref{sec:coherence} and consistent with it.
Writing $\hat\Theta=\Theta_{0}+\Psi$ removes the source exactly, $\hat\Theta''+(k^{2}/3)\hat\Theta=0$: the
effective temperature oscillates freely, and the $k$-dependence rides entirely in the amplitude at horizon
entry and in the decay of $\Psi$.

Horizon entry is fixed by the construction rather than fitted. The comoving Hubble radius is built from the
slicing paper's own turnaround function, $(rH)^{2}=(1-f)+A/r^{2}$~\cite{JanzenSlicing}, and has a single
turning point on the whole excursion, at $r_{*}=1.53\,r_{\mathrm{seam}}$ on the corpus's inherited datum; the
\emph{seam} sits just inside that turning point, on the rising branch, so the acoustic modes are already within
the comoving horizon there---a property of the turnaround function's geometry rather than a
numerical accident of the background, and a statement about the seam that Prop.~\ref{prop:subhorizon}, being about the branch point, does not make\rcpt{C2_horizon_limits}. \emph{The locus matters and the two
available ones give opposite answers}: the branch point lies at the far end of the same rising branch, where
$2M/r\to\infty$ carries $aH$ up without bound and the comoving horizon to zero, so there \emph{every} mode is
outside it (\S\ref{sec:what-crosses}). It is the seam, not the crossing, that this construction reads---the inversion derived from the metric function rather than asserted\rcpt{P15_the_locus_is_wrong_in_six_places_and_the_lint_cannot_see_the_worst}. On the leg the radiation term dominates because it is constant
while the others vanish, so $\eta=r/\sqrt A$ to about two per cent at the entry radius of the lowest mode in
question and better the deeper the mode enters---sub-percent from $k\gtrsim30\,k_{s}$\rcpt{C3_conformal_map}, and entry then occurs at $x\to1/\sqrt3$ \emph{in the deep sub-horizon limit}---the radiation
era's own scale invariance, recovered here from the substrate geometry. \emph{The envelope is therefore flat to the accuracy of that limit}: a mode at $k\simeq10\,k_{s}$ enters within $2\%$ of $1/\sqrt3$ and one at $300\,k_{s}$ within $0.1\%$, all of them reaching the \emph{seam}---where the collapse leg ends---with the same driving amplitude $\Psi(1/\sqrt3)/2$, and the residual potential is below a percent \emph{on average} while oscillating up to some seven per cent of it where $\cos x_{\mathrm{seam}}$ is near an extremum\rcpt{C4_driving_envelope}. \emph{Both are qualifications rather than identities: the limit is a limit and the average an average.} Its turnover sits at equality, and the equality is the background's own, $\Omega_m/\Omega_r$ on the leaf rate: at the epoch and rate the baryon-acoustic data fix it falls within a couple of per cent of flat $\Lambda$CDM's, and it moves with the matter density rather than being assumed\rcpt{C7_equality_and_deficit}.

Two corrections that might have been expected do not enter. The baryon loading is proportional to the scale
factor and the driving happens where the scale factor is smallest, so $R\lesssim0.1$ where the driving happens and orders below that at entry; the odd/even asymmetry is imprinted afterwards, on the expansion side at $R\approx0.6$,
which is ordinary content physics on the observable leg. \emph{The sky's own value, located by parabolic fit on the Planck binned spectrum, is $P_1/P_2=2.2564\pm0.0772$, and flat $\Lambda$CDM's is $2.200$; \emph{what this construction returns for the same quantity is $2.264$ on the polarisation path, on the transfer of \S\ref{sec:refit-bound} with its wavenumber integral converged, since a peak height is exactly what an unconverged integral misreports}\rcpt{P15_the_height_target_was_below_the_resolution_of_its_own_statistic}\rcpt{C5b_baryon_term}. \emph{The structural point stands without it}: the loading is the same in both because it is imprinted on the expansion side, and the argument is at the level of contents rather than of the driving---where the loading ratio alone would give $3.5$.} And the free-streaming
neutrinos the cooling-leg nucleosynthesis requires do carry anisotropic stress at these temperatures---the
branch point is far below their decoupling---but their deep sub-horizon effect is a constant phase shift and a
constant suppression, and a $k$-independent correction leaves a flat envelope flat\rcpt{C6_neutrino_term}.
\emph{The transfer carries the effect, and what it carries is a composite}: removing the anisotropic
stress at both of its dynamical sites---the neutrino Euler equation and the shear term in $\Psi$---leaves
the sector a perfect fluid at the same background density, and pushes every peak on both arms to larger
multipole, the direction the free-streaming pull has, with the amplitude moving separately---its envelope
by about a quarter. \emph{But that operation removes the constant phase shift and the change in the potentials' decay
together}, so what it measures is not the deep sub-horizon constant the argument above is about, and no
value for that constant is read off
here\rcpt{P15_the_free_streaming_knob_is_common_to_both_arms_and_the_switch_sweep_finds_no_further_shadow}.
What it does not leave is the closed form for $\Psi$ itself, the equation becoming integro-differential; the
closure is a property of the skeleton, and the flatness is what survives.

\subsection{What the envelope buys, and what remains}\label{sec:envelope-consequence}
With the envelope derived rather than assumed, the diffusion signature can be stated without the entanglement.
The diffusion length reduces to a single integral over the scale factor in which every microphysical constant
sits outside, $1/k_{D}^{2}\propto\int\mathrm{d}a\,g(R)/(Hx_{e})$, so in the ratio the Thomson physics and the
ionisation history cancel identically and the whole difference is carried by $H(a)$---which is the division of
labour this section has argued for throughout. Both integrals close in elementary form, and on the inherited
datum the geometric rate gives a diffusion length $10.8\%$ longer\rcpt{C8_diffusion_length}.
\emph{That is a statement about the two rates and not about the observable}: the plasma accumulates both
lengths on the leaf's rate (\S\ref{sec:tensions}), so a difference common to them cancels from
$\theta_D/\theta_*$, and the figure is quoted here because it sizes the rate gap the division of labour
turns on rather than because it sizes the signature. \emph{The elementary route and the code-validated one
agree on it to about a point and a half}, which is the relationship an elementary derivation should bear to
a code-validated one. The sound
horizon is the same kind of integral, and it must be taken \emph{from the branch point}: there is no observable
expansion below it. Doing so returns $r_{s}=146.4$~Mpc against $145.4$ on the radiation-included rate---within $0.7\%$ of each other, the exact crossing sitting a little lower at $z\simeq6.7\times10^{3}$\rcpt{C9_sound_horizon_and_ratio}---the acoustic-scale calibration, met from the
other end and to that accuracy rather than exactly. \emph{Both are the elementary integral; carried through a full recombination history the radiation-included one comes out at $144.0$ against a Boltzmann reference's $144.4$, a per cent lower than the elementary value}\rcpt{P15_damping_ratio_clean}.  The observable, in which the common distance cancels,
is then $\theta_{D}/\theta_{*}$ moved by under a per cent, its size fixed by the handover and its sign by the common endpoint both lengths are carried to (\S\ref{sec:diffusion-scale}), and $\ell_{*}=\pi D_{M}/r_{s}=302.2$
against the measured $301.76$\rcpt{C10_highl_ratio}. 

The high-$\ell$ consequence follows with no free parameter:
$C_{\ell}^{\mathrm{CR}}/C_{\ell}^{\Lambda\mathrm{CDM}}
=\exp[-(\ell/\ell_{D})^{2}(r^{2}-1)]$ with $r=\theta_{D}/\theta_{*}$---the same ratio the preceding
paragraph derives, and nothing else---so with both lengths carried to the peak of the
visibility function, $r=0.992$, it is a rise of about two per cent at $\ell_{D}$ and four at
$1.5\,\ell_{D}$, where terminating the diffusion integral at a sharp recombination cut instead, $r=1.023$,
turns it into a fall of four and ten\rcpt{P15_the_signature_collapses_at_the_adjudicated_ratio_and_the_endpoint_sets_its_sign}, the sentence and the ratio it defines being checked against each other rather than corrected separately\rcpt{R1_the_correction_stopped_at_the_sentence_and_the_number_it_defines_was_left_behind}.
\emph{Whether that is a tension is a separate question this paper does not settle}: a
confrontation refits the cosmological parameters, which is a likelihood analysis and not a closed-form
calculation, and asserting exclusion from these numbers would repeat, at one remove, the error of holding
everything else fixed while varying one thing. What has changed is the standing of the signature. The signature is a requirement of the construction, following from the level assignment and the handover and nothing else, with its sign fixed by the endpoint the diffusion length is carried to.

\emph{The early integrated Sachs--Wolfe contribution is present here, and the step that once denied it is
worth setting out because the denial was an inference and not a measurement.} The rate at recombination is
matter-dominated to nine orders, the substrate term being utterly negligible
there\rcpt{C11_early_isw}: \emph{that is true, and it is a statement about the rate}. The geometric rate
carries no radiation term, radiation being inherited content read off the clock rather than something that
sources the rate.

\emph{But the effect is driven by the radiation \textbf{content} through the perturbation source, not by the
rate.} The content enters the source under either rate assignment, and \emph{at recombination this
cosmology's content is $21.7\%$ radiation}, $\rho_r/\rho_m=0.2765$ at $z=1088$ --- which no statement about
the rate's composition bears on. \emph{So the inference from a radiationless rate to an absent term does not
hold}, and the term is present.

\emph{Measured, as the integrated $|e^{-\tau}(\Phi'+\Psi')|$ after recombination in units of the
Sachs--Wolfe amplitude there}: $0.348$ on the leaf congruence the rate rule assigns the perturbations to, and
$0.229$ on the stacking rate. \emph{Non-zero on both.} \emph{The leaf assignment makes the term half again
larger; it does not create it.}

\emph{One scope qualification is owed here, and it narrows the claim without touching the conclusion.} The
constancy argument runs on $\Phi''+3\mathcal{H}\Phi'=0$, which is the \emph{super-horizon} equation: it drops
the $k^{2}\Phi$ term. For the acoustic modes---inside the horizon through the observable leg---that term does not vanish, and the potential does decay on the observable leg, by a factor of order two across the first few
peaks\rcpt{C11TEST_radiation_zeroed}. The decay is not a radiation effect: zeroing the radiation fractions in
the constraint makes it \emph{larger}, and the rate responsible is $k^{2}/(3\mathcal{H})$, which grows with $k$
and refers to no content at all. So the statement that survives is that \emph{the substrate rate supplies no
early integrated Sachs--Wolfe term}---which is what the leftward reading delivers and what the comparison with
flat $\Lambda$CDM turns on---while the sub-horizon potential decay is an ordinary consequence of the
perturbation's own scale, present in any cosmology.

Whatever difference in that term remains between the arms cannot offset the diffusion signature, and the reason is structural rather than
numerical. It sits near the first peaks and dies away well before the damping tail begins, so the two live at
opposite ends of the spectrum; and the first peak is where the amplitude is anchored, by an $A_s$ this
construction inherits rather than predicts. A few per cent there is absorbed by that inherited normalisation,
exactly as it would be in a framework carrying $\eta$ from outside. The diffusion signature is not so absorbed:
it is a change of \emph{shape} at high multipole, and rescaling the amplitude to restore the first peak would
worsen the tail rather than repair it. \emph{What stands between the derivation and a verdict is therefore the
likelihood confrontation}---a parameter refit, not a further calculation of this kind, and it is run: the
joint fit leaves the Gaussian residual (\S\ref{sec:coherence}) and the full-spectrum likelihood rejects this
arm's spectrum on $TT$ shape (\S\ref{sec:refit-bound}).

\subsection{The diffusion scale, and the error budget}\label{sec:diffusion-scale}

The acoustic scale is computed from the rate, and the diffusion scale is computed from the same one. Both
lengths are accumulated by the plasma on the leaf rate, so the projection cancels from their ratio and what is
left depends on two things only: where the plasma is handed over, and how far the diffusion integral is
carried. \emph{Neither is a free parameter, and the first is the larger.} With the handover at the branch
point the ratio sits within a couple of per cent of flat $\Lambda$CDM's on either endpoint convention; a start placed later on the expanding leg
raises it steeply, reaching some thirty-five per cent by the redshift at which the angle would be met.

\emph{The remaining choice is the epoch both integrals terminate at, and it is settled by the same
consistency the rate assignment is.} $\theta_D/\theta_*$ is a ratio of two lengths the plasma accumulates,
and \emph{a ratio of integrals taken to different epochs is not a ratio of anything}: the observed angle is
read at last scattering, which is the peak of the visibility function, so that is where both the sound
horizon and the diffusion length are carried to. \emph{Terminating them differently is what produces the
spread}: with the sound horizon anchored at the observed angle and the diffusion length taken instead to a
sharp recombination cut, the signature reads $+2.2\%$ where a common endpoint at the visibility peak gives
$-0.9\%$ --- a swing of three points where the cosmological parameters together move it by two
hundredths\rcpt{P15_the_damping_signature_error_budget_and_the_convention_dominates_it}. \emph{So the size is the handover's and the epoch is not a free
choice}, and the ordering of the four handover-and-rate combinations is unchanged on either reading. \emph{What the signature is not} is
reabsorbable: the baryon density has about one per cent of freedom against it, and the matter density could
only absorb it by moving some thirty-seven per cent, which is a different cosmology rather than a refit.

Two contributions that
might have been expected do not enter: the last-scattering surface's thickness scales with the rate in both
its width and its conversion to comoving distance, so it cancels from the ratio; and the recombination
redshift, integrated on this rate through the Peebles equation~\cite{Peebles1968} rather than inherited, moves by only
$-0.11\%$\rcpt{PEEB_peebles_on_the_set_rate}. The same integration returns $z_{*}=1091$ on the
radiation-included rate, against the $1090$ of the standard treatment, which is the check that the ionisation
history is being handled correctly.

\subsection{The integrated Sachs--Wolfe contribution across the first-peak region}\label{sec:isw}

The first acoustic peak is a separate matter and it does not move. Its offset from $\ell_{*}$---$220$ against
$301.7$---is largely the phase shift the potential's decay imparts while the modes are driven, and that decay
has the same closed form on the collapse leg as on an expanding radiation era (\S\ref{sec:envelope}), so it
transfers. What does not transfer is the early integrated Sachs--Wolfe contribution, and that turns out to be
a nearly \emph{flat} multiplicative factor across the first-peak region---some ten per cent in amplitude,
varying by two parts in a hundred between $\ell=150$ and $\ell=300$---so it displaces the maximum by one or
two multipoles rather than by tens\rcpt{ISW_line_of_sight}. \emph{So the early integrated Sachs--Wolfe contribution does not move the first peak}; what sets this
arm's first peak, and how its phase stands against the sky's, is taken up at
\S\ref{sec:refit-bound}. \emph{The reabsorption of the damping signature, by contrast, can be bounded here}, and bounding it is
worth doing because the reabsorption question is the whole of what a likelihood would decide about this
signature. Pinning $\ell_{*}$ to the measured $301$ and minimising the residual in $\theta_{D}/\theta_{*}$ over
the baryon density and the matter density---the former held to the one per cent primordial deuterium allows,
the latter to the five per cent the distance and growth data allow---leaves a residual of several per cent
\rcpt{M2_reabsorption_minimization}. The baryon density has about one per cent of freedom
against it (\S\ref{sec:diffusion-scale}); the matter density could absorb it only by moving some thirty-seven per cent, which is
not a refit but a different cosmology. \emph{So the shift has a floor, and the floor is the prediction.} We
state this as a bound and not as a likelihood: a full comparison fits the whole spectrum with every parameter
and its covariances, where this fixes one observable and minimises one derived ratio. It settles that the
signature does not dissolve under reabsorption; it does not settle the verdict.

\subsection{What the instrument's residual is made of}\label{sec:residual-decomposition}

The two-arm comparison's floor is set by components of the instrument rather than by either
cosmology. Two candidates for the accuracy it lacks are measured here.

\emph{Of the two, one cannot pay at all}\rcpt{P15_what_is_left_is_lensing_and_not_reionisation}\ldg{optics_lensing}. Above $\ell\simeq40$ the
whole effect of reionisation on the temperature spectrum is the constant screening $e^{-2\tau}$---the
second visibility bump contributes only below $\ell\simeq30$, outside this comparison's range---and
the comparison fits one amplitude in closed form. \emph{An amplitude fitted as
$A=(m^{\mathsf T}Fd)/(m^{\mathsf T}Fm)$ is homogeneous of degree $-1$ in the model, so rescaling the
model returns $A/s$ and leaves the residual unchanged}: the degeneracy is exact rather than
approximate, and it is a property of the statistic rather than of the size of $\tau$. Checked at
$\tau=0.054$, $0.10$ and $0.30$, the change in $\chi^{2}$ is zero to machine precision at all three.
\emph{Lensing is a different matter.} A smoothing in multipole, scanned in width, takes the control
from $1320$ to $921$; and a width that \emph{grows} with $\ell$ beats a constant one by a factor of
$1.8$ in the improvement, each compared at its own optimum, so the preference is for a shape and not
merely for a size---\emph{which is lensing's own signature, since a deflection of fixed angle
subtends a larger fraction of an acoustic wavelength at high $\ell$}. \emph{We state what that is and
is not.} The width is fitted, so the improvement bounds what a derived calculation has to play for
rather than measuring a lensing amplitude; and even at its own optimum some $70\%$ of the residual
survives it. \emph{The lensed spectrum is therefore what the instrument's remaining floor is made of, and it is
computed rather than fitted.} Applying
$\Lambda$CDM's own lensed-to-unlensed ratio to the arm---a derived operation with no free width---takes
the control from $1320$ to $989$, an improvement of $331$, concentrated in the damping tail where a
fixed-angle deflection fills the troughs. \emph{That is below the $400$ the fitted width bounded, as a
zero-parameter derivation must be}, and the acoustic peaks do not move under it. The corpus's own
first-order kernel overshoots the bound to $508$ by breaking down in that same tail---returning a
spurious $+13\%$ at $\ell=1900$ where the full operator gives $+6.5\%$---which is why the derived
number is the full operator's. \emph{Most of the fitted headroom is recovered by a genuine lensing,
and about $989$ of the $1320$ survives it}: the verdict of the last paragraph stands from the derived
side, lensing real and not the last build\rcpt{P15_derived_lensing_on_the_lcdm_arm}.

\emph{And what the residual is MADE of was decomposed alongside it, which is what let the size of that
gain be anticipated rather than
discovered}\rcpt{P15_the_residual_is_contrast_and_the_lensing_potential_is_derived}\rcpt{P15_the_lensing_potential_re_derives_bit_identically_and_the_three_keys_that_do_not_are_a_cross_check_the_producer_never_computed}. Projecting named
templates through the same inverse covariance the comparison uses, the peak \emph{positions} account for
a tenth of a per cent of the residual---\emph{which is why the acoustic peaks do not move under the
lensing above, and the two findings corroborate each other}---while the peak-trough \emph{contrast} at
fixed position accounts for two-fifths of it, this transfer's contrast running some thirteen per cent
too high. \emph{Reducing contrast without moving peaks is what lensing does.} A smooth multiplicative
envelope accounts for a further sixth, and \emph{about half the residual is in neither}---so a complete
lensing calculation was expected to leave a comparable amount behind, and it did.

\subsection{The shear coefficient, derived}\label{sec:shear-coefficient}

\emph{The coefficient is derived rather than fitted, and the derivation settles it against the
fit.} The coefficient is measured by running the same
oscillator twice on the same background: once as the perfect tight-coupled fluid, which is undamped by
construction, and once as a photon hierarchy carried to $\ell=24$ with Thomson scattering, the polarisation
multipoles evolved alongside and the baryons given their own velocity. \emph{Their ratio is the damping and
nothing else}---the slowly varying prefactor, the drift of the sound speed and the baryon-loading dependence
of the oscillation are common to the two runs and divide out identically---and reading it locally, between
consecutive extrema, lets the residual be extrapolated in $(k/\tau')^{2}$ rather than smeared over an
interval. With the polarisation multipoles suppressed the quadrupole's scattering term reduces to
$-\tfrac{9}{10}\tau'F_{2}$, which is the unpolarised closure, and the coefficient comes back as $0.8852$
against $8/9$; with them carried it is $1.0618$ against $16/15$. \emph{The polarisation correction itself,
which is the quantity in dispute, is a factor $1.1996$ against $6/5$: three parts in ten thousand.} Both
absolute values sit about half a per cent low by an amount proportional to the coefficient, so the residual is
a common normalisation and cancels in the
ratio\rcpt{P15_the_shear_coefficient_derived_not_remembered}.

\subsection{The acoustic comparison}\label{sec:refit-bound}
The angular scale $\ell_{*}=\pi D_{M}/\rs$ is built from two lengths that do not ride the same rate here: the
distance carries the stacking rate's $1/H_{0}$ and the sound horizon is accumulated by the plasma on the leaf
rate, which carries the radiation the other does not. So the measured angle constrains $H_{0}$, as it does in
flat $\Lambda$CDM where the sound horizon is fixed instead by the matter and baryon
densities\rcpt{H0_acoustic_angle_and_seam}. \emph{So the acoustic scale is computed rather than accommodated}, and it is the comb of the computed
spectrum that carries it: fitted to those peaks by the procedure this paper runs on the sky, it is $298.0$
against the sky's $298.4$, with the background fixed by the distance data
alone\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}. \emph{A start placed by hand and fitted to the
angle would report a different quantity}: what such a fit sets is the scale reported, not the spacing the
spectrum carries, the two differing by four per cent.

\emph{The datum \S\ref{sec:coherence} states leaves two things to be fixed, and the handover fixes both.} That
datum is one amplitude per mode and a \emph{common} phase; it says that the phase is common without saying
which phase it is, and it does not say what the amplitude is flat in wavenumber \emph{at}. At the branch point
both follow: every mode is outside the horizon there, so the phase is an extremum with no velocity, and the
amplitude is the frozen value the closed-form leg carries, $0.4835\,\Psi_i$.

\emph{How much the comb depends on that choice is measured rather than assumed.} Across the seventeen
readings admitted by a four-peak criterion fixed before the numbers, the first peak spans $148$ to $228$---a
factor of $1.541$, and across the fifteen numeric-phase readings alone $1.118$---with the spacing spanning
$276.0$ to $330.4$, the phase intercept $-0.4044$ to $-0.2161$ and the alternation ratio $0.475$ to $1.088$.
\emph{Within that span position and alternation move against each other}, Spearman $\rho=+0.782$ at
$p=2.1\times10^{-4}$ between the first peak and the gap ratio, with every contracting reading sitting at
$\ell_1\le212$. \emph{The single-locus reading is not inside that trade}: it returns the first peak at $222$
and a contracting second gap together, which is what fixing the state at one locus rather than choosing a
reading of it buys.

\emph{The instrument that would measure the rest is built, and its guard and its control are what make it an
arbiter.} Both arms run on one set of equations, so no comparison between them is a difference of
machinery\rcpt{P15_two_arm_control_and_guard}. \emph{The guard first, because it is what licenses the rest.}
With every coupling to the potential removed at every site---the $4\Phi'$ in the photon, neutrino and
cold-matter continuity equations and the $k^{2}\Psi$ in the photon--baryon and neutrino Euler equations---the
source comb lands on the integers on \emph{both} arms. \emph{So the sound horizon, the measure, the
wavenumber grid and the phase extraction are validated as an ensemble, and every departure from $n\pi/\rs$ in
a driven run is the driving and nothing else.}

\emph{The control is the arm whose answer is known independently, and it reproduces
it}\rcpt{P15_the_photon_hierarchy_in_the_transfer}\rcpt{C59_the_control_reproduces_camb_and_the_height_defect_was_k_truncation}.
The photons are carried as a Boltzmann hierarchy with the polarisation multipoles alongside, the baryons
given their own velocity and density contrast, and the source carrying $g\Pi/4$ and
$(3/4k^{2})\,\dd^{2}(g\Pi)/\dd\eta^{2}$. Run on the flat-$\Lambda$CDM arm it returns peaks at $220$, $540$ and
$812$ against the sky's $220.6$, $538.1$ and $809.8$, and a first-to-second height ratio of $2.1969$ against a
standard code's $2.200$---agreement to $0.14\%$, and within the sky's own uncertainty.

\emph{Two conditions on how such a transfer is run are what that agreement rests on, and the tighter of
them is numerical.} The projection must carry the polarisation source, which a damping envelope cannot
supply at any coefficient; and the wavenumber integral must be carried past the highest multipole
\emph{reported}, since $\int P(k)\,\Delta_{\ell}(k)^{2}\,\dd k$ is not converged there. \emph{Each is worth a
factor on its own and neither alone reaches the answer}: the height ratio runs from $2.721$ at an
$\ell$-equivalent cutoff of $900$ to $2.393$ at $2400$, so a cutoff set at the reported multipole is a
systematic and not a
rounding\rcpt{C59_the_control_reproduces_camb_and_the_height_defect_was_k_truncation}.

\emph{The perturbation sector runs on the leaf congruence, which is what the framework assigns it.} A process
running \emph{in} the content---the sound horizon, the diffusion length, recombination, the
perturbations---takes the leaf's rate (\S\ref{sec:tensions}), while the comoving separations and the
projection keep the stacking rate; the change is an exact chain rule between two monotone conformal times, so
every spline and grid is untouched, and on the flat-$\Lambda$CDM arm the two rates are identical and the
assignment is provably a no-op there---which is its own self-check.

\emph{The comparison is made on the polarisation path, in the configuration the control is calibrated in.}
There the control returns a first-to-second height ratio of $2.195$ at peaks $220$, $536$ and $814$, against
a standard code's $2.200$---$0.23\%$: \emph{so the instrument is calibrated where this cosmology's number is
taken, and that is the figure quoted for it, the closer $0.14\%$ belonging to a different run and not to this
one}\rcpt{P15_the_third_peak_deficit_is_radiation_driving_and_both_routes_to_the_equality_agree}\rcpt{P15_two_arm_control_and_guard}\rcpt{P15_the_line_of_sight_transfer}. \emph{Both arms are converged in the wavenumber integral.} The
control's own movement between cutoffs is $0.000\%$ and $0.046\%$ on the two height ratios, and this
cosmology's arm does not move at all---$0.000\%$ and $0.000\%$---so the arm is converged at
the lower cutoff and the figures below are not a truncation artefact. \emph{The same comparison on the
fluid path reads $0.042\%$ and $0.036\%$ against $0.000\%$ and $0.045\%$}: both paths converge, and the
two sets of figures are not interchangeable. \emph{A rung below that was refused
outright by the instrument's own truncation guard, on both arms, which is data and not an error.}

\emph{On that path this cosmology's arm returns the following, with the plasma handed over at the branch
point and the background fixed by the distance data alone.}
The peaks fall at $222$, $538$, $818$ and $1134$ against the sky's $220.4$, $537.7$, $817.3$ and $1123.9$;
the comb fitted to them by the procedure this paper runs on the sky is $298.0$ against the sky's $298.4$, and
the phase intercept $\varphi/\pi=-0.2349$ against $-0.2405$. The height ratios are $P_{1}/P_{2}=2.264$ and
$P_{1}/P_{3}=2.298$ against the sky's $2.217$ and $2.277$. \emph{The first three peaks agree with the sky inside the
spread the locating itself carries, and the fourth is not resolved against it}: located through one procedure
with the likelihood's own covariance propagated, this arm sits $0.9$ to $1.3$ standard deviations from the
measured fourth peak across the admissible window, so \emph{the data cannot tell the two models apart
there}\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}\rcpt{P15_the_skys_own_fourth_peak_cannot_tell_the_arms_apart_and_the_displacement_is_shared_with_the_control}.
\emph{That one procedure is a requirement and not a convenience}, because the fitting window is a source of
bias rather than of scatter: across the admissible windows the located peak moves by a fraction of a multipole
at the first peak and by some six at the fourth, a wider fit on an increasingly asymmetric and
damping-suppressed hump pulling its apex down the envelope's slope---\emph{and it moves the sky and both
models the same way}, so it cancels from a difference taken through one procedure and does not cancel from
anything else.
\emph{And what offset there is, is mostly not this construction's}: both models sit high of the located sky,
the control by $1.2$ standard deviations and this arm by $2.1$, with well over half of this arm's offset being
the control's own. \emph{Two wavenumber cutoffs agree to every digit, and the positions are
converged in the reported multipole grid}: read on a grid of step $2$ and refined to step $1$, they move by a
single multipole at the first peak and not at all above it.

\emph{And the residual is neither of the two instrument faults a reader would reasonably suspect}, because
both are removed in this configuration: the wavenumber integral is carried past the reported multipole, and
the projection carries the polarisation source rather than a damping
envelope\rcpt{P15_the_height_target_was_below_the_resolution_of_its_own_statistic}. \emph{And the truncation's
removal is measured on the arm whose answer is known}: it was $78\%$ of the control's remaining $\chi^{2}$,
and taking it out brings the control to $1.18$ per degree of freedom over the full range against a reference
$1.01$\rcpt{P15_the_control_entered_the_regime_and_the_arm_did_not_move}. \emph{The diffusion damping is
derived on each arm's own opacity and rate rather than imported or fitted}, and putting it back moves the
height ratios by tens of per cent and drops the likelihood's floor by a factor of five without the verdict
turning over\rcpt{P15_the_derived_diffusion_damping}. \emph{The polarisation source pulls both arms, and
\emph{nothing about either operation differs between the arms}}, which is what makes what
survives them attributable to the source rather than to the machinery. \emph{The size of that pull is
not quoted here}: the operation is arm-independent by construction, which is the load-bearing point,
and the pull's magnitude on the configuration this section reports has not been measured.

\emph{Two likelihood configurations are quoted in this section and they are not interchangeable, so which is which is stated rather than left to be inferred.} The full-range lensed one, on $185$ bins, is the better-converged: it is where the control reaches $1.18$ per degree of freedom against a reference $1.01$, and it is the configuration in which an instrument fault can be told from a physical one. The $133$-bin unlensed one is where the shape rejection is quoted, and its control sits at $2.10$ per bin because the ceiling cuts the damping tail\rcpt{P15_the_133_bin_pair_is_computed_and_asserted_and_the_hardcoded_row_carried_the_other_arm}. \emph{The second is not a worse measurement of the first's quantity}: it is a different configuration, chosen for the comparison it makes, and the two sit beside each other rather than one superseding the other.

\emph{The driving's size on the observable leg is measured rather than inferred, and its wavenumber
dependence is known in closed form}---the $k$-dependence living in the potential's gradient rather than in its
decay, with the turnover at a fixed multiple of the horizon
scale\rcpt{P15_which_coupling_carries_the_k_dependence}\rcpt{P15_the_gradient_coupling_in_closed_form}.
\emph{Subtracting the driving is what measures it}: removing every coupling to the potential moves the
control's first peak from $220$ to $274$ and $\ell_{1}/\ell_{A}$ by $0.1792$, so the modes carrying the first
peak are driven and the shift is the driving's
size\rcpt{P15_the_anomalous_driving_belonged_to_the_pin_and_not_to_the_construction}\rcpt{P15_the_phase_is_the_driving_and_the_undriven_arms_agree}. \emph{Run on this arm the same
subtraction gives $0.1717$}---\emph{the potential's grip within four per cent of the control's, and slightly
weaker rather than stronger}\rcpt{P15_the_anomalous_driving_belonged_to_the_pin_and_not_to_the_construction}.
\emph{And this one is not path-specific}: the two instrument paths return the same shift to four decimal
places, where the heights do not.

\emph{One of the two position statistics is not a projection quantity, and saying which keeps them apart.}
The ratio of the first peak to the acoustic scale is $\ell_{1}/\ell_{A}=k_{1}\rs/\pi$---\emph{the distance
cancels identically}---which is why removing each source term, the damping envelope and the visibility
function in turn leaves it untouched. What it compares is the sound horizon each arm \emph{reports} against
the phase its modes actually accumulate. \emph{Both arms integrate that horizon from $a\to0$}, the plasma
being handed over at the branch point on this one and the control having no other start, so the comparison
does not confound the rate with the
start\rcpt{P15_the_symmetric_comparison_was_never_runnable_and_the_quarter_was_two_fifths}; and here the two
agree, the comb at $298.0$ against a reported scale of $302.9$. \emph{The two sides of that agreement are
computed independently}: the reported scale reaches the transfer nowhere---no source term, no projection
and no solver reads it---so the comb it is compared against is not a quantity it was used to
set\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}\rcpt{P15_the_free_streaming_knob_is_common_to_both_arms_and_the_switch_sweep_finds_no_further_shadow}.

\emph{And the likelihood is run, both arms scored on the same bins by the same code, with the amplitude
fitted so that what is compared is shape.} \emph{The result is still a rejection of this arm's spectrum and is
stated as one.} On the full-range lensed configuration---$\ell=100$--$1996$, $185$ bins, the lensing carried
by the same derived operator on both arms---the control returns $\chi^{2}=214.1$, or $1.16$ per bin, and this
arm $550.5$, or $2.98$ per bin: \emph{the arm sits at $2.57$ times the control's distance from the
data}\rcpt{P15_the_full_range_lensed_comparison_is_the_unfavourable_one_and_the_control_is_nearly_camb}\rcpt{P15_the_133_bin_pair_is_computed_and_asserted_and_the_hardcoded_row_carried_the_other_arm}\rcpt{P15_where_the_likelihood_sits}. \emph{That is the unfavourable of the two configurations and it is
the one quoted}, the same comparison unlensed over $133$ bins giving $279.4$ against $556.7$, a factor $1.98$;
lensing is worth proportionally more to a spectrum whose peaks are already in place\rcpt{P15_the_133_bin_pair_is_computed_and_asserted_and_the_hardcoded_row_carried_the_other_arm}. \emph{The band-by-band comparison is even across the range} --- $1.70$, $2.22$, $4.23$ and $9.29$ per bin against the control's $0.77$, $1.44$, $2.62$ and $3.73$ over the four quarters from $\ell=100$ to $1300$, read on the unlensed configuration --- so the disagreement is not one feature carrying the whole of it\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}. \emph{And the spectrum's own parameters are refitted, which is the comparison the standard model is usually
judged by.} With the expansion rate, the matter and baryon densities and the tilt free in each arm and the
amplitude in closed form, scored on the full range and verified by running the spectra at the minima, the
control settles at $1.01$ in $\chi^{2}$ per bin and this arm at $1.58$: \emph{the arm at $1.57$ times the
control's distance, where the computed spectrum sits at $2.56$ times it on the same bins}---so \emph{freedom closes
about a third of the gap and leaves the rest}\rcpt{P15_the_133_bin_pair_is_computed_and_asserted_and_the_hardcoded_row_carried_the_other_arm}\rcpt{P15_the_no_fit_confrontation_is_three_per_bin_and_its_residual_carries_a_nine_sigma_modulation_at_the_combs_own_period_which_binning_at_that_period_cancels}\rcpt{P15_the_full_range_refit_holds_the_background_and_the_phase_residual_was_quantised}\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}\rcpt{P15_the_refit_leaves_the_background_where_the_distances_put_it_and_does_not_close_the_phase}\rcpt{P15_the_1p57_has_no_parameter_address_because_the_arms_tt_minimum_sits_on_its_own_bao_minimum_while_the_controls_pays_fifteen_in_chi2_to_leave_planck}.

\emph{What the refit does not move is the background.} Given four free parameters this arm's expansion rate
moves by $0.028\%$ and its matter density by $0.031\%$ from the values the baryon-acoustic distances and the
acoustic angle fix with no spectrum involved: \emph{the background the distances fix is the background the
spectrum wants}. \emph{And the size of that agreement is read against the distance data's own width
rather than left as a percentage}: the refit's matter density sits $0.046$ of a standard deviation from
the distance fit's, at a cost of $0.010$ in $\chi^{2}$ to the distance data---one part in a
hundred---where the same fit run on the distances alone, on this construction's own ruler with the
distances on its radiation-free rate and the sound horizon on the leaf clock, returns the pair at
$0.958$ per degree of freedom. \emph{So the spectrum is not pulling the background anywhere}, and what
remains is left after the parameters have been given away rather than being a parameter still to be
set\rcpt{P15_the_1p57_has_no_parameter_address_because_the_arms_tt_minimum_sits_on_its_own_bao_minimum_while_the_controls_pays_fifteen_in_chi2_to_leave_planck}. \emph{The control's own displacement is
larger and is not a comparison of the same kind}: its reference matter density is a microwave fit
rather than a distance fit, so what its refit moves is the standing disagreement between those two
datasets and not anything this comparison introduces. \emph{And they are at a sharp minimum rather than unconstrained}---one-step excursions cost
$2527$, $740$, $141$ and $31$ in $\chi^{2}$ on the four parameters, which is the distinction between a
parameter that does not move and one that cannot. \emph{The one parameter that does move is the tilt}: this
arm prefers $0.998$ against the control's $0.954$, bluer by $0.044$, with the damping tail's leverage landing
there and on the baryon density as the shorter range predicted it would.

\emph{And what the refit leaves is measured rather than quoted as a ratio, because a residual's size says
nothing about whether the model could have removed it.} Taking the whitened response of the fit's own
directions on each arm---the amplitude and the four parameters at the step sizes the grid was built on---the
five are four stiff directions and one soft one \emph{on both arms, with the same degeneracy}: the expansion
rate and the matter density respond at $\lvert\cos\rvert=0.99$ on each, which is the familiar geometric
degeneracy and not something this background introduces. \emph{So this arm is not short of freedom; it has the
control's, direction for direction.} What separates them is the \emph{reach}. At the exact least-squares
minimum of the model linearised about its base---where the residual is orthogonal to all five directions by
construction, so nothing reachable is left in it---the control leaves $186.0$ in $\chi^2$ against $n-5=180$,
within a third of a standard deviation of the noise expectation, \emph{and this arm leaves $278.8$, at
$5.2$}: about a hundred in $\chi^2$ lies outside every direction the model has. \emph{And the direction it
lies along is nameable.} Decomposing each parameter's response on an amplitude, an acoustic \emph{shift} and
an acoustic \emph{contrast} template of the base spectrum, all four parameters move the shift strongly and the
contrast barely---the $4\times2$ matrix of their (shift, contrast) responses having a singular-value ratio of
$0.040$ on the control and $0.036$ here, so \emph{the contrast is nearly unmoved by any declared parameter on
either background}. Freeing one uniform contrast template as a sixth direction then removes $56.4$ of this
arm's residual, a fifth of it, against $0.8$ on the control, at a fitted coefficient of
$-0.0636\pm0.0085$ against the control's $-0.0075\pm0.0083$: \emph{the data ask this arm's acoustic contrast
to be $6.4$ per cent lower at better than seven standard deviations, and ask the control for nothing}. An
$\ell$-modulated contrast buys $4.5$ and a modulated shift $0.4$, so what is wanted is a uniform level and not
a drift. \emph{And the leftover is not noise}: in $\ell$ order this arm's residual changes sign $54$ times
against $88\pm10$ for correlated draws of the likelihood's own covariance with the same five directions
projected out, while the control's $87$ sits inside one standard deviation of
it\rcpt{P15_the_fit_has_the_controls_freedom_and_the_contrast_the_data_ask_for_lies_outside_every_direction_it_has}.
\emph{Two things follow, and the second is the one that matters.} The $6.4$ per cent is the size of this
sector's standing arm-to-control contrast excess, measured independently and reported above, so the quantity
the fit cannot supply and the quantity the projection manufactures are one quantity. And \emph{no
re-parametrisation reaches it}: a residual lying outside the span of every declared direction is closed by
changing what \emph{sets} the contrast, which is the assignment of the scales and the distances to their rates
(\S\ref{sec:scope}), and not by freeing a parameter.

\emph{One thing the ratio does not carry, and it is stated here rather than folded in.} The two arms fit the
same four parameters, so no information criterion separates them---penalties of about two per fitted parameter
against a difference of some hundred in $\chi^{2}$---and the asymmetry between the frameworks is not in what was
fitted but in what had to be fixed before there was a model to fit. \emph{The standard model reaches flat
$\Lambda$CDM by setting a curvature parameter to zero on inflation's authority, and uses the same added epoch
to supply the coherent phases}; here the flat slices, the expansion law and the coherence are consequences of
the construction (\S\ref{sec:flatlcdm}, \S\ref{sec:coherence}) and there is no curvature parameter to free.
\emph{And it runs the other way too}: both frameworks inherit the composition, the amplitude and the tilt, and
neither derives the spectrum it is scored on. \emph{Two axes, and no single number crosses
them}~\cite{JanzenSynthesis}.

\emph{And the phase intercept is not a residual at all, once the sky's own is located by the same
procedure.} Running one locator over the binned likelihood and both arms alike, the sky's intercept is
$-0.2378\pm0.0099$ on the first three peaks and $-0.2448\pm0.0074$ on all four; this arm's is $-0.2278$ and
$-0.2445$. \emph{So the arm sits inside the locator's own window-to-window spread on the one fit and on the sky's value in the other},
and the sign of the offset flips between them --- \emph{the shape of a difference carried by a single peak
rather than a phase
error}\rcpt{P15_there_is_no_phase_residual_and_the_fourth_peak_is_not_the_damping_envelope}. \emph{What
dissolves it is the sky's own locating spread and not a correction to the arm}: the binned likelihood cannot
pin that intercept tighter than a hundredth across the windows the locator admits, and the offset is inside
that range. \emph{The spread quoted is the locator's movement across those windows on one realisation and not a
noise level}, the covariance-propagating reading of the same quantity having been left unopened.

\emph{What remains is about one multipole at each peak, and it is not the damping envelope.} Imposing the
control's envelope on this arm moves the fourth peak by a seventh of a multipole against an offset of two,
and the inverse operation returns the same displacement with the opposite sign, so the test is sound and the
answer negative. \emph{And read as the difference between the two arms, located sub-bin by the same procedure on each, the
offsets are one near-constant multipole at all four peaks}: $+1.61$, $-0.19$, $+1.07$ and $+1.31$. \emph{That
is the shape of a small common phase and not of a ruler or a driving error}---a constant displacement fits it
half again better than a constant fractional one, which would have to grow from half a multipole at the first
peak to two and a half at the fourth, and a driving error would grow faster still. \emph{What each arm shares
with the other is larger than what separates them}: both sit low of the located sky at the second and third
peaks and high at the fourth, and at the fourth the control carries four fifths of the
offset\rcpt{P15_the_locator_is_good_to_three_hundredths_and_the_fourth_peaks_residual_is_the_controls_too}.
\emph{At the spread the sky's own locating carries, one multipole is not distinguishable from none}, so this
is a shape the comparison exhibits rather than a disagreement it records.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_acoustic_two_arm.pdf}
\caption{Both frameworks refit on the same bins with the same freedom, against the measured temperature
spectrum. \emph{Above}, the acoustic range in $D_\ell$, each arm at its verified refit minimum with the
amplitude taken on the full bandpower covariance; \emph{below}, the same on a logarithmic multipole axis from
$\ell=2$, so the discrete low-multipole floor and its minimum at $\ell=4$ sit in one frame with the peaks ---
that panel's floor being a reconstruction against the control's own low-multipole spectrum, since the sweep
carries it as a ratio. \emph{Whitened residuals beneath each.} The peak positions and the spacing are the
result to read in the upper panels; \emph{the residuals are where the rejection lives}, and the control's own
are drawn beside this arm's so the size of the difference is visible.}\label{fig:acoustic}
\end{figure}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_acoustic_nofit.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{The other confrontation: the comb computed on the background the distance data fix, with
nothing fitted to the microwave data.} \emph{Above}, both arms in $D_\ell$ against the Planck temperature
bandpowers, on a background recovered from DESI DR2 baryon-acoustic data alone---$(68.62,0.2974)$ at $0.958$
per degree of freedom\rcpt{P15_at_its_own_preferred_H0_the_crossing_arm_predicts_the_acoustic_comb_with_no_fitted_number}\rcpt{P15_the_133_bin_pair_is_computed_and_asserted_and_the_hardcoded_row_carried_the_other_arm}---with the overall amplitude in closed form and no other number taken from the spectrum;
the dashed curves are the refit minima of Fig.~\ref{fig:acoustic} for comparison on the same bins. Over $179$
bins on $100\le\ell\le1900$ the control stands at $1.13$ per bin and this arm at $3.00$, a factor $2.66$,
against $0.99$ and $1.58$ when four parameters are freed in each arm. \emph{The peaks are the thing to look
at}: positions and spacing agree while the extrema are overshot, which is the rejection in the form a reader
can see. \emph{Below left}, the whitened residual averaged in bins one comb period wide. It shows a slow swing
changing sign twice across the range and no alternation---and it cannot show one, because averaging over a
full cycle of a modulation at that period returns that modulation's own mean. The panel is drawn because the
operation is the one a reader reaches for, and what it does show is a true and separate statement about the
residual's long-wavelength shape. \emph{Below right}, the measurement: the same residual folded by phase
within the comb period, anchored on this arm's own first peak. One harmonic at $\ell_{A}$ has amplitude
$1.37\pm0.15$ for the unfitted arm---$9.1\sigma$---against the control's $0.20\pm0.11$, consistent with none;
freeing the four parameters halves it to $0.81\pm0.12$ and leaves $6.9\sigma$. \emph{So the rejection is
shaped at the acoustic period, the shape survives the refit, and the free parameters absorb part of it
without reaching its
cause.}\rcpt{P15_the_no_fit_confrontation_is_three_per_bin_and_its_residual_carries_a_nine_sigma_modulation_at_the_combs_own_period_which_binning_at_that_period_cancels}
\emph{The fold is taken at $\ell_{A}$ by construction; where the residual's period actually sits is a
separate question and is treated in \S\ref{sec:refit-bound}.}}
\label{fig:acoustic-nofit}
\end{figure}

\emph{And what the rejection is made of is measured rather than described.} Whitened against the full
covariance, the two arms' residuals sit at a cosine of $0.855$, and projecting the control's direction out of
this arm's leaves $73\%$ of it shared --- stable across three bin cuts. \emph{That fraction is not an
attribution, and reading it as one would be an error}: both arms are fitted to the same bandpowers, so their
residuals differ by the model difference alone and the fraction measures only how alike the two models are ---
a control deliberately tilted away from its own best fit, which nobody would defend, scores higher still.
\emph{What the decomposition does say runs the other way.} The control already fits these bins at $0.99$ per
bin, so there is no shared defect for this arm's misfit to be an amplification of; this arm exceeds it by
$105$ in $\chi^{2}$ and the model difference alone carries $77$ of that, so \emph{the excess is this
construction's}, and the factor of $1.3$ between the two residuals' sizes is that difference's own
size\rcpt{P15_the_shared_fraction_does_not_mean_what_i_said_and_the_peak_trough_pattern_is_the_arms_alone}.
\emph{Within it, position rather than
amplitude, tilt or damping}: admitting one further parameter at a time, a peak-position rescale buys $4.7$ in
$\chi^{2}$, the damping $1.4$ and the tilt $0.1$ --- and on the control the position direction buys nothing at
all, so \emph{the position part is this construction's}. It is small: the rescale is under a multipole at the
fourth peak, and $4.7$ of $283$ is under two per
cent\rcpt{P15_three_quarters_of_the_arms_residual_is_the_controls_and_what_is_left_is_position_not_amplitude}.
\emph{Amplitude, tilt, damping and position together do not account for the shape rejection}: they carry
$5.7\%$ of the model difference between them. \emph{What does is a contrast difference}, and the sky's noise
does not enter it.

\emph{Taking the realisation out is what makes the difference readable.} With $\Delta$ the whitened
difference of the two arms at their own minima, $\chi^{2}$ decomposes exactly as $282.96=177.88+27.82+77.26$:
\emph{the model difference carries three quarters of the excess and the cross term a quarter}, so the
rejection is not this sky's luck --- set the cross term to its expectation and this arm still scores $1.43$
per bin against the control's $0.99$ on a typical sky. \emph{And $\Delta$ is trough-dominated}: $+0.011$ at
the peaks against $-0.760$ at the troughs, so the two arms agree about where the peaks are and differ between
them. Split about a smooth envelope, the oscillatory part carries seven tenths of it and is peak-high and
trough-low---\emph{antisymmetric about the envelope rather than about each peak, which is a contrast and not
a phase and not an envelope}. This arm's acoustic oscillation is $1.040$ times the control's about its own
envelope, and one direction built from the control alone---raise the oscillation at fixed envelope, with
nothing fitted---carries half of
$\lVert\Delta\rVert^{2}$\rcpt{P15_the_model_difference_is_an_acoustic_contrast_difference_and_it_is_not_one_skys_luck}\rcpt{P15_the_monopole_zero_point_is_not_the_channel_and_the_two_residuals_point_opposite_ways}.

\emph{Decomposed about its own envelope the excess is an amplitude and not a lopsidedness}: splitting the band
variance of the normalised oscillation at its zero gives $1.067$ on the peak side against $1.056$ on the
trough side, and at the located extrema themselves the arm exceeds the control by $5.7$ per cent at the maxima
against $5.3$ at the minima. \emph{So heights and depths carry it equally}---and $\Delta$'s trough dominance is
a property of the whitened residual rather than of the contrast, the two not being the same decomposition.
\emph{The trough depths are nevertheless where the excess becomes observable, and for a reason that is the
sky's rather than the model's}: the arm exceeds the control by the same amount in each, $+0.0106$ in depth
against $+0.0103$ in height, while the sky measures depth twice as precisely---so the same excess reads at
$1.8$ of the sky's own spread in the depths and $0.9$ in the heights. \emph{And the depths are the one
statistic in this comparison whose residual is not shared}: the control lands on the measured trough depths at
five hundredths of a standard deviation while this arm sits $1.8$ above
them\rcpt{P15_the_contrast_excess_is_symmetric_about_the_envelope_and_the_troughs_are_where_the_sky_measures_it_best}.
\emph{That is a shape the data can see and not a detection}, at the spread one statistic carries; and the
locating must be anchored to the comb rather than searched for, a free search under the same noise returning a
spread six times wider because it latches onto noise minima. \emph{One systematic is stated rather than
minimised}: the envelope's abscissa moves the measured depth by about a third of that spread across the
admissible acoustic scales.

\emph{And the three features this arm's residual shows and the control's does not are all $\Delta$'s}: the
peak-and-trough asymmetry, the $\ell\simeq1000$ trough where $\Delta$ carries $-1.074$ of the arm's $-1.177$
in fifteen bins, and the growth with multipole---\emph{which $\Delta$ does monotonically where neither
residual does}, a residual being $\Delta$ on a noise floor of order one that flattens
it\rcpt{P15_the_model_difference_is_an_acoustic_contrast_difference_and_it_is_not_one_skys_luck}.
\emph{The channel is identified and the cause is not}: the contrast direction is the balance between the
monopole and the Doppler terms of the source, bracketed with opposite signs by removing each and
re-projecting, on both instrument paths and with the same signs on both; and \emph{neither deletion
returns the two arms to a common contrast}, so what the bracket locates is where the difference enters
rather than what produces
it\rcpt{P15_the_source_decomposition_is_reachable_on_the_reporting_path_and_the_neutrinos_have_a_knob}.
No mechanism for the imbalance is offered here. \emph{And the neutrinos' free-streaming, which the shape
invites, is not the difference either}: removing it moves both arms' peaks by the same amount---band by
band the two agree to a fifth of a multipole, and in units of each arm's own acoustic scale to two parts
in ten thousand---while its large effect falls on the envelope rather than on the contrast. \emph{A
correction the two arms share cannot be what separates
them}\rcpt{P15_the_free_streaming_knob_is_common_to_both_arms_and_the_switch_sweep_finds_no_further_shadow}.

\emph{And the trough-filling a contrast excess would be a shortage of is not short on this arm.} Four
channels carry that, each measured and each larger here. Deeper troughs are what excess baryon
loading produces, through the displacement of the oscillation's zero point---and measured against each arm's
own loading, \emph{this arm's monopole swings about a point that falls short of its own $-R\Psi$}, by an
effective baryon density of $-2.9$ per cent, which is the wrong sign for that reading. And the visibility is \emph{wider}, $43.6$ against $38.0$~Mpc, so this arm averages over more acoustic
phase and not less. \emph{So the channels that would each shallow the troughs are larger here}, and the same
is true of the driving, whose measured grip is weaker.

\emph{And the Doppler term, which fills troughs, is stronger here}: the dipole-to-monopole ratio of
\emph{oscillation amplitudes} at the visibility peak sits two per cent above the control's and rises with
wavenumber. \emph{Which of two ratios that is matters, and the distinction is the physics rather than a
convention}: trough-filling is an oscillating term a quarter period out of phase adding into another's
troughs, so \emph{what fills a trough is an oscillating amplitude}, while the smooth part of the same field
displaces the envelope and fills nothing. A ratio of band-integrated powers mixes the two and reads the
opposite sign on the same spectra---\emph{so the two readings are different quantities and not a disagreement
about one}, and the one this comparison needs is the amplitude ratio\rcpt{P15_the_two_readings_are_different_quantities_and_the_projection_width_is_the_unmeasured_one}.

\emph{One channel does carry a definite share, and it is the first in this comparison that does.} The
source is weighted across the visibility by a function rather than a constant, and that weighting is
not the same on the two arms: read in the phase the oscillation actually accumulates, the window's
spread is $0.0652$ of the acoustic scale on the control against $0.0642$ on this arm. \emph{That the two
are within a per cent and a half of each other is itself the two-rate structure, and it is a
cancellation rather than an absence}---the arm's visibility is fourteen per cent wider in conformal
time while its phase accumulates fourteen per cent more slowly per unit of it, and the product of the
two comes to within one part in ninety of unity. \emph{Imposing one arm's weighting on the other moves
the contrast the way a narrower window should}, and the size can be read two ways that share no
machinery: from the instrument's own response to the weighting, which gives between a quarter and a
half of the band excess above the damping scale, and from the direct substitution, which closes
between a fifth and two fifths of it. \emph{So the weighting is a real channel carrying about a third,
measured rather than modelled, and from a quantity nobody chose}.

\emph{And it cannot be the whole of it, for a reason that is independent of every one of those sizes.}
The excess is symmetric about the envelope, heights and depths carrying it equally; the weighting is
not. Imposed at the arm's own size it delivers four fifths of the peak-height excess and one seventh of
the trough-depth excess---\emph{a five-fold asymmetry against a symmetric
object}\rcpt{P15_the_windows_phase_spread_is_nearly_equal_because_the_visibility_widens_as_the_leaf_clock_slows}.
\emph{A peak-weighted channel cannot carry a symmetric excess alone, whatever its share}, and two
further readings agree without being needed: the measured response extrapolates to a non-zero offset at
zero wavenumber, where a window's own characteristic function is identically one, and a weighting large
enough to deliver the whole excess moves the first peak by eighteen multipoles and the heights by nearly
half. \emph{So what this settles is a share and not a mechanism.}

\emph{A second channel carries the other half of the shape, and it is the term mix rather than the window.}
The two arms hold different fractions of their window's power in the monopole, and imposing one on the other
at a coefficient solved from the profiles themselves---one constant to two per cent across every band---moves
the contrast with the signature the long-wavelength offset needs: \emph{the response is independent of
wavenumber}, an intercept against a slope worth three per cent of it, where a window's smearing must die at
long wavelength. \emph{It is the first channel measured to have that shape.} \emph{And it is not the
remainder either, for the same kind of reason the window was not}: it is peak-weighted where what the window
leaves is trough-weighted, it over-delivers the whole excess by between one and three times, and it moves the
first two peaks the wrong way and outside the widths the sky's own locating carries\rcpt{P15_the_term_mix_has_the_shape_the_offset_needs_and_over_delivers_its_size}.

\emph{And the two together are what the measurement actually settles, because their shares do not add to
what is observed but to something larger}: a third from the window and above one and a half from the term
mix make more than one and a half times the excess. \emph{How two such channels compose is then a
measurement rather than an assumption}, and it is made by imposing both on one control spectrum at the
sizes their own comparisons solved, with neither coefficient re-chosen and no third channel admitted.

\emph{The rule is multiplicative.} The joint response lands within a pre-registered tolerance of the
product in every band and of the sum in every band, and of a quadrature combination in none of
them---\emph{the last by three and a half times that tolerance, which is the one separation this
measurement can make and the only one claimed}. That the product and the sum differ here by less than a
third of the tolerance was declared before the run, so they are not separated and are not reported as
though they were. \emph{The pair is slightly sub-multiplicative and consistently so}, below both the
product and the sum in every band and above quadrature in every band. \emph{And the composed pair
reproduces the two shares' arithmetic directly}: what adding the separate measurements put at a little
over one and a half times the excess reads, composed in one spectrum, within one per cent of that
value\rcpt{P15_the_two_channels_compose_multiplicatively_and_the_pair_is_flat_where_the_excess_grows}.

\emph{So the remainder left by dividing one response into the excess is a well-defined object, and the
over-delivery is evidence about the sizes rather than about the rule.} \emph{What does not survive is a size
reading of it at all, and the reason is a shape.} The joint response is the flattest thing this comparison
has measured, and the excess is not flat: the pair over-delivers by nearly four times at the longest
wavelengths and by under one and a half at the shortest. \emph{No coefficient on either channel reproduces
the excess}---set to match at long wavelength it falls short at short, and set to match at short it is an
order too large at long---\emph{which is an obstruction of a different kind from a size discrepancy, because
a size discrepancy is what a coefficient absorbs and a shape mismatch is not}. \emph{And that reading is
available only because the rule was measured}: with the rule assumed, the same numbers read as a size a
smaller coefficient could take up.

\emph{What the excess's own dependence on wavenumber is has then to be measured before it can be searched
for, and the instrument is what has to be measured first.} Six forms injected into the control's own
spectrum, two of them carrying a scale, are recovered to about a per cent. \emph{But the estimator
manufactures structure between bands}: its bias varies from band to band by about a per cent while the six
injected forms agree with each other on that bias seven times more closely, \emph{so a contrast that is
constant reads as a wobble, and band ordering at the per-cent level is a property of the statistic and not
of the spectrum}. \emph{One alternative anchoring is excluded by its mechanism rather than by its number}:
a median envelope returns a value three times larger, and the tell is that it does not move with its own
smoothing window---at short wavelength the running median collapses onto the curve it is meant to be an
envelope of, a median of a locally monotone stretch being its central value whatever the window. \emph{So
the rise is real and the wobble is not}, and the distinction is established on the instrument before
anything is concluded from it\rcpt{P15_the_excess_decelerates_and_nothing_measured_in_this_arm_carries_its_wavenumber_dependence}.

\emph{And the rise decelerates within the statistic as defined, which excludes the form this comparison's
own statistics are written in.} A departure growing linearly in the squared wavenumber is rejected---the
only one of the six so rejected---and every concave form is admitted with none preferred among them, so
\emph{the direction is settled and the functional form is not}. \emph{No scale is separated from another
either}, and the spread that comparison is judged against is the fit's own injection-and-recovery error rather
than a noise level. \emph{And two
things about that deceleration are stated here because a reader would otherwise assume both.} It is
\emph{robust within the statistic}---negative under every variation of the envelope's width, the band edges
and the sampling---\emph{and it is carried by the lowest band alone}: with that band set aside the sign is
not determined at all, the deceleration being that band lying low rather than the upper bands bending.
\emph{And it does not survive a change of envelope}: a running median in place of the arithmetic one, with
everything else held, reverses it. \emph{That is a different statistic and the difference is measured}---the
median absorbs about half the highest band's oscillation on both arms, so its ratios are formed on a much
smaller residual---\emph{but it is not on its face the worse one, since it leaves a departure of zero mean
where the other does not}. \emph{So the sign of the curvature belongs to the statistic and not yet to the
excess}\rcpt{P15_the_lowest_band_is_closed_by_the_fields_and_the_excess_deceleration_is_carried_by_it_alone}. \emph{And the rise is a preference
over flatness rather than an exclusion of it}, which is stated because the difference matters: a constant
departure is a worse description, not an impossible one. \emph{The consequence for the numbers above is
that a slope read against an intercept is a description and not a model}, and the growth of the departure
itself is the scale-free quantity that comparison needs.

\emph{Read with that quantity, the search this comparison can run in the positive direction leaves exactly
one candidate standing.} The excess's departure grows by a factor of three and a half across the range with
negative curvature, which gives two conditions rather than one, and \emph{the source terms and the three
channels with measured shares all fail the first}: they are flat where the excess grows. \emph{The dipole's
amplitude ratio does not}, and the range over which that can be said has since been extended by an estimator
built for it: holding the oscillation's period and fitting its amplitude and phase, rather than reading an
amplitude off a window, reaches well below the window estimator's floor. \emph{Its own first finding is that
the period is not the period}---set by construction to one value, it runs about two per cent short of it in
the fields and by a \emph{different} amount for the two of them, so the period is measured per field and per
arm rather than assumed. \emph{And it is validated before it is used}: the two estimators agree on the mean
where both are sound, and where the window estimator does not survive its own width---its ripple running from
under one per cent to above ten as the window narrows---the held-period one stays near two and a half per cent
at every width. \emph{The one cost it carries is measured rather than noted}, and it is half a per cent of the
