dependence being measured, for a mechanism: the fit re-fits the phase in every window, so an error in the
held period is absorbed there and costs only a common amplitude factor, which cancels in a ratio of ratios.

\emph{On the range that unlocks, the candidate's departure is positive in every band as the target's is and
grows faster}, so \emph{it passes both of the conditions that can be applied to it}. \emph{And it stops where the fields stop rather than where the method does, at a floor that is exact and
was named before it was used.} An oscillation's amplitude is identified at a point only if the window holds
a turning point on either side of it, because across a span with no turning point the held-period pair is
monotone and the baseline already spans it. \emph{The monopole has no turning point below its first}---the
first excursion is one-sided---\emph{and the monopole is what binds}, putting the floor well above the
lowest band, which lies far below it while every other band lies entirely above. \emph{And the floor is the
fields' and not the window's, shown three ways}: the same widths spread four times as far below it as above
and change sign there; sweeping the window's edge, the departure is negative for every window that opens
past the monopole's first turning point and positive for every one that stays above it, \emph{what lies
below being the field's rise from its initial condition rather than an oscillation at all}; and every centre
below the floor spreads more than every centre above it, which the floor was not fitted to. \emph{So no
estimator of an oscillation amplitude can reach inside the first excursion}---an acoustic oscillation has a
first extremum, and before it there is no amplitude because there is no oscillation\rcpt{P15_the_held_period_estimator_reaches_below_the_floor_and_the_tooth_that_would_decide_cannot_be_applied}.

\emph{And the condition that would decide cannot be applied, for an exact reason rather than a statistical
one.} The excess decelerates across the full range, and \emph{that deceleration is carried entirely by the one
band no estimator of the candidate can reach}: removing that band alone reverses the sign of the excess's own
curvature, while removing any other single band leaves it where it was. \emph{So the filter is out of
conditions rather than the candidate out of chances}---the candidate's own curvature is stable there, and what
is missing is the target's sign and not the candidate's number. \emph{That is a statement about the filter, and
it was one of the two outcomes written down before the estimator was built}\rcpt{P15_the_held_period_estimator_reaches_below_the_floor_and_the_tooth_that_would_decide_cannot_be_applied}.

\emph{And the way that condition is unavailable is worse than a simple absence, which is worth stating
plainly.} It needs a value in the band the fields close, and that band is shut to \emph{amplitude}
estimators but not to a candidate computed from the projection's kernel---which had one, and which the
condition duly excluded. \emph{So the filter's full strength is available exactly for the class that cannot
carry the excess, and its third condition is permanently unavailable exactly for the class that can},
because what fills a trough is an oscillating amplitude and that is what closes the band.

\emph{Which leaves the question of what a two-condition filter is, and the answer is that it is what it
always was.} \emph{As a device for excluding it has lost nothing}: each remaining condition is necessary,
and every exclusion this comparison has made was made on growth alone. \emph{As a device for confirming it
was never one, with three conditions or with two}---all three are conditions on shape, and the surviving
candidate matches the excess in sign and in direction of growth while being several times too small at the
highest band. \emph{So the question this comparison now carries is the coupling and not the shape}: a
quantitative question, living where the candidate is measured, and needing the closed band not at all.

\emph{That coupling is measured, and its sign comes out the same everywhere.} Scaling the Doppler source
across three banked spectra gives the contrast's response as a finite difference---\emph{three points, so
linearity is checked rather than assumed}---and the response steepens towards the unscaled value, so the
slope there is both the applicable one and the one taken over the shortest step. \emph{It is negative at
every band, on both envelopes, over every interval}: forty-two readings of the sign and not one positive.
\emph{Which is this comparison's own trough-filling physics read forwards}---more of an oscillating term in
quadrature fills more trough and \emph{lowers} the contrast.

\emph{And the construction fixes it, shown by what fails to change it.} The control's own ratio of that
term's band power to the monopole's runs across a factor of more than six from the lowest band to the
highest, \emph{and the coupling's sign is the same at every one}. \emph{A quantity whose sign is invariant
across a factor of six in the very ratio it depends on is not set by a knob}: the knob is the probe and not
the setter, so the size is a prediction rather than a fit.

\emph{And it is measured on the arm itself rather than carried across from the control.} The decomposition the
instrument already writes resolves each spectrum into its bilinear terms multipole by multipole---ten of them,
summing to the spectrum to a part in $10^{15}$---and their scaling in the source amplitude is exact, quadratic
on the term's own square and linear on each of its crosses, so the response follows analytically on either arm
rather than from a difference of runs. \emph{It runs from $-0.48$ to $-0.86$ across the bands, the two arms
agreeing to a few per cent and each agreeing with the finite difference above to better than a
tenth}---\emph{the same quantity reached two ways, on the arm's own spectrum}.

\emph{The contribution the coupling multiplies is then read by a swap rather than by a product, which is what
removes the ambiguity in its input.} Replacing the arm's four Doppler-carrying bilinear terms by the
control's, envelope-scaled and on the arm's own grid, gives the share of the excess those terms carry as a
ratio of contrast differences---\emph{the same operation the instrument already sanctions for the clock, and
with no coupling-times-amplitude product anywhere in it}. \emph{The answer is a minority share on either
envelope}: from a per cent to a quarter of the excess against the arithmetic envelope, and from a tenth to a
half against the median one. \emph{So the Doppler term is a real contributor and it is not the carrier.}

\emph{And the share grows with wavenumber while being sign-indefinite at the bottom}---negative at the second
band on both envelopes---\emph{so at the bands where the excess carries its own structure it changes sign
rather than settling to a share}. \emph{Which is exactly how far the envelope ambiguity reaches}: the coupling's sign survives both
envelopes at every band, so the contribution does not reverse with the statistic, while the share's magnitude
moves by about a factor of two between them---\emph{so the share inherits that ambiguity where the sign does
not}\rcpt{P15_the_bank_already_existed_and_the_doppler_carries_a_growing_minority_of_the_excess}.

\emph{And the excess every candidate here is measured against has one dominant feature, which is a step and
not a trend.} Its lowest band is a fraction of the mean of the others and sits below them by nearly five times
the spread the upper bands themselves show about their own trend, while above that band a constant already
fits---a line adds a mild slope and halves the residual and no more. \emph{So the bands above the lowest carry very little structure with which to tell a flat candidate from
a rising one}, and that stretch is where every shape comparison across wavenumber is made: a flat candidate
matches the featureless part and misses the step, a rising one matches the mild rise and misses the step, and
across those bands the two are barely distinguishable.

\emph{That the step is the excess's own and not the statistic's is established before anything is read off
it, and by a reading that uses no statistic at all.} Three running envelopes differing in \emph{kind}---an
arithmetic mean, a median and a local quadratic---and a fourth quantity that needs no window whatever, the
peak-to-trough depth of each acoustic cycle, all put the lowest band below the rest, and none of them puts it
near parity. \emph{What the window-free family settles is the step's freedom from the statistic and not its
size}: in the lowest band it holds a single half-cycle transition, the first locatable pair of extrema lying
above that band's lower edge, so what it returns there is consistent with zero and equally with no step at
all---\emph{unresolved rather than small}. An extremal envelope built from located extrema alone reaches
only a quarter of that band, rests on the same single extremum below the step and interpolates across it.
\emph{So the size is read from the statistics that resolve the band, and on every one of them the lowest
band runs between a third and two thirds of the rest}---a range because the statistic moves it, and a range
that excludes parity throughout. \emph{And the
one artefact that could produce a step at the lowest band is excluded in the direction that excludes it}: the
band's envelope comes within five multipoles of truncating, and moving the band's lower edge \emph{away} from
that truncation makes it read \emph{higher}, where an artefact of truncation would have to make it read lower.

\emph{Located rather than merely noticed, the step sits at the second acoustic peak.} Its half-rise point,
taken in sliding windows at three widths, is stable to better than a per cent and lands within about one per
cent of the second peak on either arm, where the nearest other scale this construction fixes is seven times
further away; and it is sharp, a clean factor of two on all three widths reached within about a tenth of a
comb period---\emph{narrower than the narrowest window that resolves it}. \emph{So the excess in the first
acoustic cycle runs between a third and two thirds of its size in every cycle above.} \emph{What that locates is not a length
belonging to this arm}: the peaks sit at the same wavenumber on both arms, so the step says that the first
acoustic cycle behaves differently rather than that some new scale has entered.

\emph{Scored against that feature rather than against a trend, every candidate in this comparison is
evaluable, and between them they carry a minority of the step.} A candidate's step is its lowest band's
departure from the trend its own upper bands set, read on four bases---linear and logarithmic, in the
wavenumber and in its square---with a departure counted only where it survives all four. \emph{The window
weighting steps the wrong way}, upward by about half, working against the step rather than making it; \emph{the
term mix steps the right way} at some two thirds of the excess's own departure; and \emph{the pair as
actually composed}---one spectrum with both coefficients at the sizes their own profiles solve---\emph{carries
about two fifths of the excess's own step}, exceeding twice that same upper-band spread on three of the four bases; the
share is against the excess's own extrapolated size and not against the larger step the arms have in common,
the two denominators differing by about a factor of six on the one departure. \emph{The
projection width carries none, and the basis family is what shows why}: being linear in wavenumber to a few
per cent of its own range, it flips sign across the four, so a logarithmic basis would manufacture a step
for it. And the Doppler contribution, read at the lowest band by the swap above, changes sign repeatedly in
sliding half-period windows with means on either side of the step equal and both near zero---\emph{scatter at
the step's own resolution, and no transition at all}. \emph{So against that
statistic the step has a partial account}: no single channel produces it and two of them together reach a
minority of it.

\emph{Scored instead on the differential estimator---where the shared nine tenths never enters and only the
part belonging to this cosmology remains---the same channels read differently, and the accounting does not
survive the change.} Each knob is read there as it must be, by the width of its own difference from the
control, against the arm's. \emph{The window weighting steps, and steps the opposite way to the arm}; \emph{the
term mix no longer steps at all} at that resolution, where against the mostly-shared quantity it reached two
thirds; and \emph{the realised pair changes sign across the bases and measures zero to within its own
scatter}, where against the mostly-shared quantity it reached two fifths. \emph{So a channel that accounts for
a substantial share of a quantity nine tenths of which is common to both cosmologies accounts for nothing of
the part that is this one's.} \emph{The weaker word is the supported one}: those two are not resolved on the
differential estimator, which is not the same as being excluded.

\emph{And they cannot be resolved by the family of statistics this comparison is built on, which is a
statement about that family proved over its whole reach rather than inferred from a failure.} A channel is
decided when its own departure at the lowest band exceeds
twice the scatter of its upper bands, so the smallest share of the step a channel could carry and still be
decided is a computable floor. \emph{Every route this construction can take to lower that floor was taken}:
both aggregations of the width, both pairings of the two arms' multipoles, a wider span of bands and a finer
division of the same span. \emph{The floor does not move below three fifths of the step, where the two
channels carry about a third and about a thirtieth.} \emph{And the reason it does not move is worth the
sentence}: the phase-insensitive aggregation lowers the scatter by the factor predicted and lowers the signal
by the same factor at the same time, so \emph{a reduction in scatter is a gain only where the signal does not
fall with it}. \emph{More of the same statistic would therefore decide nothing.}

\emph{Nor would it, for a second and independent reason.} Of the three channel readings, only the one that
steps the wrong way holds its sign across every aggregation; \emph{the other two change sign between two
legitimate aggregations of the same statistic}, so their departures are not established quantities that a
sharper measurement would sharpen. \emph{What is demonstrated is accordingly narrower than that the channels
do not produce the step, and narrower is what is earned}: \emph{no statistic of this family could tell whether
they do}\rcpt{P15_no_statistic_this_construction_can_build_resolves_the_two_channels_and_the_phase_step_dies_on_a_matched_width_control}.

\emph{And the family is what the limit belongs to rather than the comparison, because the likelihood these
spectra are confronted with does not share it.} Its bins are a thirty-fourth of a comb period where every
statistic above averages over seven tenths of one, two dozen of them fall inside the lowest band, and its
bin-to-bin correlation is a flat floor rather than a coupling that grows across a period---\emph{so it bins
without banding, and it does not inherit the width limit through its covariance either}. \emph{Read band by
band from its own bins, the two arms are distinguishable everywhere, and at the lowest band the step appears in
that distinguishability}---standing where its upper bands' trend predicts half again as much, with the same
sign as every other reading of it and with the instrument's own noise for an uncertainty rather than a scatter
taken across a noiseless theory spectrum. \emph{And the two channels separate from each other there at some
twenty-five standard deviations}, which is the measurement the banded family cannot make at all. \emph{The two
floors are not one number and neither bounds the other}---one a share of the lowest band's difference under
real instrument noise, the other a share of the step under an empirical scatter of a noiseless
spectrum---\emph{so what compares is the verdicts}: the banded family cannot separate the channels there and
the likelihood separates them\rcpt{P15_the_likelihood_sees_the_step_and_separates_the_two_channels_so_the_demonstration_does_not_cover_it}.

\emph{But what the data can distinguish and what it rejects the arm on are two quantities, and at the lowest
band they part company completely.} Of the excess in fit that the covered bands carry between them, the lowest
band holds \emph{under one per cent}---\emph{and that share changes sign} as smooth global shape is absorbed
into free coefficients, where every band above it holds to within a few per cent of its value. \emph{So the
step is not a distinct feature of the residual and not the shape rejection read locally either, the shape
rejection being barely present there at all}: \emph{it lives in the upper half of the range, where four bands
carry between them some six sevenths of the total.} \emph{That location is read off an exact decomposition and
not a per-band approximation}: because the two arms' residuals differ by the difference of the spectra, the
excess is identically a term in that difference alone plus a term coupling it to the control's own residual,
and the two reproduce the total to a part in $10^{13}$. \emph{And the split is the distinction itself written
as algebra}---the first term never asks where the data sits and the second is the only part that
does---\emph{which is why a quantity can stand at some twenty-five standard deviations in the one and at
nothing in the other}. \emph{Which puts the comparison's own structure and the data's own verdict in different
places}---the excess's one prominent feature across wavenumber sits where the rejection is cheapest, and the
rejection sits where no smooth shape across wavenumber finds anything at all.

\emph{But the rejection is not a level there, and what it does carry is acoustic.} Across the individual bins
those four bands hold, a constant accounts for none of the scatter, a trend for under a fiftieth of it and a
quadratic for under a thirtieth---\emph{and a projection at the comb's own period is reached by none of two
thousand draws of the instrument's own covariance carried through the same statistic, nor by any of twenty
thousand when they are pooled}. \emph{The period it sits at is fixed only to the resolution the range allows}: the peak
spans periods $0.88$ to $1.19$, which is the acoustic period to within the resolution of the two and four fifths
periods of data the bands cover, and not to a hundredth of it.

\emph{And no banded reading of that stretch can find it, which is not attenuation but a limit of
representation.} One number per band at seven tenths of a period spacing has a Nyquist period of twice that, so
a modulation at the comb period lies beyond what such numbers can represent at all, and the averaging inside
each band passes barely more than a third of it before that. \emph{The likelihood bins thirty-four times finer},
at a Nyquist period a twentieth of the comb's and a transfer indistinguishable from unity at it---\emph{so the
instrument that rejects the arm is the one instrument in this comparison that can represent what the rejection
carries}, and the limit that closed the lowest band is the same limit, met at the other end of the
range\rcpt{P15_the_resolution_table_eight_of_the_eleven_instruments_cannot_represent_the_comb_period_and_four_smallest_shares_are_honest_blanks}.

\emph{And the modulation is a fact about the data rather than the difference looking at itself}---the difference
is a comb, so the question is which term carries it, and five sixths of it comes from the term that couples the
difference to the control's residual against one sixth from the term in the difference alone. \emph{The first
clears the same noise null and the second has no noise in it to clear}, being a property of the difference and
not of the
data\rcpt{P15_the_term_mix_is_the_first_channel_to_produce_cost_where_the_arm_is_rejected_and_the_excess_there_is_combed_at_the_acoustic_period}.

\emph{Scored in the metric the step does live in, every candidate departs the way the arm does.} Read as each
channel's own lowest-band departure from the trend its upper bands set, in the likelihood's units, the window
weighting delivers about a third more than the arm's, the term mix about six sevenths of it, and the pair as
actually composed about half---\emph{the first time this comparison has held candidates that depart the arm's
way on an instrument able to carry the question}. \emph{And the window weighting's verdict differs between
instruments}, going the wrong way on the differential estimator and the right way here, \emph{which is reported
as a disagreement between instruments rather than resolved into a story}. \emph{The pair again falls well short
of every composition rule}, product, sum and quadrature alike, delivering about a third of the closest.
\emph{And what that costs the account belongs in the same breath as the shares}: these are shares of a
departure in what the data can distinguish, and that feature carries one part in a hundred of what the data
rejects the arm
on\rcpt{P15_the_band_one_departure_is_not_in_the_likelihoods_excess_and_all_three_channels_match_it_in_separating_power}.

\emph{Scored instead in the metric the data's own rejection lives in, the channels sort differently, and one of
them sorts the right way.} Read across the four bands that carry the rejection, the term mix delivers about
four thirds of the arm's cost \emph{and delivers it where the arm's falls}---the two patterns correlating at
seven tenths band by band, on a regression slope of three quarters, with nearly half the arm's cost left
unexplained. \emph{That is the first channel this comparison has held whose cost is in the right places at
all}, and it is neither the carrier identified nor no carrier found: \emph{four thirds of the cost at that
correlation is not the absence of a quantity, and half of it misplaced is not the presence of one}.
\emph{The window weighting is the contrary case, and its small share is the wrong reading of it}: its cost is
\emph{anti}-correlated with the arm's at seven tenths, \emph{so it is a different cost and not a small share of
the same one}---the reverse of its standing on separating power, where it led. \emph{And the pair as composed
matches worse than the term mix alone}, raising the total while degrading the pattern, \emph{which is the
sharpest form in which this comparison can say that a total is not a match}: any spectrum that is not the
control costs something, so what separates costing the arm's way from merely also being rejected is the pattern
and never the share.

\emph{Whether that channel carries what the cost is made of, as against carrying the cost in the right bands, is
a second question, and against the instrument's own noise the two do not come apart.} Projected at the comb
period and scored against two thousand draws of the covariance through the same statistic, the channel's own
cost is reached by no draw, and neither is the part of the arm's excess the channel does not explain---the latter
at the arm's own phase to within a sixth of a radian. The part the channel misplaces is reached by none either,
and is not quoted with them. \emph{The statistic quoted is the tail count and not the ratio to the ensemble's
largest value}, that maximum being itself a noisy quantity whose spread across seeds is about a tenth, where the
tail count is stable and holds when the draws are pooled tenfold. \emph{The channel's
own clearance is carried by the term that knows where the data sits}, its other term being one the null holds
fixed and therefore one no draw could have made it fail. \emph{So every piece of this decomposition is
acoustically modulated, which is what a detection test can establish here and is not what authorship
needs}---\emph{a knob that moves the balance of two oscillating source terms produces an oscillating cost by
construction, so clearing a noise floor does not distinguish a carrier from a participant}. \emph{A null built
instead from the same data at neighbouring frequencies cannot be used for the question at all}, its amplitudes
carrying the feature they are meant to score: over this range such an ensemble spans only about three
independent draws, and its largest values run twice those of the instrument's own
noise\rcpt{P15_all_three_projections_clear_the_instrument_noise_null_so_the_term_mixs_cost_is_combed_after_all}\rcpt{P15_the_term_mix_does_not_carry_the_modulation_and_it_survives_in_the_part_no_channel_explains}\rcpt{P15_the_resolution_table_eight_of_the_eleven_instruments_cannot_represent_the_comb_period_and_four_smallest_shares_are_honest_blanks}.

\emph{And the anti-correlated channel is combed too, in antiphase, which locates the reversal without naming
it.} Its own cost projects at the comb period above its null with a phase $\pi$ from the arm's to within a
$0.021$ rad of $\pi$, so it is a structure and not a smooth offset. \emph{Yet the two channels' modulations in
the spectra themselves are in phase}, each a quarter to a third of a radian from the arm's, and the smaller
one's amplitude predicts the larger's to within about an eighth from that ratio alone---\emph{so the magnitude composes and the sign does
not, and the reversal appears only after the likelihood's own weighting is applied}. \emph{What that is not is
one structure read with two signs}: the window's spectrum difference is not a negative multiple of the arm's,
the residual of the attempt keeping almost all of the window's power\rcpt{P15_the_term_mix_does_not_carry_the_modulation_and_it_survives_in_the_part_no_channel_explains}.

\emph{The accounting that would settle authorship closes exactly and still cannot attribute, and the reason is
an identity rather than a want of precision.} Writing each channel's modulation as a vector in the plane the
cosine and sine of the comb span, the channel's contribution and the residue left after it reconstruct the arm's
own vector to numerical precision---\emph{so it is an accounting and not an estimate}. \emph{But the share a
least-squares projection returns in such a plane is the cosine of the phase offset and nothing else}: it depends
only on the direction the channel points and not at all on how large it is, so \emph{a channel a millionth the
size scores the same}. \emph{And the anti-correlated channel is the proof rather than an illustration}---pointing
$\pi$ from the arm it is assigned essentially all of the authorship, at a negative scale that turns its own cost
upside down to reach it. \emph{A quantity that credits a channel with the whole of a feature for pointing the
opposite way is not measuring authorship.} \emph{The other available scale, the ratio of the two modulations in
the spectra themselves, fails differently and by measurement}: it predicts the realised amplitude for one channel
to about an eighth and misses for the other by more than that channel's whole size. \emph{So one scale falls to
algebra and the other to measurement, and neither failure is the other one twice.}

\emph{And the second scale has no statistical content to appeal to, which closes the last route.} The difference
between the two spectra moves with the data only through two fitted amplitudes, so a share or a phase read at
that level is a property of the two theories with a systematic uncertainty and \emph{no statistical floor at
all}---the same thing the contrast statistic is, and for the same reason. \emph{At the level of the fit the floors
are finite}, about a twentieth of a radian in phase and a few per cent in ratio, \emph{but it is there that the
projection is amplitude-blind}. \emph{And the degeneracy is exactly dimensional}: two channels span the plane, so
any two of them reconstruct the arm exactly and do it as arithmetic. \emph{So what this comparison leaves is a
discrepancy whose size, location, period and shape are measured and whose carrier no instrument it holds can
attribute}---and what attribution would take is named rather than awaited: \emph{a channel independent of the
plane these two span, or an estimator that uses the channels' amplitudes where those amplitudes carry statistical
content}. \emph{Neither is a refinement of the instruments above, which is why the question is recorded as
located rather than
open}\rcpt{P15_the_accounting_closes_but_cannot_attribute_because_the_share_is_the_cosine_of_a_phase_offset}\rcpt{P15_the_null_harness_survives_its_one_assumption_except_for_the_misplaced_part_and_floors_a_phase_at_the_excess_but_not_at_the_spectrum}.

\emph{And the step does not compose the way the contrast does, which is a property of the step rather than of
the knobs.} The contrast composes multiplicatively, measured band by band. On the step, the two channels' own
departures put a product, a sum and a quadrature all at a large \emph{positive} value where the realised pair
measures zero to within its scatter on the phase-dependent aggregation, every rule several scatters
away---\emph{so on that reading the two very nearly cancel on the step where they multiply on the contrast}.
\emph{The cancellation is a property of the reading and not of the pair, and that is measured rather than
left open}: it vanishes within its own scatter on every basis of the phase-dependent aggregation and on none
of the phase-insensitive one, and in the metric the data's own rejection lives in \emph{the two channels add},
the realised pair standing at the sum of the two singles to within a fiftieth---\emph{so of the three metrics
that can carry the question, two show the cancellation and the third shows the contrary, which is what makes it
the reading's property and not the pair's}. \emph{The
residuals are quoted in departure units against that scatter and not as a share}, a fraction of a near-zero
measurement being no fraction at
all\rcpt{P15_the_surviving_step_is_amplitude_and_not_phase_and_the_candidate_shares_do_not_survive_the_change_of_denominator}\rcpt{P15_the_term_mix_is_the_first_channel_to_produce_cost_where_the_arm_is_rejected_and_the_excess_there_is_combed_at_the_acoustic_period}.

\emph{What remains to be explained is accordingly what distinguishes the first acoustic cycle from the
rest}\rcpt{P15_the_window_free_reading_cannot_resolve_the_first_cycle_the_flatness_was_a_trend_and_nine_tenths_of_the_step_is_common_to_both_arms}\rcpt{P15_the_lowest_band_is_closed_by_the_fields_and_the_excess_deceleration_is_carried_by_it_alone}.

\emph{And the step is very largely common to the two arms, which is the decomposition that says where it
belongs.} Read on each arm's own contrast against the trend its own upper bands set, with no reference to
the other arm, \emph{both arms step upward at the lowest band}---on all four bases and on the windowed and
window-free contrasts alike---\emph{and this arm's step is always the shallower, by about a tenth and no
more}. \emph{So roughly nine tenths of the step is shared and cancels in the ratio, and the excess's step is
the part that fails to cancel}: the feature belongs to the acoustic physics the two arms have in common, and
what this cosmology contributes is the small residual---\emph{which is also why the step's location is the
same wavenumber on both}. \emph{That residual is readable because the ratio is smoother than either spectrum},
standing at some seven times its own upper-band spread where each arm's own step stands at four. \emph{And a small residual of two large common features is exactly where a nearly-common
systematic would sit}, so the feature is read again on an estimator that is differential by construction
rather than a difference of two large
numbers\rcpt{P15_the_window_free_reading_cannot_resolve_the_first_cycle_the_flatness_was_a_trend_and_nine_tenths_of_the_step_is_common_to_both_arms}.

\emph{On that estimator it survives.} Dividing the two spectra multipole by multipole before any width is
taken leaves the common oscillation cancelled rather than subtracted, so the quantity read is the width of
the \emph{difference} of the two arms' oscillations and the shared step is never formed: the lowest band's
departure is negative on all four trend bases, on both pairings of the two arms' multipoles, and on both
aggregations of the width---\emph{a band's own dispersion and a held-period amplitude, which are not the same
quantity because a band spans less than one full comb period and its dispersion is therefore phase-dependent
by construction}---the two differing by about a third in size and neither chosen. \emph{So the part that
fails to cancel is not an artefact of forming two large numbers and differencing them}---\emph{and the same
construction shows which step of the two it belongs to}: dividing instead by a smoothed control removes the
differencing while keeping the division, and the departure turns positive, back toward each arm's own.

\emph{And the width of a difference is fed by a relative phase as well as a relative amplitude, so which of
the two it is has to be settled rather than assumed---and it is settled by an identity.} For two oscillations
of the same period the width of their difference is a closed function of the amplitude ratio and the phase
offset alone, and that function reproduces the measured difference pointwise, so the split into an
amplitude-only and a phase-only part is exact rather than fitted. \emph{Split that way, the step is the
amplitude's}: the amplitude-only term carries almost all of the statistic at the lowest band and steps with
the same sign, while the phase-only term steps the \emph{other} way. \emph{So the first acoustic cycle is
weakened in this cosmology rather than displaced in it.} \emph{What the split does not settle is the phase
channel's size}: the relative phase is a fraction of a degree, an order of magnitude below the relative
amplitude, and its step reverses sign as the reading window widens where the amplitude's does not---\emph{so
the phase term sits at this estimator's own resolution and the honest statement is the sign without the
share}. \emph{Read against the comb over several periods rather than locally, the relative phase is
determined to a few tenths of a degree, at seven times its stretch-to-stretch standard error}---\emph{and the lowest band
still cannot be reached that way}, being narrower than the period the method needs, so a projection across it
leaks the baseline and is both noisier and biased. \emph{Against a control of stretches matched to its own
width the lowest band's phase sits inside one and a half scatters, where against the wide-window error bar it
would have read five}---\emph{so the phase step is not resolved and the amplitude reading is the complete
account rather than a verdict on the sign alone}. \emph{That the lowest band is narrower than every period
this comparison measures with is now the same limit met in three places}, on the window-free family, on the
amplitude-and-phase split, and
here\rcpt{P15_no_statistic_this_construction_can_build_resolves_the_two_channels_and_the_phase_step_dies_on_a_matched_width_control}\rcpt{P15_the_surviving_step_is_amplitude_and_not_phase_and_the_candidate_shares_do_not_survive_the_change_of_denominator}.

\emph{The shared part, for its own sake, does not reduce to one term of the source.} Removing each bilinear
term of the decomposition in turn and asking whether the step leaves with it, the fractional losses sum to
nearly twice the whole---\emph{so the step is a property of the sum and no term carries it}. \emph{What the
same table does show is that leverage is not flat}: weighted by how much of the step each term accounts for
against how much of the oscillation it holds, the two leading terms are both Doppler---one at more than five
times its weight and one at nearly four---where the monopole holds seven eighths of the oscillation and
carries the step at less than its weight. \emph{The ordering is not simply Doppler against the rest}, one
Doppler cross term telling against it and the integrated Sachs--Wolfe autocorrelation lying lower still.
\emph{So a step-weighted reading of the source points at a sector where an amplitude-weighted one points at
the monopole}---a ranking and not an
attribution\rcpt{P15_the_step_survives_a_differential_estimator_and_no_single_bilinear_term_carries_the_shared_step_though_the_doppler_leads_it}.

\emph{One whole class is excluded by structure rather than by measurement, and needs no run at all.} Every
quantity that belongs to the two clocks---the leaf clock, the ruler, the Jacobian between them, the
visibility and the optical depth---is a function of conformal time alone, and \emph{a function of conformal
time has the same value at every wavenumber}. So it cannot vary across one. \emph{That is neither
measured-and-failed nor unmeasured}: it is a statement that the class cannot carry the effect, from the shape
of the objects in it.

\emph{And the projection itself holds a width nothing in this construction has ever imposed, which is where
the row's own name has pointed since it opened.} Two widths govern the last-scattering window and they are
not the same width: \emph{the acoustic phase swept across it} goes with the window's width measured in the
leaf sound horizon, while \emph{the projection's own smearing} goes with its width measured in conformal
time, that being what the projection's argument carries. \emph{In a one-rate cosmology those are one object
up to the sound speed; on two rates they are not}, because the clocks differ. \emph{And the difference between them is one object and not a residue}: the two sound horizons on this
arm are related pointwise by the Jacobian between the two rates, checked across the window to a part in a
million, and identically unity on any single-rate cosmology. \emph{Measured against the ruler clock the two
arms are the same instrument}---their windows agree in width to a few parts in a thousand on either
definition, the visibility being laid down by scattering on the physical background, which is the same
physics on both. \emph{Measured against the leaf clock the arm's window is some fourteen per cent wider, and
the whole of that is the Jacobian}, the arm's own leaf-to-ruler width ratio reproducing the Jacobian's mean
to a fraction of a per cent. \emph{Which quantity a width is has to be said before the number means
anything}: a root-mean-square width returns half as much, because the window has long tails that it weights
and that neither the projection nor the phase sweep responds to, while the full width at half maximum and the
characteristic function's own half-fall agree with each other to better than a hundredth of a per cent.

\emph{So the different ratio is a prediction of the two-rate assignment rather than a property of this
instrument}---\emph{the Jacobian carries no free coefficient and is fixed by the background solution once the
arm is specified}---and the ruler clock is what establishes it, the two arms agreeing there being what leaves
the leaf-clock difference nowhere else to come from. \emph{And it cannot be imposed as a knob, which follows
from that same identity rather than from the instrument's limitations}: giving the control this arm's
conformal width at the same leaf width is giving it this arm's Jacobian, and a control carrying the Jacobian
is not a control.

\emph{Applied in the projection's own kernel instead---holding the source's phase and stretching only the
projection's argument---it is a real channel whose size the statistic does not fix and it is not the carrier.}
\emph{Its share depends on the anchoring by an order of magnitude}: in the band where the excess is largest it
delivers about a quarter of what the excess needs on the mean anchoring and about a fiftieth on the peak
anchoring, so the quarter is one of the two readings and not the channel's size. \emph{What both readings agree
on is the sign of its wavenumber dependence}, and it is the wrong one---\emph{it accelerates across wavenumber
where the excess decelerates}, under either anchoring. \emph{So it is excluded as the carrier on its shape
rather than on its size, and the size is left as the range the two anchorings give.} \emph{And the width ratio is a second reading of the Jacobian rather than a restatement of the
first}: the comb reads its \emph{cumulative} value at last scattering, which is what sets the peak spacing
this paper already matches, while the widths read its \emph{window-local average}---and the two differ by
about a third. \emph{So a construction tuned to the comb must still return the right local Jacobian to match
the widths}, which is a second and independent demand on the same object, with neither carrying a free
coefficient and both identically unity on a single rate. \emph{What it is not is separately observable}: the
width the damping reads differs between the arms by under a per cent where the width the projection reads
differs by more than ten, and the projection's own contrast effect is bounded above at about two per cent with
its sign undetermined\rcpt{P15_the_two_projection_widths_differ_by_the_jacobian_alone_and_the_channel_that_opens_is_a_quarter_of_the_excess}.

\emph{One thing about the statistic belongs with those numbers, because without it none of them can be
read.} The anchored contrast is measured against a running-mean envelope that is recomputed for each
spectrum, so a purely symmetric change of amplitude does not read as unity on it: applied to the control's
own spectrum, amplitude and envelope operations both read near two, a loading below one and a smearing
negative, and the near-two is stable across a fivefold range of sizes. \emph{So the baseline against which a
class is identified is that value and not one}, and read against it the anchored statistic and the
band-variance split of the excess agree to about a tenth---\emph{the same decomposition reached twice
rather than two readings in tension}. \emph{And the baryon density cannot supply the contrast at any value}: its
response to $\omega_b$ saturates, spanning $0.028$ over the whole range with increments falling
monotonically, so four per cent is beyond its reach rather than away from its fitted
point\rcpt{P15_the_monopole_zero_point_is_not_the_channel_and_the_two_residuals_point_opposite_ways}.

\emph{And what kind of statement this statistic makes is worth saying once, because every number above inherits
it.} The amplitude it fits to the data cancels exactly against the envelope it divides by, \emph{so as defined it
compares two computed spectra and reads no data at all}: its floor is numerical, at some six parts in a thousand,
and every spread quoted on it above is a spread of computed quantities rather than a noise level. \emph{Its sky
counterpart is a different instrument and carries a real floor}, built by running the same statistic on noisy
realisations of the control against the control through the likelihood's own covariance: across the whole range
that floor is $0.018$, against which this arm's $+0.047$ stands at about two and a half floors. \emph{Read one
band at a time it is coarser than the excess it would measure}---between $0.043$ and $0.086$ per band, where the
excess's own per-band values run from $+0.022$ to $+0.076$, \emph{so band by band the contrast is a statement
about theories and would not be an observation}, the lowest band's value standing at under half of that band's
floor. \emph{Which is why the rejection this section closes on is read in the likelihood and not here}: the
whole-range contrast is what this instrument supports, and the band-by-band structure is a property of the
computed spectra that the sky's own noise would not
resolve\rcpt{P15_the_three_instruments_with_no_noise_model_one_can_be_given_a_floor_and_ten_passages_quote_a_significance_or_share_the_instrument_cannot_bear}.

\emph{Those four channels all act on the source, and the contrast difference is not in the source.} The
same statistic that reads $1.047$ on the reported spectrum reads $0.996$ on the source at last scattering
and $0.992$ on the $\eta$-integrated source, over four envelope windows, both envelope definitions, three
term subsets and with or without the $k$-measure---the source rungs spanning $0.971$ to $1.005$ and the
multipole rung $1.042$ to $1.052$, against a floor of $0.6$ per cent set by the statistic's own bias on a
known injected contrast. \emph{So the difference is made between wavenumber and multipole}, and in
wavenumber this arm's relative oscillation is if anything the shallower of the two---which is the direction
all four channels point, and is why none of them has anything to overcome. \emph{The stage is located here
and the mechanism is named below}: within the projection, this arm retains $1.054$ times as much of its own source
oscillation as the control does, the excess rises with multipole where the source ratio is flat, and it is
neither the visibility width acting before the kernel, nor the lensing, nor the binning, nor the two arms'
different samplings of wavenumber---replacing this arm's physical ladder with the control's uniform grid at
the same mode count leaves the multipole rung unchanged. \emph{Nor is it the distance}: the two arms'
comoving distances to last scattering differ by $0.449$ per cent with this arm's the larger, their acoustic
angles agreeing to $0.085$ per cent, and projecting either arm's own source through the other's distance
moves its contrast by under a per cent---in the direction that makes the difference larger rather than
smaller\rcpt{P15_the_acoustic_contrast_is_not_in_the_source_and_the_projection_makes_it_without_the_distance}.

\emph{Nor is it one term of the source, nor the interference between two.} Decomposing the projection
bilinearly---one transfer per source term, so that $C_\ell=\sum_{a\le b}w_{ab}\sum_k P\,\Delta^a\Delta^b$
closes on $D_\ell$ to one part in $10^{15}$---\emph{deleting the monopole--Doppler cross term moves the
contrast ratio from $1.0452$ to $1.0451$}, its carry running $-0.0013$ to $+0.0008$ across four envelope
windows. \emph{Each piece separately already carries the excess instead}: the monopole at $1.034$, the
Doppler at $1.099$ and the polarisation at $1.058$, against the full spectrum's $1.045$, while every term's
source ratio is at or below $0.992$---\emph{so the projection raises this arm's contrast on nearly
everything it projects, and no single term is the channel.} Of the difference, about two fifths is this arm
weighting the terms differently and the rest is each term's own response, two reweightings run in opposite
directions agreeing to $0.001$. \emph{And the fraction of the source's oscillation the projection retains
rises with wavenumber}: its arm-to-control ratio goes from $1.030$ in the lowest acoustic band to $1.089$ in
the highest, with a slope of $+0.0139$ per acoustic period, the twelve envelope settings spanning $0.0021$
about it and none of them returning a negative value. \emph{The intercept of that rise covers one across the
same twelve settings}, $1.017$ with a spread of $0.010$, so what is
established is the growth with wavenumber and not a constant offset: \emph{whatever the projection does
differently here, it does nothing at the longest acoustic wavelengths and more of it at each shorter
one}\rcpt{P15_the_cross_term_is_not_the_channel_and_every_projected_term_carries_the_excess}.
\emph{That slope's size is not all retention, and the part that is not is measured rather than allowed for.}
Fed one comb on both arms---no difference to retain---the statistic still returns a rise at the real comb's
phase, $36$ to $39$ per cent of the reported slope and in its direction, \emph{because the two arms' own source
combs sit at periods $1.0170$ and $1.0160$ and so drift apart by between $0.013$ and $0.042$ of a radian across
the range, which a band root-mean-square taken over seven tenths of a period reads as a difference in
retention}. \emph{Replacing the two combs by one removes it}, the null slope falling to a sixth or less, while
flattening the envelope leaves most of it---\emph{so the carrier is the source's phase and not the envelope}.
\emph{The rise survives and its size does not}: about two thirds of the quoted slope is retention and the rest
is that drift. \emph{The step at the lowest band is the sounder of the two readings}, its false value at the
real phase running a quarter to two fifths of the measured step. \emph{And the statistic's phase systematic is
what limits both}, carrying three to three and a half independent modes across the seven bands where its noise
response carries nearly seven---\emph{the slope's own effective count being between five and nine}, so a
wavenumber slope read across these bands rests on fewer independent points than its regression error
assumes\rcpt{P15_the_phase_systematic_leaves_the_step_standing_and_carries_a_third_of_the_retention_slope_through_a_phase_drift_between_the_sources}.

\emph{And it is not in the source at all.} Replacing the source with a pure oscillation on the
visibility---no transfer, no terms, no weights, carrying $k^{(1-n_s)/2}$ so that the smooth part of the
projected integrand is the same on both arms---and projecting it through each arm's own kernel,
visibility and wavenumber grid gives an arm-to-control retained ratio of $1.066$ at a slope of $+0.023$
per acoustic period, \emph{exceeding} what the real source gives; the injection's phase does not change
it. \emph{So the projection's geometry accounts for the whole of the difference and the source is
irrelevant to it.} And the two factors left in that integral are not separable: the comoving distance
the kernel reads is $\eta_0-\eta$ on both arms, so there is nothing to exchange there, while the
visibility's width and the clock the acoustic phase accumulates on close the gap only together---forcing
both arms onto one clock moves the comb rather than isolating a factor, and exchanging the widths
amplifies the wavenumber dependence eightfold instead of removing it. \emph{What they close on is one
ratio.} Across the visibility's full width at half maximum the \emph{sound horizon} this plasma
accumulates agrees between the arms to $0.08$ per cent, $17.307$ against $17.294$~Mpc, while the
\emph{comoving distance} across that same window differs by $14.6$ per cent, $38.04$ against
$43.59$---because the scales accumulate on the leaf rate while conformal time, and so that distance, is
the stacking rate's. \emph{The sound speed the kernel sees is therefore $0.455$ on the control and
$0.397$ here}, and a difference in that ratio is term-independent by construction, grows with
wavenumber, and vanishes for a window narrower than one acoustic period---which is the signature
above\rcpt{P15_a_source_with_no_physics_reproduces_the_contrast_and_the_two_clocks_part_company_at_the_visibility}\rcpt{P15_the_projection_read_recombination_at_the_wrong_conformal_time_and_one_clock_moves_it_72_per_cent}.
\emph{That places the residual on the rate assignment of \S\ref{sec:tensions} rather than on the
projection}: what is open is whether that assignment is right, not whether the transfer implements it.

\emph{And the retention is not merely located at that acceptance; it is a law, and there is nothing in the law
to fit.} For a \emph{standing} oscillation the line-of-sight integral factorises with no approximation at all,
$\Delta_{\ell}(k)=\cos(k\rs^{*})\,k^{(1-n_s)/2}G_{\ell}(k)$ with $G_{\ell}(k)=\int g\,j_{\ell}(k\chi)\,\dd\eta$,
and the factorisation holds to a relative $3\times10^{-15}$ on multipoles spread across the reported
range---\emph{so the comb reaches the transfer undamped, and the only thing left that can damp it is the sum
over $k$ at fixed $\ell$}. That sum's weight is $W_{\ell}(k)=P(k)\,k^{1-n_s}G_{\ell}(k)^{2}=G_{\ell}(k)^{2}\,\dd
k/k$, the tilt cancelling identically, and since
$C_{\ell}=\int W_{\ell}\cos^{2}(k\rs^{*})\,\dd k=\tfrac12\int W_{\ell}\,\dd k+\tfrac12\,\mathrm{Re}\int
W_{\ell}e^{2ik\rs^{*}}\,\dd k$, the comb's fractional amplitude in $C_{\ell}$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:acceptance-law}
A_{\ell}=\frac{\bigl|\int W_{\ell}(k)\,e^{2ik\rs^{*}}\,\dd k\bigr|}{\int W_{\ell}(k)\,\dd k},
\end{equation}
built from the window the kernel reads and the comb's period, both of them the background's own. \emph{There is
no coefficient in it, so it predicts an \emph{absolute} amplitude and one measurement can kill
it}---and against the amplitude measured band by band it holds to within ten per cent everywhere, worst
deviation $6.7$ per cent. \emph{What that comparison is worth turns on the measuring statistic's own error,
which is not a single number}: fed a comb of known amplitude the statistic is accurate to $3.74$ per cent at
one injection phase and to ten or eleven per cent at the worst phase, \emph{so the smaller figure is one
phase's error and not a floor}. \emph{But the phase is measured rather than assumed, and the real comb sits at
that phase to $0.03$ of a radian}, where the statistic is wrong by about four per cent in the lowest band and
by under one per cent in the other six. \emph{Divided out, the law holds at every band on both arms to better
than four per cent}---worst $3.4$ and $3.6$---\emph{and the lowest band's apparent six-and-a-half per cent
departure is around two thirds the statistic's own}. \emph{And the two arms are one test of it rather than
two}: their deviation patterns correlate at $0.998$, as do the statistic's errors at the two arms' phases.
\emph{Within an arm the seven bands are close to independent under the instrument's noise response}, $6.9$
effective of seven across four hundred draws of the whole instrument, \emph{while the instrument's phase
systematic carries only some two and a half independent modes across the same seven}---so the comparison is
seven measurements against noise and nearer two against that systematic. \emph{The acceptance the law integrates over is the narrower on this
arm}, spanning $4.47$ of acoustic phase against the control's $5.14$---$13.03$ per cent narrower, \emph{and
by that same amount at every one of twelve numerical settings, the spread across them being five thousandths of
a percentage point}, where the sound-speed ratio above independently gives $12.8$: \emph{two routes to one
quantity, and the agreement is built in nowhere}. \emph{Read physically the law says one thing}: a wider $\chi$-extent narrows the kernel's
acceptance in wavenumber, so less of an acoustic period is averaged over and more of the comb survives; this
arm's visibility is the $14.6$ per cent wider in $\chi$ while accumulating the same sound horizon to $0.08$ per
cent, and that is why it retains
\emph{more}\rcpt{P15_the_retention_is_the_combs_average_over_the_kernels_own_k_acceptance_and_the_law_has_no_fitted_coefficient}\rcpt{P15_the_acceptance_does_not_move_under_refinement_and_three_of_the_twelve_axes_could_not_have_moved_it}\rcpt{P15_the_laws_six_of_seven_is_five_at_the_quoted_floor_which_is_one_phases_and_with_the_instruments_own_error_divided_out_all_seven_hold}.
\emph{Three things the law does not do, and they are stated because it would otherwise be read as doing them.}
\emph{The statistic the comparison is scored on does not move under refinement, and that statistic is the
band-RMS ratio rather than the acceptance or the retention}: swept to full coverage over every numerical axis---the
wavenumber ceiling and the mode count, the reported multipole grid, the conformal-time resolution, and the
visibility's half-width and its split---the ratio sits at $1.0587$ on the control and between $1.0659$ and
$1.0660$ on this arm at every refinement of every axis, \emph{the worst last step over all twelve axis readings
being $0.008$ per cent against a floor of $0.6$ per cent pre-registered before the sweep ran}, with both base
points reproducing their banked values exactly. \emph{What that buys is stability and not convergence, and the two
are not the same purchase}: by the rule fixed in advance a sequence that has not turned over is not converged
whatever its last step, and monotonicity is undefined on two points---so \emph{exactly one reading of the twelve
is reportable as converged}, seven having not turned over and four carrying two points only. \emph{The axis count
is read from the run log rather than from the design}: five axes moved the ratio on this arm and six on the
control, not twelve, because this arm's mode count is inert by construction---its wavenumber ladder never reaching
the decimation cap, so those six configurations never ran at all---while the same setting moves the control
outright. \emph{And the acceptance's own inert set is a different set, which is why the two are counted
separately}: three of the twelve pairs cannot move $A_{\ell}$, the reported multipole grid because the acceptance
is read at the same multipoles by construction and this arm's mode count for the reason just given, the two
refinements writing a wavenumber axis, measure, conformal time, kernel argument and visibility identical to the
base run to the byte\rcpt{P15_the_band_rms_ratio_does_not_move_with_any_numerical_setting}. \emph{What none of
that settles is the sum the spectrum is made of}: the retention and the peak heights are measured on spectra
rather than on the window and are a different quantity from the ratio swept here, and \S\ref{sec:scope} keeps that
as its own open reading---\emph{a stable statistic beside an unconverged one would itself be a finding, which is
why the two are not reported together}. The oscillation it is checked against is a
known input transferred through the projection, so that the law over-delivers is sufficiency and not identity
with the real source. And naming $A_{\ell}$ does not say why the construction puts its scales and its distances
on different rates, which is where the residual sits and is untouched by anything here.

\emph{What the rule does reach, and it reaches the projection directly, is the distance the kernel
argument is built from.} The assignment is not a modelling choice on either side: a comoving separation
read across leaves---$D_M$, $D_H$, $D_V$, the observable expansion---takes the stacking rate, while a
process running \emph{in} the content---the plasma's sound horizon, its diffusion length, recombination,
the perturbations---takes the leaf's, and \emph{there is no locus at which the rate switches}. \emph{So
the two rates do not compete for the line-of-sight integral; they divide it.} The integral
$\Delta_\ell(k)=\int S\,j_\ell(k(\eta_0-\eta))\,\mathrm d\eta$ runs over one variable, and that variable
is the stacking conformal time because $\chi=\eta_0-\eta$ is a separation read across leaves; the
plasma's quantities then enter it as functions of that variable, carried through the Jacobian
$\mathrm d\eta_{\rm leaf}/\mathrm d\eta_{\rm stack}$, which on this arm runs $0.789$ to $0.913$ across
$\pm3$ half-widths of the visibility and is identically one on the
control\rcpt{P15_a_source_with_no_physics_reproduces_the_contrast_and_the_two_clocks_part_company_at_the_visibility}\rcpt{P15_the_fitted_onset_is_the_wrong_clocks_artefact_because_the_stacking_ruler_keeps_a_square_root_memory_of_the_start_and_the_leaf_ruler_keeps_none}.
\emph{Two rates inside one
integral is what the rule requires. One rate throughout is what it forbids}, since the single rate
would have to be the leaf's for the plasma's sake and would then carry $D_M$ on it---\emph{reading a
stacking quantity on the leaf's rate, which is the reification the construction's own proposition
rules out.}

\emph{And the division is carried out rather than asserted, which is checkable and is checked.} The
sound horizon the oscillator's phase is reckoned in accumulates on the leaf rate, and the integral runs
over the stacking variable, so the two statements of one quantity---$\int c_s\,\mathrm d\eta_{\rm
leaf}$ and $\int c_s\,(\mathrm d\eta_{\rm leaf}/\mathrm d\eta_{\rm stack})\,\mathrm d\eta_{\rm
stack}$---must agree identically. \emph{Across the plasma's whole run on this arm they agree to a part
in $10^{8}$}, while the same integrand carried on the stacking rate throughout stands $75$ per cent
away. \emph{So the Jacobian is doing the work the rule assigns it}, and the phase the oscillator turns
over on is the leaf's even though the variable it is expressed in is not.

\emph{And the size of what the division decides is worth stating, because the distance is a poor
witness to it.} One event---recombination at a fixed redshift---carries two conformal times, $485.5$~Mpc
on the stacking rate and $282.3$ on the leaf, and \emph{both readings are correct}: the first is what
$\chi$ and $D_M$ are built from and the second is what the plasma's accumulations run on. \emph{What
does not distinguish them is $D_M$}: built either way it moves only from $14017$ to $13947$~Mpc, half a
per cent, because the present epoch moves with the recombination epoch. \emph{So the distance cannot
be read as a check on the assignment}---the two clocks differ by seventy per cent in a quantity the
projection distance records at
half-a-per-cent\rcpt{P15_the_projection_read_recombination_at_the_wrong_conformal_time_and_one_clock_moves_it_72_per_cent}\rcpt{P15_the_phase_is_already_carried_into_the_projections_variable_and_the_two_statements_of_the_sound_horizon_agree_identically}.

\emph{The same division settles where the plasma's evolution starts, and settles it as a consequence
rather than a choice.} A sound horizon's memory of its own lower endpoint scales as a power of the
scale factor there, and \emph{the power is the radiation term}---the one thing by which the two rates
differ. On the stacking rate, with no radiation era, the integrand runs as $a^{-1/2}$ and the memory is
a square root: measured at $0.5008$, the integral is still $1.1$ per cent short at $z=10^{7}$ and
$43.1$ per cent short at $z\simeq6.8\times10^{3}$. On the leaf rate the integrand is flat in $a$ and
the memory is linear, measured at $0.9983$, leaving the accumulation $0.0064$ per cent short at
$z=3\times10^{7}$. \emph{So a sound horizon carried on the leaf rate has no lower endpoint to place and
the acoustic angle is an output, exactly as the handover requires}; the same quantity carried on the
stacking rate would need its start supplied by hand, and the control, whose single rate obeys the leaf
law at $0.9985$, is why integrating from $a\simeq0$ was never in question
there\rcpt{P15_the_fitted_onset_is_the_wrong_clocks_artefact_because_the_stacking_ruler_keeps_a_square_root_memory_of_the_start_and_the_leaf_ruler_keeps_none}.
\emph{And the start is super-horizon on both rates alike}---the comoving horizon vanishes at the
handover whichever rate is read, so the condition the coherence argument needs does not turn on this
division at all.

\emph{With every assignment settled, the configuration the construction specifies can be run against
the one it rules out, and the two are measured side by side on the same grid with the same steps.} The
test was fixed before either was read: a $\chi^{2}$ that improves while the residual still swings is not
the fix, and a residual that stops swinging is, even if $\chi^{2}$ moves little---a settled residual
having \emph{more} sign changes, \emph{shorter} runs of one sign and \emph{smaller} excursions.
\emph{The configuration the rate rule specifies moves neither statistic.} The contrast coefficient the
declared parameters cannot supply moves by $0.08$ of its own error, its magnitude \emph{rising} one per
cent to $-0.0642$; the longest run of one sign goes from $18$ to $22$ bins, the wrong way; $\chi^{2}$
per bin goes from $3.05$ to $3.20$. \emph{Carrying the projection's distance on the leaf rate
instead---which is what the rule forbids---moves the same coefficient by $6.7$ of that error to
$-0.0069\pm0.0083$}, \emph{its magnitude falling $89$ per cent onto the value the control itself
returns}, while the sign changes rise from $30$ to $44$, the longest run falls from $18$ to $12$ and
$\chi^{2}$ per bin falls to $1.47$. \emph{And the acoustic scale follows it}: $\ell_A$ moves from
$302.889$ to $301.380$ against the control's $301.375$. \emph{The control's own coefficient is identical
across all three grids}, which is what says the move belongs to this arm and not to the
method\rcpt{P15_the_licensed_configuration_moves_neither_statistic_and_the_swing_was_read_above_where_it_converges}.

\emph{What that comparison can and cannot carry is fixed by where the discriminating feature lives, and
it is measured rather than assumed.} Across four reading ceilings the three configurations are
\emph{indistinguishable below $\ell\simeq800$}---every one of them with a longest run of eight or nine
bins---and separate only above it, where the specified configuration's run grows $9$, $9$, $13$, $22$
and the forbidden one's stays $9$, $9$, $9$, $12$. \emph{So the statistic decides nothing when read low, and what it decides when read high is not an
artefact of where the grids stop, which is measured rather than conceded.} The projection's reach is a
fixed multiple of the inverse distance on every arm---$k_{\max}D_M=3998$ against $\ell_{\max}=2000$ on
each---so a shorter distance does not carry an arm nearer its ceiling and the coupling that would
manufacture the separation cancels by construction. \emph{More than half the separation is already
present eight hundred multipoles below the ceiling}: re-fitting all three statistics to data truncated at
a succession of ceilings, the specified configuration's excess over the forbidden one runs $-1.0$,
$+14.4$, $+51.0$, $+67.9$, $+74.7$, $+84.0$ and $+93.8$ as the truncation moves from $\ell=850$ to
$1996$, with the bulk of it, about half, falling between $\ell\simeq850$ and $1500$; and \emph{at every
one of those truncations the forbidden configuration sits within about one unit of the control}, which a
flatness produced by distance from a ceiling would not do. \emph{What the comparison therefore carries is
a rigidity across scales rather than a feature confined to high multipole}: its onset falls between
$\ell\simeq850$ and $1000$, it is already decisive by $\ell\simeq1200$, and it is \emph{not} converged---it
grows to the last bin---so the quoted separation is a statement about the range read and not a saturated
value. \emph{Below the onset the parameters absorb everything}, even though a third of the separation on
the full-range fit sits there, which is what makes the failure a cross-scale one: no single parameter set
fits the low and the high multipoles together\rcpt{P15_the_three_grid_comparison_is_like_for_like_and_not_a_ceiling_artefact_because_the_projection_reach_is_fixed_on_every_arm}.

\emph{Taken together these fix the open end of this section precisely, and it is narrower than the
section has previously been able to state.} \emph{The implementation now does what the construction says
on every object}: the plasma's sound horizon, its diffusion length, its ionisation history and the
perturbations on the leaf rate, the optical depth and the projection's distance on the stacking rate,
and no free time origin anywhere. \emph{What the sky prefers differs from that in exactly one
assignment}---the rate the projection's own comoving distance is built on---\emph{and it prefers it by
a large margin on three statistics that do not share a definition}. \emph{Rebuilt consistently on the
assignments the construction makes, the fit's unreachable $\chi^{2}$ moves from $278.795$ to $278.788$---
$0.007$ per cent of a gap of $92.8$ against the control's $186.007$---and the residual's sign changes and
longest run of one sign do not move at all, on a rebuild that moved the spectrum itself by six and a half
per cent.} \emph{Built instead with the projection's distance on the leaf rate it closes the whole of it}:
$\chi^{2}=184.989$, below the control's own value; $91$ sign changes against the control's $87$; a longest
run of $8$, which is the control's exactly\rcpt{P15_the_licensed_rebuild_leaves_all_three_rigidity_numbers_where_they_were_and_the_forbidden_one_puts_the_arm_on_the_controls_own_floor}\rcpt{P15_the_controls_base_log_is_banked_beside_the_grids_and_the_peak_heights_concur_with_the_three_rigidity_statistics}.

\emph{And what that second configuration is, said plainly, is what makes the comparison a statement about
this cosmology rather than about its implementation.} The leaf rate and the stacking rate differ by the
radiation term alone, and \emph{the control's single rate carries that term}---so building this arm's
distance on the leaf rate gives it a rate of the control's own form, radiation present, differing from the
control's only in the parameters the distance data fix. \emph{The arm keeps everything else that makes it
this cosmology's}: its own handover datum, its own discrete wavenumber ladder, its own sound horizon. \emph{So
the configuration the acoustic contrast prefers is the one in which radiation is restored to the rate that
builds the distance}---which is the single feature this construction removes, and removes not as a choice
but because the vacuum kernel fixes the stacking rate from $\Lambda$ and the cut's offset alone with
radiation a bend of the cut and no part of it (\S\ref{sec:background}).

\emph{Stated as the two-sided thing it is}: that same absence is what the distance ladder confirms at
$\chi^{2}/\text{dof}\simeq1$ and what reproduces the acoustic \emph{scale}, $\pi D_C/r_s$ coming to
$302.87$ from the background alone against the $302.889$ the grid measures. \emph{What disagrees is the
acoustic \emph{contrast}, and it disagrees in the one direction the construction cannot move.} \emph{So
this section leaves neither a residual in search of a parameter nor an arithmetic to re-run}: it leaves a
localised, quantified disagreement between this cosmology and the microwave sky, with one bounded
calculation outstanding that could still bear on it (\S\ref{sec:scope}).

\emph{And the rule does reach the object that difference lives in, by the one property that
distinguishes the two sides of it.} The visibility $g=\tau'e^{-\tau}$ looks ambiguous because the optical
depth counts the plasma's own scatterings while being differentiated per unit conformal time. \emph{The
rate it carries is settled by asking what it is an integral \emph{over}, not what it is an integral
\emph{of}.} Thomson scattering has a single rate per unit proper time, so $\tau'=\Gamma a/c$ is the same
expression on either reading and the whole question is the accumulation; and by the definition of a
conformal time, $a\,\mathrm d\eta=c\,\mathrm dt$, so the proper length a photon covers between two
leaves is $a\,\mathrm d\chi=c\,\mathrm dt_{\mathrm{stack}}$. \emph{Weighting $\mathrm d\tau$ by the
leaf-to-stacking Jacobian would therefore count the scatterings of a photon crossing that same proper
length in less time}---at $1.130c$ at recombination and $1.175c$ by $z=1500$---\emph{and a measure that
moves the photon off the null cone of the path it is integrated along is not a second admissible
reading}. \emph{So the optical depth takes the stacking rate, as a photon-path observable alongside the
redshift and the distances, and the content enters it through $n_e$ rather than through the measure.}
\emph{The apparent counter-case is distinguished by its integrand rather than overruled}: the diffusion
length's $1/k_D^2$ carries the baryon loading and the polarisation coefficient, so it is the
oscillator's own viscous damping and shares the oscillator's clock, while $\tau$ carries nothing but
$n_e\sigma_T a$ and shares the kernel's. \emph{Each integral takes the clock of the evolution it runs
over, and the Thomson rate appears in both while setting the measure in
neither}\rcpt{P15_the_optical_depth_is_a_photon_path_observable_so_it_takes_the_stacking_rate_and_the_visibility_ruling_is_withdrawn}.
\emph{What is left in the visibility's neighbourhood is the ionisation history alone}, which is the
content's own chemistry over its own duration and so the leaf's: carried there it moves last scattering
by $0.59$ per cent in redshift and its width by a tenth of a per cent.

\emph{The family that assignment sits in is measured anyway, because what the sky can see of it is worth
knowing even once the derivation has settled it.} \emph{The peak positions are the only one of the three
readings with an external referent}---the retained oscillation's level and its wavenumber slope have
none---so they are the only one against which the span can be scored at all, and the measurement below is
of what that referent resolves rather than of what it decides. Letting the weighting on the optical depth run
continuously from one clock to the other through the one-parameter family $1+f(\mathrm{d}\eta_{\rm
leaf}/\mathrm{d}\eta_{\rm stack}-1)$, every reading is linear in $f$: the first peak runs
$\ell_1=221.93+4.21f$ to three hundredths of a multipole. \emph{So the whole span between the two
admissible assignments is four multipoles}---$0.01395$ in $\ell_1/\ell_A$, which is $4.2$ times a
one-multipole locating width and $2.1$ times a two-multipole one---and the peak positions
therefore pin the assignment to about a quarter of that family and no further. \emph{Those two widths are
assumed rather than measured here}: the only locating uncertainty this comparison propagates through the
sky's own covariance is the fourth peak's, at $2.19$ multipoles, \emph{so whether the first three locate to
one multipole is not settled by anything above and the span is quoted against a stated assumption}. The retained oscillation's
\emph{level} is the shallower reading, at $1.75$ of its own band spread across the family, and its wavenumber
\emph{slope} reaches $3.80$ of its own regression error over that family. \emph{Those two are not the peak
positions' statistical equals and are not quoted as such}: the comb's width is a locating width assumed of
the sky where both of the others are spreads taken across a family of computed curves, \emph{so what the comparison
supports is which reading moves and which does not}---the contrast's spread grows about fivefold across the
family while the comb's locating width does not move at all. \emph{And the decisive result is not about the choice between the
clocks.} The sky's $0.7312$ lies at $f=-0.304$ on that same straight line---outside the family, on the
far side of the clock the transfer already uses---so no intermediate weighting fits the peak positions
better than the endpoint in use, there is no fitted clock to declare, and \emph{the residual first-peak
disagreement cannot be absorbed by the clock assignment at all, because the direction it would need is
not admissible}. The height ratio $P_1/P_2$ agrees independently, moving $2.141\to2.017$ away from the
sky's $2.217$. \emph{And the motion the alternative produces is separable into its causes}, the
projection of an analytic oscillating source with no plasma dynamics in it isolating them: about a third
is the visibility peak relocating, so that the sound horizon at last scattering is read at a slightly
different time, a tenth is the visibility's re-weighting of the projection kernel, and three fifths is
the plasma's own acoustic phase responding to the re-weighted optical
depth\rcpt{P15_the_combs_resolution_is_four_multipoles_and_the_skys_own_value_lies_outside_the_family}.

\emph{The comparison between the arms is taken as a distance between
the two models rather than as a difference of two numbers each read against the sky}, which is what makes it
independent of the configuration's own floor\rcpt{P15_the_floor_is_a_distance_between_models_not_a_number_from_the_data}.

\emph{The instrument's own accuracy is measured rather than assumed.} On the full range its control sits at
$1.16$ per bin lensed, within about a seventh of what a standard code returns on the same bins, so the ratio
above is a statement about the arm and not about the machinery. \emph{What the comb does not carry is the
disagreement}: at $298.0$ against $298.4$ the spacing is right to a part in six hundred, on both instrument
paths, so what remains is about one multipole at each peak.

\emph{The result is reproduced independently on a second instrument.} A separate implementation---the
hierarchy path rather than the line-of-sight one, $185$ covered bins rather than $133$, its own wavenumber
grid---returns peaks at $221.96$, $536.11$, $815.40$ and $1130.53$, located sub-bin on a grid refined to unit
step and recovered there to a few hundredths of a multipole, against this path's $222$, $538$, $818$ and
$1134$; height ratios $2.273$ and $2.319$ against $2.264$ and $2.298$; and $4.02$ per bin in the band from
$\ell=700$ to $1000$ against $4.23$: \emph{within a multipole on every peak and within one per cent on the
heights}. \emph{The fourth peak's excess reproduces there too}, so it is a property of the
spectrum rather than of either implementation---and on that path, where the sub-bin location is available on
both arms, it is a property the control carries with
it\rcpt{P15_the_crossing_spectrum_reproduces_on_a_second_instrument_and_the_172_is_the_radiation_free_ruler}\rcpt{P15_the_locator_is_good_to_three_hundredths_and_the_fourth_peaks_residual_is_the_controls_too}.

\emph{The heights are the polarisation path's and the positions are not.} Both paths return the same comb to
four figures; the height ratios differ between them by more than either differs from the sky --- $2.264$ and
$2.298$ on the polarisation path against $2.448$ and $2.934$ on the fluid one --- so every height quoted here
is the polarisation path's and is stated as such, while the position, the phase and the alternation are the
construction's.

\emph{Where the plasma starts moves the scale and not the peak, which is why the start is not free.}
Scanning a hand-placed start over a factor of $3.6$ runs $\ell_{A}$ from $360.6$ down to $239.3$ while the
first peak sits between $204$ and $208$ throughout\rcpt{P15_the_one_fitted_number_moves_the_scale_and_not_the_peak}:
it moves the denominator of $\ell_1/\ell_A$ and leaves the numerator where it
is\rcpt{P15_zonset_determinations}, so the spacing such a start reports is not the spacing its spectrum carries.

\emph{What that leaves is the equality, and it is measured rather than
supposed.} Holding everything else fixed and moving matter--radiation equality alone carries the third peak
and the band it sits in with it: the height ratio $P_1/P_3$ tracks equality on both routes to it --- through
the radiation density and through the matter density, which move the projection in opposite directions ---
agreeing to within a per cent across a $12$ to $24\%$ change in the matter density, so what the band responds
to is the equality and not the route. \emph{And the epoch the distance data fix is what sets it}: equality is
$\omega_m/\omega_r$ with the radiation density measured, so the background that meets the baryon-acoustic
distances is the same background that drives the peaks.

\emph{One candidate for the residual's carrier is measurable, and it is measured shut.} The visibility's
width in comoving distance is $14.3$ per cent greater on this arm than on the control while the two
accumulate the same sound horizon to $0.08$ per cent, and it is what sets the kernel's acceptance in
wavenumber---so it is the quantity to reach for. The swap is well posed and both directions are run.
Widening the control's visibility to this arm's moves its folded harmonic at $\ell_{A}$ from
