$0.3704\pm0.0132$ to $0.3639\pm0.0139$, \emph{away} from the arm's $0.4003$ rather than toward it; narrowing
the arm's to the control's leaves it at $0.4007$, unmoved to one part in a thousand. All four folds are
resolved at $26$--$30\sigma$, so a two-per-cent move is one this statistic would have seen, and the
arm-to-control difference survives the swap and slightly grows, from $+8.1$ to $+10.1$ per cent.
\emph{A mechanism that made the residual would unmake it when undone, and this one does neither}. The same
candidate fails a second way: a wider visibility is Silk damping, which acts strongly at high multipole and
barely at low, so it predicts an arm-to-control ratio that climbs with $\ell$---and the measured ratio is
flat to $9.5$ per cent of its mean across $100$--$1900$, against the $230$ per cent the absolute amplitude
moves over the same
bands\rcpt{P15_the_visibility_width_is_not_the_carrier_the_swap_moves_the_fold_at_ell_A_by_under_two_per_cent_and_fails_the_undo_test_in_both_directions}\rcpt{P15_the_residual_does_not_sit_at_ell_A_it_sits_four_per_cent_off_and_the_visibility_width_candidate_predicts_an_ell_dependence_the_ratio_does_not_have}.

\emph{And the residual's own period is not the comb's, which is where this sector's open work now sits.} The
fold is taken at $\ell_{A}$ by construction and nothing had asked whether that is where the modulation
actually lies. Fitted as a free parameter over $240\le p<380$, the unfitted arm prefers $312.0$, on a
one-sigma interval $[308.5,315.2]$ that excludes $\ell_{A}=298$ at $\Delta\chi^{2}=15.0$---an offset of
$+4.7$ per cent; freeing the four parameters pulls it back to $307.3$ without reaching $\ell_{A}$, which is
the pattern the amplitude shows in the one quantity the figure does not report. \emph{The control prefers a
longer period too}, $347$, so preferring one is not by itself this arm's; what is this arm's is the
determination, its interval being $3.6$ times the tighter because its amplitude is $6.8$ times the larger.
\emph{And the control cannot serve as the null against which that offset is read.} With the period free in
both arms and the difference taken as the statistic, on draws of the bandpower noise shared between them,
the observed $-35.25$ sits at $0.97\sigma$ of $-22.86\pm36.29$: not significant. \emph{The reason carries
more than the null does.} Of that $36.29$, the control contributes $35.52$ and this arm $2.99$: the arm's
period is determined to one per cent and the control's to eleven, so the control's preference for a longer
period is not a preference but noise, and there is no measurable period there to compare against.

\emph{The comparison the data can make is against $\ell_{A}$ itself, which is reproduced to $0.15$ per cent
where the control's period is known to $11$.} Against it the unfitted arm is $+14.00$, which is
$4.69\sigma$, with fewer than one draw in a thousand at or below $\ell_{A}$; refitting pulls it to
$1.05\sigma$ and widens its determination threefold, the same pattern the amplitude shows. It is not the
single-harmonic fit's bias either: giving the second harmonic its own freedom moves the fundamental by
$+0.25$ and leaves $4.90\sigma$.

\emph{And the offset is not a competing period at all.} A period of $312$, and $\ell_{A}$ with a phase
running linearly in multipole, are the same two-dimensional model---identical residual sums of squares---so
what the residual carries is a \emph{drift of the acoustic phase at the correct spacing}, $-96.6^{\circ}$
across $104\le\ell\le1886$, which is $0.268$ of a comb period over the $5.98$ the range spans.
\emph{So the offset is not a statement about $r_s/D_M$}: $\ell_{A}$ sets the spacing and the offset is
$31$ times $\ell_{A}$'s own error, far too large for the spacing to be slightly wrong. \emph{It is the
phase again, and in the one parameter this section had not yet read it in.} The phase intercept is already
measured here, $\varphi/\pi=-0.2349$ against the sky's $-0.2405$; what the period offset adds is the
\emph{slope}. Both are the peak phase and neither is the ruler---which is where this section's own finding
already points: the disagreement is in the phase and not in the scale. \emph{What could carry a running phase at correct spacing is
therefore the driving and the baryon loading, and that is named rather than
measured}\rcpt{P15_the_residual_does_not_sit_at_ell_A_it_sits_four_per_cent_off_and_the_visibility_width_candidate_predicts_an_ell_dependence_the_ratio_does_not_have}\rcpt{P15_the_period_difference_null_is_not_significant_because_the_control_has_no_measurable_period_and_the_offset_is_exactly_a_phase_drift_at_fixed_ell_A}\rcpt{P15_the_phase_slope_does_not_separate_the_loading_from_the_driving_because_a_primordial_tilt_moves_it_as_much_as_the_baryon_density_and_the_clean_lever_is_not_banked}\rcpt{P15_the_de_tilted_peak_set_is_the_phase_observable_the_slope_was_not_and_it_separates_the_baryon_direction_from_a_tilt_by_twenty_but_the_driving_half_needs_its_own_pair}.

\emph{The identity holds and the attribution does not follow from it, and the two are separated here
because the free-period slope turns out not to be a phase observable.} Moved along each of the four
banked background directions, the slope asks for nearly the same fractional change of all of them---
between $4$ and $6$ per cent, a spread of only $1.55$ from most to least responsive. \emph{The tell
is the tilt}: $n_{s}$ multiplies the primordial power and leaves the transfer function untouched, so
it carries no acoustic phase at all, and it asks for the same move as the baryon density to within a
factor of two. A slope fitted to a residual with one amplitude free is therefore reading smooth
spectral gradient alongside any phase, and the drift's \emph{size} is an identity while its
\emph{name} is not something that statistic can supply\rcpt{P15_the_phase_slope_does_not_separate_the_loading_from_the_driving_because_a_primordial_tilt_moves_it_as_much_as_the_baryon_density_and_the_clean_lever_is_not_banked}\rcpt{P15_the_baryon_loading_moves_the_common_offset_OPPOSITE_to_the_full_baryon_direction_so_omega_b_was_never_a_contaminated_loading_lever_but_an_opposing_one}.

\emph{The peak set is the observable the slope is not, and it is read on the same banked spectra.}
Taking the peak positions against integer index after dividing out one global power law---a de-tilt
the same control forces, since without it a primordial tilt appears to move peak positions it cannot
move---the common offset and the odd--even alternation separate the baryon direction from a pure tilt
by factors of $11.3$ and $21.4$ on this arm and $11.4$ and $22.6$ on the control, against $1.3$ for
the slope\rcpt{P15_the_de_tilted_peak_set_is_the_phase_observable_the_slope_was_not_and_it_separates_the_baryon_direction_from_a_tilt_by_twenty_but_the_driving_half_needs_its_own_pair}\rcpt{P15_the_peak_plane_survives_the_merge_the_sky_lands_within_one_sigma_of_the_control_and_a_full_driving_unit_is_thirteen_but_half_the_draws_lose_the_fourth_peak}. \emph{And the two candidate carriers move that pair in opposite senses on both
components}: on the control the driving gives $\Delta\varphi=+0.126$ with
$\Delta\mathrm{alt}=-0.0247$ while the baryon direction gives $-0.0278$ and $+0.0108$---a difference
of sign on two independent components rather than of magnitude on one\rcpt{P15_the_de_tilted_peak_set_is_the_phase_observable_the_slope_was_not_and_it_separates_the_baryon_direction_from_a_tilt_by_twenty_but_the_driving_half_needs_its_own_pair}. \emph{So the plane separates the two candidate carriers from each other and from a pure tilt, and
the arm's own driving signature is read on it rather than borrowed.} With the driving switched
off at the arm's own configuration the acoustic scale is unchanged to the last printed digit, so the
pair differs in the driving and in nothing else, and the signature it returns reproduces the
control's to half a per cent, $\Delta\varphi=+0.1259$ against $+0.1265$. \emph{On the plane's own
discriminant $\lvert\Delta\mathrm{alt}/\Delta\varphi\rvert$ the two candidate carriers sit at
$0.193$ for the driving and $1.039$ for the loading}---a factor of $5.4$ apart, and they move the
common offset in opposite senses, more driving lowering it where more loading raises it. \emph{The
loading is separated from a reparametrization of the clock as well, by a factor of six; the driving
is not}, sitting within a sixth of the largest clock reading and inside the spread of the three.
\emph{So the component pair settles which of the two candidates carries a phase, and leaves open
whether a driving signature is the driving or the clock it is read on}\rcpt{P15_the_arms_own_driving_pair_reproduces_the_banked_one_and_the_peak_plane_separates_the_loading_from_the_driving_but_not_the_driving_from_a_clock_error}\rcpt{P15_the_peak_statistic_cannot_be_pointed_at_the_observed_spectrum_because_a_local_maximum_finder_reads_twenty_one_maxima_where_the_model_has_five}.

\emph{And the clean lever corrects what the baryon density was taken to be on this observable.}
Scaling the baryon loading alone moves the common offset by $+0.0164$ per unit logarithmic change,
where the full baryon direction moves it by $-0.0276$: the part of that response which is not the
loading is $-0.0440$, \emph{larger than the loading's own part by a factor of $2.7$ and opposite to
it in sign}. So the baryon density is not a loading lever carrying a second effect alongside---it is
a lever whose two effects oppose, with the larger of them not the loading, and a reading that took
it for the loading would have placed that carrier on the wrong side of
zero\rcpt{P15_the_baryon_loading_moves_the_common_offset_OPPOSITE_to_the_full_baryon_direction_so_omega_b_was_never_a_contaminated_loading_lever_but_an_opposing_one}.

\emph{What the plane has not been given is the observed residual's own position on it, and the
statistic as built cannot supply it.} Every response above is read on model spectra---smooth curves
with no noise in them---so what is established is that the two candidates are distinguishable and
not which of them the sky's leftover wiggle sits with. \emph{The obstruction is the peak locator
rather than the data's precision, and it is specific.} The binned residual carries the statistic
perfectly well: passing a model through the likelihood's own binning moves the common offset by
$0.0013$ and the alternation by $0.0003$, a bias consistent in sign and applied to models and data
alike. \emph{What fails is that a maximum-finder cannot tell an acoustic peak from a noise wiggle.}
On the observed spectrum it reads $21$ local maxima where the model has $5$, and three of the five
it then takes---at $\ell=464$, $527$ and $617$---lie inside the second acoustic peak's own
neighbourhood, so the refinement fits curvature that is noise. \emph{The resampling confirms the
diagnosis rather than widening the answer}: over $2000$ draws of the full $179\times179$ covariance,
$98.2$ per cent fail to yield five finite peaks at all, and the survivors return a scatter in the
common offset ten times the driving's entire signal while collapsing onto a single direction at a
correlation of $-0.955$---\emph{so not a wide interval in this plane but no interval in it}.
\emph{The two regimes are separated by exactly one property}, whether a spectrum carries noise at
the scale of its own curvature, which is why this bounds where the statistic may be used and
withdraws nothing measured on the model side. \emph{And that property is set by the binning rather
than by the sky, which is what carries the statistic across.} At the likelihood's native bin width the
per-bin scatter is $2.4$ times the signal's own bin-to-bin increment and the located maxima come back
out of order, one of them displaced by $547$ multipoles. Merging the bins in pairs doubles the
increment and reduces the scatter by $\sqrt{2}$, so the ratio falls by $2\sqrt{2}$, to $0.8$, and the
locator then reads the observed spectrum's first four peaks within $3.4$ multipoles
of its own reading of a model spectrum passed through the identical merge, the located gaps coming
back $303$, $268$ and $309$ against the model's $302$, $269$ and $312$. \emph{The usable merge is a
factor of two and only a factor of two}: merged in threes the locator returns nothing on either, its
own search window then holding fewer than four points, which is a property of the locator rather than
of the spectrum. \emph{So the two halves of this comparison want opposite binnings}---the comb wants
points the correlation above shows it cannot use, and the locator wants the bank merged, which costs
exactly those points---\emph{and it is the located peak positions, on a merged bank and at four peaks
rather than five, that reach the observed
spectrum}\rcpt{P15_the_peak_statistic_cannot_be_pointed_at_the_observed_spectrum_because_a_local_maximum_finder_reads_twenty_one_maxima_where_the_model_has_five}\rcpt{P15_the_three_parameter_comb_fails_its_validation_because_the_peak_spacing_DRIFTS_and_the_missing_parameter_is_the_one_the_sector_is_about}\rcpt{P15_the_carrier_separation_on_L_p_is_the_combs_peak_count_not_the_carrier_and_the_closure_has_no_number_because_the_degeneracy_is_sampling_invariant}.

\emph{Carried onto that bank the plane reproduces its own banked driving pair, and the observed
spectrum can be placed on it.} The de-tilted peak statistic through the identical merge gives
$+0.12703$ and $-0.02494$ for the driving difference against the banked $+0.126$ and $-0.0247$, so
the merge costs under one per cent on both components; the restriction to four peaks leaves the
common offset unchanged to a tenth of a per cent and rescales the alternation by a fifth, which is a
property of the peak count rather than of the merge, since the instrument's own unmerged samples
give the same $1.20$. \emph{Re-measured at four peaks the separation survives}: the baryon direction
is still between $9.8$ and $12.1$ times a pure tilt on the common offset and between $8.2$ and $9.9$
on the alternation, against $1.3$ for the free-period slope, and the lever lost on the alternation is
the tilt control's rather than the signal's, $n_{s}$'s residual leakage onto it growing four-fold at
four peaks. The observed spectrum then sits $(+0.00756,-0.00255)$ from the matched control on the
plane, the two candidate carriers remaining separated there by a factor of $5.6$ on the plane's
discriminant. \emph{What that position is worth follows from the likelihood's own covariance carried
through the merge exactly}---the merge is a linear map on the binned spectrum, so no correlation is
dropped---\emph{and through the locator by simulation}: the statistic's distribution is heavy-tailed
by a factor of eleven, and on its robust scale one full unit of the driving is $13$ standard
deviations on the common offset and $3.1$ on the alternation, while the observed point sits $0.8$ and
$0.3$ from the control. \emph{So the observed spectrum is consistent with the control's own driving,
and one full unit of difference either way is excluded}---and it is the driving that this bounds, the
loading moving the plane by only $1.8$ and $2.4$ standard deviations per unit. \emph{Three things
bound that statement and none of them is the noise, and they divide it: the exclusion carries and the
agreement is the conditional half.} The comparison made here is the observed spectrum against a model
rather than one arm against the other, so the projection's own running phase is not differenced away
by it: in these units that phase is $5.7$ times the displacement measured, and were it to fail to
cancel at its full size the observed point would move to $5.3$ standard deviations, while the
thirteen-deviation scale is a model-against-model quantity and is untouched. \emph{And that condition
is of the same kind as the one below rather than merely unmeasured}: what it asks is whether the
model's projection kernel is the sky's, which cannot be settled from the same spectrum without
circularity and from outside is another model's answer, so no bounded form of it exists. \emph{The
acoustic scale
is assumed rather than measured here}: moving it by one per cent moves the observed offset by $2.4$ of
those standard deviations, so the agreement would require the scale to be the control's to $0.4$ per
cent where the exclusion requires only $5.5$---\emph{and that condition is not one this measurement
can discharge}, since a scale read off the same peaks enters the statistic that is being tested and
any scale brought in from outside is a standard fit to the same sky, so either route restates the
condition instead of checking it. \emph{And one of the maxima is not certain to be there at all, but
it is the third rather than the fourth}---on $48$ per cent of noise realisations of this spectrum the
locator returns
three peaks instead of four, and the slot that goes missing is the third in all but a twelfth of
those, the fourth being located in $96$ per cent---so every figure here is conditional on the
statistic existing, which it does on about half of the realisations. \emph{That condition costs
efficiency rather than width.} An offset formed from the first two peaks is defined on $99.6$ per
cent of realisations, and inside the four-peak selection its robust width is $0.982$ of its width
over the whole ensemble, with a bootstrap interval of $0.953$ to $1.010$; the realisations that are
discarded are $1.04$ times wider, and the loss rate is flat to five points across quintiles of how
far the offset already sits from the median, where a selection that cut the statistic would climb.
\emph{Carried across to the four-peak width}---the only route to it, since that statistic does not
exist off its own selection---\emph{that ratio widens the deviations above by under two per cent,
which is why the selection enters this paragraph as a condition on the statistic's existence and not
as a correction to its scale}. \emph{Nor does that reading turn on where the threshold for it was
set}: taken at the unfavourable end of the interval instead of at its centre, one full unit of the
driving is $12.5$ standard deviations and the observed point sits at $0.75$, so both figures quoted
above survive the change\rcpt{P15_the_selection_does_not_truncate_so_the_thirteen_sigma_stands_at_twelve_point_nine_and_the_peak_that_goes_missing_is_the_third_not_the_fourth}\rcpt{P15_the_peak_plane_survives_the_merge_the_sky_lands_within_one_sigma_of_the_control_and_a_full_driving_unit_is_thirteen_but_half_the_draws_lose_the_fourth_peak}\rcpt{P15_the_selection_does_not_truncate_so_the_thirteen_sigma_stands_at_twelve_point_nine_and_the_peak_that_goes_missing_is_the_third_not_the_fourth}.

\emph{Built with those three parameters it does not survive its own control, and what it fails on is
this section's own quantity.} It recovers the acoustic scale on three spectra of six---both
driving-off cases, to about a tenth of a per cent, and one of the driving-on cases to $0.27$---and
misses the other three by between $18$ and $20$ per cent. \emph{What separates the two groups is not
the driving.} Each arm's driving pair straddles the divide: the same arm is recovered to a tenth of a
per cent with the driving off and missed by $18.5$ per cent with it on, so a difference taken across
that pair is not a difference in the driving but a difference in whether the instrument worked.
\emph{The reason is visible in the gaps.} A comb's successive gaps are $\ell_{A}(1\mp2a)$, so its
high gaps equal one another and its low gaps equal one another; the arm's run $302$, $270$, $312$,
$295$---alternating, and \emph{rising}, the highs by $10$ and the lows by $25$. On every spectrum
tried both the high and the low gaps rise, and a single linear drift term reduces the residue of the
gap fit on every one of them---from $9.5$ multipoles to $3.8$ on this arm. \emph{It is the size of
that residue and not the presence of the driving that sorts the three the comb recovers from the
three it misses.} \emph{And omitting the term does not merely fit worse, it returns a different
alternation}---larger by between a third and nine tenths on the driving-on spectra---which is one of
the two components the separation above is read on. \emph{A drift in the spacing is a linear phase
drift at fixed $\ell_{A}$}, which is what this section's residual was found to be, so the instrument
is short of exactly the freedom the measurement is about\rcpt{P15_the_three_parameter_comb_fails_its_validation_because_the_peak_spacing_DRIFTS_and_the_missing_parameter_is_the_one_the_sector_is_about}.

\emph{Two cautions travel with a fit of this kind, and both were met here.} Given each harmonic its
own envelope the fit improves by a factor of $542$ while the acoustic scale runs to the edge of the
search box and the answer worsens by a further $24$ points: \emph{a good fit is not evidence that the
comb was found}, the extra oscillatory freedom absorbing the spectrum instead of locating its
periodicity. \emph{And a search started where the answer is expected cannot report that the answer is
elsewhere}---a grid of starts offset from the known scale returns a worse optimum on five spectra of
six, and on the one the comb recovers it had reported a minimum a fifth away from the true
one\rcpt{P15_the_three_parameter_comb_fails_its_validation_because_the_peak_spacing_DRIFTS_and_the_missing_parameter_is_the_one_the_sector_is_about}.

\emph{Admitting the drift as a fourth fitted parameter confirms that it is the right one and shows
that this data set cannot carry it.} It is the right one on a test it was not fitted to: the drift
the fit returns agrees with the curvature measured independently from the gap sequence, in sign and
to within a sixth, on four of the six spectra. \emph{And the driving-off pair, which looks ruined on
a careless reading, is not}---in a drifting comb the fitted spacing is the value extrapolated to the
window's edge rather than the acoustic scale, and read without that pivot the drifting spacing passes
through the known scale at $\ell\simeq815$ and $813$, inside the fitted window, while the fit
improves by nearly a factor of two. \emph{What defeats it is the resolution of the data rather than
the model, and the shape of that limit is sharper than a correlation.} Across every spectrum whose fit
is interior to its search box the spacing and the drift come back correlated between $-0.94$ and
$-0.98$, and \emph{that correlation is a property of the coordinates and not of the data}: referring
the comb to a pivot inside the fitted window rather than to its edge is an exact shear on the two
parameters, leaves the fitted curve identical to the digits, and drives the correlation to zero, with
the decorrelating pivot falling inside the window on every spectrum that has a curvature to transform.
\emph{What it does not do is deliver the acoustic scale.} The combination the data determine at that
pivot is the spacing in the neighbourhood of the second peak, and it lies below the computed scale on
every fitting window tried, by between $5$ and $20$ multipoles with one sign throughout. \emph{The
formal error quoted on it is not a measure of how well it is determined}: moving only the window's
upper edge moves the number by five per cent while that error runs between $0.01$ and $4.3$, so the
window spread and not the error bar is the honest statement of its reproducibility. \emph{So this bank
pins one combination of the scale and its drift and the acoustic scale is not that combination}: one
direction of the pair is tight, the orthogonal one is loose, and the scale lies along the loose one.

\emph{What the tight direction turns out to measure is the fit's own grip on the peak train rather
than anything about the sound.} Pointing the peak locator at the fitted curve instead of at the data,
the pivot spacing \emph{is} that curve's mean peak gap---to a fifth of a per cent on the two
driving-off spectra, where the fitted maxima sit $1.6$ multipoles from the located ones---and is
$21$ to $27$ per cent away from it on the other four, the observed spectrum included, where they sit
$21$ to $24$ out. \emph{So a difference taken along that direction separates a fit that holds the peak
train from one that has lost it}, and the matched differencing that would carry it to the sky is blind
to the quantity of interest: arm against control, with the observed spectrum differenced out of both,
comes to $0.3$ multipoles against a within-pair difference of $57$. \emph{And the degeneracy behind all
of it is invariant to everything this bank can vary.} The correlation between the spacing and its
drift stays between $0.94$ and $0.98$ across thirteen fits spanning $78$ to $176$ points, five windows
and six spectra, and halving the point count moves it in the fourth decimal while inflating the errors
by the expected $\sqrt{2}$ and doing nothing else. \emph{The reason is geometric rather than
statistical}: a level and a slope in the same variable are monomials whose correlation over an
interval is fixed by the interval's endpoint ratio, not by its width or by how densely it is sampled,
which is both why the shear removes it exactly and why no amount of data touches
it\rcpt{P15_the_fourth_parameter_recovers_the_drift_the_gaps_show_but_the_spacing_and_the_drift_are_not_separable_so_the_data_half_still_does_not_happen}\rcpt{P15_centring_removes_the_correlation_exactly_and_closes_the_route_anyway_because_the_decorrelated_parameter_is_not_the_acoustic_scale}\rcpt{P15_the_carrier_separation_on_L_p_is_the_combs_peak_count_not_the_carrier_and_the_closure_has_no_number_because_the_degeneracy_is_sampling_invariant}. \emph{A four-parameter comb is the better description of
these spectra and the worse instrument for this measurement}, and a finer binning would not change
that.

\emph{And the drift itself is a determined quantity of this construction rather than a feature of the
numerics, which is settled by derivation and localises it away from the acoustic physics entirely.}
The source comb in wavenumber carries no drift at all: measured on the source extrema at
recombination it lands on the integers to $0.015$, and switching the driving on displaces it by a
nearly constant $-0.12$ in $kr_{s}/\pi$---a spread of $0.029$ across six peaks---so the driving
supplies the offset and the alternation and cannot drift a comb, a constant phase shift having no
curvature. \emph{The projection manufactures the whole of it.} A perfect comb, extrema placed exactly
at $kr_{s}=n\pi$ with no dynamics behind them, acquires gaps of $278.5$, $289.5$, $293.4$ and $295.4$
on projection---rising, decelerating and saturating below $\ell_{A}$. \emph{And it is obtained in
closed form rather than read off a spectrum}: replacing $j_{\ell}^{2}(kD_{M})$ by its own asymptotic
average and substituting $x=\ell\cosh t$ so the inverse-square-root edge integrates exactly returns
every peak to better than $0.5$ in $\ell$ and the drift to $0.01$, which identifies the drift as the
centroid shift of the projection kernel's own window acting on a damped comb, with $r_{s}$, $D_{M}$
and $r_{D}$ as its only inputs. Moving the handover epoch by a decade shifts the comb by $0.30$ in
$\ell$ and the drift by $0.002$---\emph{the collapse leg's contribution being an amplitude and not a
phase, here measured rather than quoted}---and the instrument's discrete wavenumber ladder and a
uniform grid agree on the drift to $0.6$ per cent. \emph{So the drift is a parameter-free prediction
of the spectrum and no test at all of the rate split}: this arm returns $+4.38$ against the control's
$+4.42$, with every peak position agreeing to better than $0.4$ in $\ell$, because the three lengths
that carry it are shared between the two frameworks to within a per
cent\rcpt{P15_the_spacing_drift_is_made_in_the_projection_so_the_construction_determines_it_and_the_handover_contributes_nothing}.

\emph{The same kernel also carries a running phase, and asking it the question the residual asks
clears it as a candidate while leaving a floor behind.} The kernel's phase depends on exactly two
dimensionless ratios---checked by scaling all three lengths together, which leaves it unmoved to ten
decimal places---and the two arms differ in one of them by $0.27$ per cent and share the other, the
acoustic angle, to a part in seventy thousand, because the angle is what this construction computes.
\emph{So the phase difference between the arms is $0.014$ degrees in magnitude across
$104\le\ell\le1886$, against a residual of $-96.6$}: nearly four orders of magnitude below it, with
the closed-form window and the full spherical-Bessel sum agreeing to the fifth decimal place and each
converged in its own parameter. \emph{Only the magnitude of that difference is a convention-free
statement, and the sign is not.} The two arms' damping lengths respond to the choice of endpoint in
opposite directions, so on the other convention their separation in the governing ratio widens tenfold
and the difference between them changes sign, at $0.14$ degrees; the clearance survives either way, at
no less than $2.8$ orders of magnitude. \emph{The projection is therefore not a carrier of this
residual and the candidates remain the driving, the baryon loading and the clock the driving is read
on.} \emph{What it does carry is a floor}: across the same range the kernel's own running phase is
$-15.5$ degrees, $16$ per cent of the residual, and common to the two arms to a tenth of a per
cent---\emph{so a sixth of a running phase at correct spacing is manufactured in the projection and is
differenced away between the arms, which is where the matched-procedure discipline earns its place
rather than being a precaution}. \emph{That fraction is a statement about a range and not a constant}:
it is insensitive to the endpoint convention, moving under a tenth of a point, and runs from $9$ to
$24$ per cent as the range is taken from $200$ or from $50$ instead, so the figure must be quoted with
the range it was read over. \emph{And the Bessel route reaches that
answer only when it is sampled finely enough}: at under two points per Bessel period it manufactures a
difference between the arms $74$ times the true one, while the window route is immune because it
averages the kernel analytically---the instrument's own documented aliasing failure arriving in the
phase rather than in the peak
positions\rcpt{P15_the_kernels_phase_difference_between_the_arms_is_four_orders_below_the_residual_so_the_projection_is_cleared_and_a_sixth_of_the_scale_is_common_mode}.

\emph{What the plane does not separate is the driving from the clock the driving is read on, and that
is established on the construction's own two clocks rather than supposed.} The lap's conformal
parameter and the seams' own leg parameter disagree about the seam-bounded stretches by a factor of
$4.6083$ and disagree about which of them is the longer, so reading the drive on one where the
oscillator's own clock is the other is a reparametrization of the drive---and its consequence divides
in two. \emph{The spacing cannot move}: $r_{s}=\int c_{s}\dd\eta$ is an integral on the oscillator's
own clock, which a reparametrization of the drive does not touch, so $\ell_{A}$ is identically the
same number and whatever moves is a phase at fixed spacing. It is not a constant one, rising by
$0.161$ to $0.179$ radians between $\ell=200$ and $\ell=500$ against the $0.0305$ the same estimator
reports with the drive switched off, where that control is flat to $0.03$ across the whole range.
\emph{The direction on the component plane, though, is not fixed by the clock alone.} Taken on three
pairs of stretches of the one object the common offset changes sign, the ratio
$\lvert\Delta\mathrm{alt}/\Delta\varphi\rvert$ runs between $0.087$ and $0.168$, and every reading
falls inside the cone the driving's $0.196$ and the baryon direction's $0.388$ already span, each
carrying one of those two sign pairs rather than a third. \emph{So the component pair names the
carrier between the two candidates and does not distinguish the driving from a reparametrization of
it}, and the two are distinguished instead by the shape of the run: the reparametrization spends
between two thirds and nine tenths of its whole $200\le\ell\le1400$ rise below $\ell\simeq500$ and is
nearly flat above, where the driving's own phase rises to $+0.468$ at $\ell=500$ and then falls
monotonically to $+0.251$ at $\ell=1400$. \emph{A weighting of the high-multipole half of the range
against the low is therefore where that separation sits, and it is located rather than
built}\rcpt{P15_a_drive_clock_error_cannot_move_the_spacing_so_it_is_a_running_phase_but_its_direction_on_the_peak_plane_depends_on_which_pair_of_stretches_is_read_and_the_two_clocks_disagree_about_which_is_longer}.

\emph{Two things this section does not claim, and they are separated because a reader would otherwise
supply them.} The alternation figures are read on a multipole grid whose step is the same size as the
sky's own gap contraction is large, so \emph{a contraction of the sky's size is resolved while the smaller
ones at lower $\ell$ are not}\rcpt{P15_the_phase_moves_with_the_datum_and_only_the_spacing_does_not}; a finer
grid is what would settle those. And the equality result accounts for the band it moves and not for the phase
residual: \emph{what the phase is to be read against is the driving subtraction on this arm, and
\S\ref{sec:scope} carries it as such.}

\section{What the substrate determines: the amplitude floor and the classical character}\label{sec:amplitude}

One might ask whether the observed fluctuation amplitude is the substrate's own de Sitter vacuum---the natural CR analogue of the inflationary spectrum-is-the-stretched-vacuum story. It is not, by a decisive margin.

\begin{proposition}[The substrate vacuum is negligible against the observed amplitude]\label{prop:amplitude}
The substrate curvature scale is the cosmological-constant scale: in Planck units $\Lambda\ell_P^2\approx3\times10^{-122}$, and the de Sitter vacuum metric-fluctuation power is of the same order, $(H_\Lambda/\Mpl)^2\sim10^{-122}$. Against the observed $A_s\approx2\times10^{-9}$ this is smaller by $\sim10^{113}$.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\emph{Argument.} $\Lambda=3(H_0/c)^2\Omega_\Lambda$ gives $\Lambda\ell_P^2$ directly; $H_\Lambda=c\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ and $(\hbar H_\Lambda/E_P)^2$ give the vacuum power (receipts: \texttt{verify\_numeric.py}, anchor~5\rcpt{P15_verify_numeric}). The prefactor ($\sim10^{-122}$ versus $\sim10^{-123}$) is a $2\pi$ and reduced-versus-full Planck-mass convention; the conclusion is robust to it. $\qquad\square$

\emph{One scope qualification is owed immediately, and closing it is what makes the classical reading a result rather than an inference.} Proposition~\ref{prop:amplitude} rules out the \emph{substrate's} vacuum. But the reading it supports locates the fluctuations in the \emph{progenitor}, and the progenitor's own vacuum is a different quantity: it is normalised at the collapse leg's curvature scale, not at $H_\Lambda$, and it is amplified on its way here by the branch point's mode mixing. Neither effect is small, and neither is addressed above. Both can be computed, because the collapse-side background is a single function: with $A=2M$ and $\rho=2\sqrt B/A$ the exact closed dust-plus-radiation interior gives $a=M(\rho|\sigma|+\sigma^2/2)$ near the crunch, whose Mukhanov--Sasaki potential $z''/z=2/(|\sigma|(|\sigma|+2\rho))$ is \emph{independent of the mass}---so $M$ enters only through the final division $\zeta=v/a$. The passage then has exactly three stages, and each contributes one factor of $1/\rho$. A mode leaves the sound horizon on the $\beta=2$ leg and freezes as $v\to D_k/|\sigma|$ with the scale-invariant $D_k=\sqrt{\hbar/2}\,k^{-3/2}$; the connection through equality is elementary, since $v=a\int\!\mathrm{d}\sigma/a^2$ solves the $k=0$ problem in closed form and carries $D_k/|\sigma|$ to the constant $(3/2\rho)D_k$ at the crunch; the crunch monodromy then leaks that constant into the survivor with coefficient $4\pi/\rho$---twice the tensor value, because the true hydrodynamical $z_S=a\bigl(a+\tfrac{4B}{3A}\bigr)/a'$ has $z_S''/z_S\to2/(\rho x)$ where $a''/a\to1/(\rho x)$---the companion paper's numerically obtained $-2\pi i/\rho$~\cite{JanzenCosmogenesis} being the \emph{tensor} value of the same object, recovered here in closed form as the continuation of the $(\sigma/\rho)\ln\sigma$ term the $(0,1)$ resonance forces; and the survivor's curvature is $\zeta=C/(M\rho)$, the last $1/\rho$ being the crunch's own slope $a'(\eta_c)=-M\rho$. The result is a transfer law with no free constant,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transfer-law}
\mathcal{P}_\zeta \;=\; \frac{18\,k^3 D_k^2}{M^2\rho^6},
\end{equation}
which is \emph{exactly} scale-invariant on vacuum data---the $k^3$ cancels the $k^{-3}$ identically---and which therefore delivers, for the vacuum, $A_s=9(\ell_P/M)^2\rho^{-6}$ (receipt: \texttt{P15\_the\_progenitor\_vacuum\_is\_negligible\_too.py}\rcpt{P15_the_progenitor_vacuum_is_negligible_too}, the connection coefficient confirmed against integration from vacuum data to $0.1\%$ over a decade in $\rho$). \emph{One check is owed here and is not optional, because the hydrodynamical variable differs from $a$ in three places and not one}: $z_S\propto a^{3/2}$ in the matter era, so the exponents there are $(-2,3)$ against $a$'s $(-1,2)$; the crunch residue doubles; and the final division is by $z_S$ rather than by $a$. \emph{Propagating the same physical initial condition under each potential returns a ratio of exactly $2$ in amplitude at every composition tested}---the matter-era exponents and the change of divisor cancel, and the crunch residue alone survives. So the $z\propto a$ chain is corrected by one factor rather than rebuilt, and Eq.~\eqref{eq:transfer-law} is the true-variable law.

\begin{proposition}[The progenitor vacuum is negligible too]\label{prop:progenitor-amplitude}
With the mass capped at the recollapse threshold $M\le\alpha/3\sqrt3$ and the radiation fraction at the largest value the observed spectrum permits, Eq.~\eqref{eq:transfer-law} on vacuum data returns $A_s\sim1\times10^{-112}$, short of the observed $2\times10^{-9}$ by $\sim10^{103}$. \emph{The figure is read on the potential's scale-free branch}; on the observed band, which lies above
the break the radiation content sets, the residue is some thousands larger in power, which moves the
shortfall by a few orders out of a hundred and three and leaves the conclusion where it is.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\emph{Argument.} $(\ell_P/M)^2\ge27(\ell_P/\alpha)^2\approx2.6\times10^{-121}$ at the cap, and the companion paper determines $\rho\simeq5.45\times10^{-2}$~\cite{JanzenCosmogenesis}; $9\times2.6\times10^{-121}\times\rho^{-6}$ then gives the figure. Running it backwards, the vacuum would reach the observed amplitude only at $\rho\sim3\times10^{-19}$, thirteen orders below what nucleosynthesis on the same leg permits. $\qquad\square$

\emph{So ten orders separate the substrate's vacuum from the progenitor's amplified one---Eq.~\eqref{eq:transfer-law} carries the crunch mixing already, so the amplification is the $\rho^{-6}$ and is not a second factor on top of it---and it is still short by a hundred and three}, and the classical character of the primordial statistics is established for the source that actually supplies them rather than only for the one that does not. Two consequences of \emph{character}, not of number, follow. 

First, the observed amplitude is inherited classical content---boundary data from the progenitor, not a quantum fluctuation generated within the Schwarzschild--de Sitter era, nor one generated in the progenitor's collapse. 

Second, there is no inflationary consistency relation: $r=-8n_t$ presupposes that scalar and tensor spectra are one vacuum stretched by one expansion, and with the amplitude not vacuum-sourced that link is absent; the substrate tensor floor is the same $\sim10^{-122}$, so there are no substrate-sourced primordial $B$-modes, and any observed tensors would themselves be inherited content rather than a measure of a substrate inflationary scale. \emph{And the tensor statement can be made unconditionally, which the scalar one cannot.} The scalar law, Eq.~\eqref{eq:transfer-law}, is the true-variable law only through the hydrodynamical $z_S$ of the progenitor's own fluid, which carries that fluid's equation of state and sound speed---and across the progenitor's own equality neither $w$ nor $c_s$ is constant. 

\emph{For tensors that restriction is absent}: $z_T=aM_{\rm Pl}/2$ exactly, for any content, any equation of state and any sound speed, so $z_T''/z_T=a''/a$ identically and the entire passage---background, connection, monodromy---applies verbatim with nothing assumed. It returns
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tensor-floor}
\mathcal{P}_T \;=\; 144\pi\,(\ell_P/M)^2\rho^{-6}\;\simeq\;5\times10^{-111},
\end{equation}
evaluated at the recollapse cap and the largest composition the spectrum permits, against an observational ceiling of $\sim7\times10^{-11}$: \emph{below it by a hundred orders} (receipt: \texttt{P15\_no\_primordial\_B\_modes\_unconditionally.py}\rcpt{P15_no_primordial_B_modes_unconditionally}). \emph{So the absence of primordial $B$-modes is a prediction of this construction at any conceivable sensitivity, and it does not rest on the substrate's own floor}---the floor argument, like its scalar twin, ruled out the substrate's vacuum and said nothing about the progenitor's. What the crossing does to the ratio is likewise fixed and free of $k$: every factor of the passage is shared by the two sectors, so $r_{\rm out}=6\,r_{\rm in}$: the passage alone would carry the ratio by $24$, and the scalar mixing being twice the tensor's divides that by four in the power, and the observed bound becomes a statement about the parent, namely that its own tensor-to-scalar ratio was below $5\times10^{-3}$. \emph{An observed tensor signal would therefore not falsify the construction but measure the previous generation}, which is the same reading the amplitude receives. The substrate fixes that the fluctuations are classical, inherited, and not governed by a vacuum consistency relation, and leaves their size to the handover. \emph{What it does fix is the multiplier.} Eq.~\eqref{eq:transfer-law} holds for any incoming growing-mode amplitude, quantum or classical, so the crossing determines how much an amplitude is magnified without determining the amplitude---which is this construction's one-constant character read at the level of the perturbations rather than of the couplings. Two corollaries follow and are worth stating because each closes a natural hope in one direction and opens a statement in the other. \emph{$A_s$ does not measure the progenitor's mass}: with $D_k$ free, the relation cannot be inverted, and the mass must be reached, if at all, by a route that does not run through the amplitude. And \emph{the classical datum has a size}: since $A_s\propto D_k^2$, the parent's growing-mode amplitude must have exceeded its own vacuum's by $\sim10^{47}$ at the top of the permitted composition window---so this cosmology does not merely decline to predict $A_s$, it says the previous generation's perturbations were enormously super-vacuum, which is a claim about the parent and not about us.

\section{What the substrate determines: the throat and isotropization}\label{sec:throat}

At the branch point the near-horizon geometry is fixed, and what the perturbations cross is fixed with it.

\begin{proposition}[Equal-radii $\dS_2\times S^2$ throat]\label{prop:throat}
At the Nariai locus $\Lambda G^2M^2/c^4=1/9$ the two positive horizon roots of $f(r)=1-2GM/c^2r-r^2/\alpha^2$, $\alpha=\sqrt{3/\Lambda}$, merge at the \emph{Nariai radius} $r_{N}=\alpha/\sqrt3=1/\sqrt\Lambda$ (the symbol is the algebroid paper's~\cite{JanzenAlgebroid}; it is written $r_{N}$ and not $r_\star$ because $r_*$ is the tortoise coordinate used below), where $f(r_{N})=f'(r_{N})=0$ and $f''(r_{N})=-2\Lambda$ (the cosmology selects this $M>0$ member of the parity-conjugate Nariai pair; the $M<0$ member has instead one positive and two negative roots, the two exchanged by $r\mapsto-r$~\cite{JanzenSlicing,JanzenGroupoid}). The near-horizon geometry is $\dS_2\times S^2$ with both curvature radii equal to $1/\sqrt\Lambda$: the $S^2$ has two-Ricci $R_2=2/r_{N}^2=2\Lambda$, and the $\dS_2$ has radius$^2=-2/f''(r_{N})=1/\Lambda$.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\emph{Argument.} Direct from $f$ at the Nariai mass (receipts: \texttt{verify\_geometry.py}, anchor~2\rcpt{P15_verify_geometry}; this is the Ginsparg--Perry near-horizon limit~\cite{GinspargPerry1983}). $\qquad\square$

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.74\textwidth]{seam-merger.png}
\caption{The Schwarzschild--de~Sitter metric function $f(r)=1-2GM/c^2r-r^2/\alpha^2$ across the three regimes. Under-critical masses ($\Lambda G^2M^2/c^4<\tfrac19$) give two distinct positive horizons; over-critical masses give none. The cosmogenesis branch point is carried on the \emph{Nariai} member ($=\tfrac19$), where the two horizons merge into a single \emph{degenerate} double root at $r_{N}=\alpha/\sqrt3=1/\sqrt\Lambda$ with $f(r_{N})=f'(r_{N})=0$ and hence vanishing surface gravity $\kappa=0$. The degeneracy of this root is what the throat geometry (Proposition~\ref{prop:throat}) and the transmission dichotomy (\S\ref{sec:transmission}) turn on.}
\label{fig:seam-merger}
\end{figure}

A field on $\dS_2\times S^2$ (Fig.~\ref{fig:seam-merger}) decomposes in $S^2$ harmonics; the degree-$\ell$ harmonic becomes a $\dS_2$ field of effective mass $m^2=\ell(\ell+1)/r_{N}^2$, i.e.\ $m^2/H_{\dS_2}^2=\ell(\ell+1)$. With the $\dS_2$ index $\nu^2=\tfrac14-m^2/H^2$, the monopole $\ell=0$ has $\nu=\tfrac12$---a scale-invariant base---while every $\ell\ge1$ has $\ell(\ell+1)\ge2$, hence $\nu^2<0$: the heavy principal series, which oscillate and decay through the throat. We read this as an angular no-hair, and the reading is grounded rather than asserted: the $\dS_2$ factor of the throat is a positive-$\Lambda$ epoch, and the de~Sitter no-hair theorem~\cite{Wald1983} damps exactly the anisotropic content---every $\ell\ge1$ harmonic is heavy principal-series, oscillating and decaying, while only the isotropic monopole $\ell=0$ survives as the $\nu=\tfrac12$ base---so the throat isotropizes what passes through it (receipts: \texttt{verify\_throat\_tower.py}\rcpt{P15_verify_throat_tower}). The geometry is established (Proposition~\ref{prop:throat}) and the mode tower follows from it; the no-hair reading is the standard theorem read on the throat, and we flag one guard as load-bearing: the index $\ell$ of this throat tower is the $S^2$-harmonic degree of the \emph{near-horizon} geometry at areal radius $1/\sqrt\Lambda$, and is \emph{not} the observable microwave-background multipole, which lives on the last-scattering sphere of radius $D_C$. \emph{And the same conclusion is now reached a second time by a route that shares neither the decomposition nor the theorem, which is why it is worth having}: carrying the layer's $S^3$ harmonics along the cosmogenetic bead in its own conformal time, where the lift from the comoving turnaround to $r=0$ is a segment of \emph{purely imaginary} $\eta$ of finite length $s_{\rm tot}=\Gamma(\tfrac16)\sqrt\pi/\Gamma(\tfrac23)\sqrt3\,2^{1/3}=3.3387380$ ($\S$\ref{sec:scope}). \emph{There the isotropic mode crosses with amplitude exactly unity}---$u=a$ solves the lift equation identically at $k=0$---\emph{while every anisotropic one is damped by a pure-number function of its own degree}, $T(k)\to2^{7/3}k^2e^{-k\,s_{\rm tot}}$, \emph{and the eigenvalue $k^2$ is the carried layer's own rather than a round sphere's.} \S\ref{sec:largescale} obtains that layer with squashing $\varepsilon$, and its Laplacian---built from the left-invariant forms of the same group manifold---has spectrum
\begin{equation}
\lambda(L,m)=L(L+2)+4(\varepsilon^{-2}-1)m^2,\qquad |m|\le L/2,\quad m\equiv L/2 \;(\mathrm{mod}\;1),
\label{eq:squashed-spectrum}
\end{equation}
so $L(L+2)$ is its $\varepsilon=1$ member. \emph{For scalars the deformation reaches the spectrum and not the basis}: the same matrix elements diagonalise the Laplacian at every squashing, so $(L,m)$ is conserved along the bead and nothing mixes. \emph{The scope of that sentence is the scalar sector and is not general}---it holds because a scalar on a left-invariant metric carries no frame index, and the vector sector below is where that premise fails. \emph{Two sectors the squashing cannot reach at all}: $\lambda(0,0)=0$ identically, so the isotropic mode crosses at unity for every $\varepsilon$; and $m=0$, admissible exactly when $L$ is even, keeps $\lambda=L(L+2)$ with no $\varepsilon$ in it. \emph{And every other scalar mode is bounded below uniformly}---the minimum at $|m|=L/2$ is $2L+L^2/\varepsilon^2$, so $\lambda\ge2L$ for every $\varepsilon>0$---\emph{which puts the suppression exponent at no less than $\sqrt{2L}\,s_{\rm tot}$}: at $L=1$ that is $4.722$, giving at most $8.97\times10^{-2}$ against $4.66\times10^{-2}$ for the same asymptote at the round value---\emph{and the exact transport, below, weakens it by $2.49$ at that degree rather than by the bound's factor of two}, a suppression still above ten and monotone in degree\rcpt{P15_the_bead_routes_damping_survives_on_the_squashed_layer_because_the_berger_harmonics_carry_no_squashing_and_the_spectrum_is_bounded_below_uniformly}. \emph{Where the squashing drops out of the eigenvalue, the constant-$k$ transport is exact rather than approximate}, so on the $m=0$ slice the transmission is a number and not an envelope: $4.158\times10^{-3}$, $1.116\times10^{-5}$, $2.438\times10^{-8}$, $4.730\times10^{-11}$ and $8.497\times10^{-14}$ at $L=2,4,6,8,10$, against $T(0)=1$ to a part in $10^{14}$ as the control. \emph{Those degrees sit below the asymptotic regime of the parameter-free form, which therefore understates every one of them and the lowest by most}---exceeding it by $30$, $17$, $12$, $9$ and $7.5$ per cent \emph{of the asymptotic value} in turn---\emph{so $2^{7/3}k^2e^{-k\,s_{\rm tot}}$ is a lower bound on this sector's transmission rather than an estimate of it, approached from above and not crossed}\rcpt{P15_the_transmission_figure_is_deliverable_after_all_because_the_descending_sectors_eigenvalue_carries_no_squashing_so_constant_k_is_exact_exactly_where_the_sector_descends}\rcpt{P15_the_floors_understatement_is_measured_against_the_floor_and_not_the_exact_value_and_sec_largescale_never_carries_the_asymptote}. \emph{The charged modes do carry a squashing-dependent eigenvalue, and it diverges at the double root}---so an exact figure exists on the slice whose amplitude the transport delivers, and is absent on the modes whose amplitude it does not. \emph{The Berger parameter is the squashing normalised to the horn's round datum}, $\alpha\sqrt{-f}/r$, since the bare ratio tends to $1/\alpha$ rather than to unity; \emph{so normalised it passes through unity at exactly $r=2M=\tfrac23r_{N}$, free of $\alpha$}. \emph{On the lift the layer is timelike, $-f<0$, so that parameter is imaginary there and what enters the spectrum is its modulus}---\emph{and the $m=0$ modes, which carry no $\varepsilon$ at all, reproduce the round exponent exactly, which is the control that fixes the modulus as the right object to feed it}. \emph{That radius is positive and the lift occupies $-A<r<0$, so the squashing exceeds its round value along the whole of it}---$1.3747$ at the comoving turnaround and rising without bound at the close---\emph{and the stretch where a squashing below unity would raise the eigenvalues lies on the expansion side, which the transport never visits}. \emph{So the uniform bound above is not attained, and what the lift actually supplies is exact.} Dividing \eqref{eq:squashed-spectrum} by its round member gives $\lambda/L(L+2)=1-\sigma(1-\varepsilon^{-2})$ with $\sigma=4m^2/L(L+2)$, \emph{the fraction of the eigenvalue the Hopf charge carries}---so the exponent's ratio to the round one is a function of $\sigma$ alone, and $(L,|m|)=(1,\tfrac12)$ and $(6,2)$ share $\sigma=\tfrac13$ and return the same ratio to ten figures. \emph{Both endpoints are exact}: the ratio is unity at $\sigma=0$, and at $\sigma=1$ it is $\sigma_{\rm tot}^{-1}\!\int|\varepsilon|^{-1}\dd\eta=0.2591$, free of $\alpha$ by three independent routes---\emph{so the most transparent band sees an effective segment length $0.8651$ rather than $3.3387$}. \emph{And because the infimum $\lambda\ge2L$ is reached only as $\varepsilon\to\infty$, which is a set the transport does not dwell in, the exponent stays LINEAR in degree rather than turning into the bound's $\sqrt{2/L}$}: at $L=1024$ it is $934.0$ against the bound's $151.1$, the gap widening past a factor of six. \emph{What the squashing buys is a shortened segment and not a change of functional form}\rcpt{P15_the_lifts_exact_envelope_on_the_carried_layer_is_a_one_parameter_deformation_indexed_by_the_hopf_charge_and_the_uniform_bound_is_not_attained}.

\emph{The uniform floor above is the scalar sector's, and the vector sector has no counterpart to it.} The Hodge Laplacian commutes with $\dd$ and $\dd^{*}$ on any Riemannian manifold, so the gradient and co-exact subspaces are each invariant at every squashing---and the gradient sector returns \eqref{eq:squashed-spectrum} member for member, which is the control that fixes the convention. \emph{Inside the co-exact sector the deformation does reach the basis}: a $1$-form carries its own charge in the frame index, so the conserved label is the difference and the invariant blocks are the triples $\{e_{+}\!\otimes\!(q{+}1),\,e_{3}\!\otimes\!q,\,e_{-}\!\otimes\!(q{-}1)\}$, with coupling $2\sqrt2\,\varepsilon$ at $L=1$ and $4\varepsilon$ at $L=2$---proportional to the squashing itself and vanishing only in the collapsed limit. \emph{So $(L,m)$ is not carried unmixed there and the transport is a three-channel problem}, bounded at $3\times3$ and never a new infinite one; \emph{and the extreme-charge block is $1\times1$, mixing with nothing}, which is also the smallest block at large squashing and therefore available exactly where the lift goes. \emph{The transverse eigenvalue is $\lambda^{\perp}=(L+2)^2/\varepsilon^2$ exactly}---$9$, $16$, $25$, $36$, $49$, $64$ for $L=1$ to $6$---\emph{so it runs to zero as $\varepsilon\to\infty$, which is the end of the lift where the scalar floor still held.} \emph{And the suppression survives the broken floor}, because $\varepsilon\to\infty$ happens only at $r\to0$ and almost no measure sits there: carrying the exact minimum through the lift's own measure gives $2.595216$ at $L=1$---exactly $3\times0.8650719$, the scalar effective length, free of $\alpha$ to thirteen figures and with the minimiser the unmixed block, so nothing adiabatic is assumed---and $2.555$, $3.780$, $4.793$, $5.742$, $6.661$ at $L=2$ to $6$, against the most transparent scalar band's $2.447$, $3.350$, $4.238$, $5.118$, $5.993$. \emph{The exponent is linear in degree and larger than the weakest scalar band at every degree computed}, so a pointwise bound running to zero costs the damping nothing\rcpt{P15_the_vector_sectors_pointwise_bound_fails_while_its_exponent_exceeds_the_scalars_and_the_tensor_sector_has_no_transverse_traceless_spectrum_off_the_round_point}. \emph{Above $L=1$ the vector figures are a short-wavelength reading on a rotating basis and are quoted as such.}

\emph{For the tensor sector this route does not reach a spectrum at all, and the obstruction is identified rather than merely met.} The Lichnerowicz Laplacian preserves tracelessness at every squashing and \emph{generates divergence as soon as the layer stops being Einstein}---and the layer is Einstein only at the round point, its Ricci eigenvalues being $(4-2\varepsilon^2,\,4-2\varepsilon^2,\,2\varepsilon^2)$, equal if and only if $\varepsilon^2=1$. \emph{So the transverse-traceless subspace is not invariant off that point}: the generated divergence is linear in the Einstein deficit near it and is already a quarter to a third of the image at the lift's entry. \emph{What the tensor route needs is therefore a decomposition the squashed layer does not supply, and not an eigenvalue on it}---the object such an eigenvalue would belong to is not preserved by the transport. \emph{This bounds a computation this paper's own route would require, and is not a claim about the tensor sector}. \emph{And the obstruction belongs to the reduced description rather than to the geometry it reduces}: the four-metric the layer sits in is exactly Einstein, $R_{ab}=\Lambda g_{ab}$ identically with $R=4\Lambda=12/\alpha^{2}$, the mass and the throat constant free---so the traceless Ricci that drives the obstruction vanishes identically one dimension up. \emph{And the obstruction is proportional to that deficit rather than merely correlated with it}, the ratio holding to four figures across four decades of it at $0.255551$ for $L=2$ and $0.183473$ for $L=3$ with the trace at machine zero throughout, \emph{so a vanishing deficit is a vanishing obstruction and not a smaller one}. \emph{What is obstructed is therefore the layer-by-layer harmonic method, and the question is well posed in four dimensions}\rcpt{P15_po83_does_not_move_because_the_companion_avoids_the_obstruction_by_symmetry_not_by_einstein_while_the_four_metric_the_layer_sits_in_is_einstein_identically}. \emph{And the four-dimensional treatment keeps that economy, with more of it than the three-layer route has.} The four-chart's angular factor is a \emph{round} two-sphere at every radius, the areal radius its only radial dependence and no squashing parameter present on that chart at all---\emph{so moving along the curve rescales the fibre and does nothing else to it, and a round fibre has no shape to deform}. \emph{The angular dependence separates, and the reduced radial operator is identical across every azimuthal label at each degree}, with the degree entering only as $\ell(\ell+1)$: \emph{a one-label family of radial problems where the layer route is a two-label one}, since \eqref{eq:squashed-spectrum} depends on $m^2$ and needs the second label. \emph{What does not travel is the label itself}: the layer's $m=0$ harmonics are exactly the fibre-independent ones, so exactly that sector descends to the Hopf base, and there the two spectra are one number---$L(L+2)=4j(j+1)$ at $j=L/2$, identically in the degree. \emph{So the bridge is exact where it exists and absent elsewhere, and the sector it exists on is named three times over}: by the Hopf projection requiring $j=L/2$ to be an integer, by the parity rule admitting $m=0$ exactly for even $L$, and by the layer spectrum depending on the squashing only through $m^2$---\emph{which makes it the squashing-free sector as well}\rcpt{P15_the_four_dimensional_treatment_keeps_the_economy_and_keeps_more_of_it_but_in_the_sphere_label_so_the_bridge_exists_only_on_the_squashing_free_sector}. \emph{The modes the bridge does not reach carry charge under the fibre direction the degeneration closes up}, and what becomes of such a charge is a question about the degeneration rather than about this sector. \emph{And the sector the bridge does reach is thin and begins late}: the layer's multiplicity at degree $L$ is $(L+1)^2$ with $L+1$ charges carrying $L+1$ states each, so the zero-charge slice is exactly $1/(L+1)$ of the degree on even $L$ and empty on odd---$\tfrac13$, $\tfrac15$, $\tfrac17$, $\tfrac19$ at $L=2,4,6,8$---\emph{and it contains no $L=1$ mode at all}. \emph{What that exclusion does not do is reach past the sector it describes.} The parity it rests on empties the neutral slice at \emph{every} odd degree, so the degrees it removes properly contain the ones the companion's tensor tower removes---\emph{but it is a statement about which slice keeps the round eigenvalue, and not about which modes exist}. At odd degree all $L+1$ slices are present and every one of them is charged, and all are carried along the bead exactly, the harmonics conserving $(L,m)$ with nothing mixing\rcpt{P15_po75_the_supplier_is_already_in_print_and_the_floor_is_two_derivations_not_one_so_the_odd_ladder_is_the_test_that_separates_them}. \emph{Neither route by which that parity might have bounded the arriving content survives measurement.} The crossing cannot carry it: its kernel is a function of the degree alone, while $L(L+2)$ is the same on all $L+1$ charges of a degree, so the charge the parity distinguishes does not appear in the kernel at all---\emph{a function constant along the ladder cannot implement a condition that distinguishes its rungs}\rcpt{P15_order_one_answered_negative_the_crossing_cannot_share_a_measure_with_the_layers_parity_because_its_kernel_is_a_function_of_the_degree_and_the_parity_is_a_condition_on_the_charge}. \emph{And composition cannot carry it either}: the charged eigenvalue diverges where the squashing \emph{vanishes}, and the normalised squashing attains its minimum $1.3747$ at the comoving turnaround and rises from there to the close, \emph{so the divergence lives on the leg the transport never traverses}.

\emph{So the odd ladder arrives, and it arrives with a number rather than a sign.} The exponent's ratio to the round one is a function of $\sigma=4m^2/L(L+2)$ alone---two modes of different degree sharing $\sigma$ agree to ten figures---and at the lowest odd degree it gives an exponent of $4.868603$ against $5.782864$ for a round mode of that degree: \emph{exponentially small, non-zero, and less suppressed than the round mode by a margin that grows with degree}\rcpt{P15_the_composition_route_is_answered_negative_too_because_the_eigenvalue_divergence_lives_on_the_leg_the_transport_never_traverses_and_the_odd_ladder_arrives_suppressed_rather_than_absent}. \emph{And the projection carries that degree lower in the sky than the even floor.} The window in which \eqref{eq:lowell} is quasi-injective is two degrees wide, so $L=1$ sits inside the mode spacing comfortably where $L=2$ sits inside it by less, and the two land at $\ell\simeq3$ and $\ell\simeq6$---three multipoles apart, and stable across the range of the stretch this paper admits. \emph{With the even floor's own neutral slice at exponent $9.443377$, the gap is $e^{4.57}$ in the odd degree's favour}, so \emph{the lowest multipoles the projection reaches draw on the degree a parity bound would have removed}, and exponent and modal multipole both rise monotonically over the computed degrees, so no higher degree reaches below it\rcpt{P15_the_projection_join_inverts_the_expected_picture_because_the_odd_degree_lands_lower_than_the_even_floor_and_arrives_less_suppressed_so_the_lowest_multipoles_draw_on_the_degree_the_bound_would_have_removed}. \emph{What that locates is a window and not a spectrum}: these are exponents and not amplitudes, the charged slice's eigenvalue moving along the path so that no prefactor follows from a ratio of exponents; one slice is carried per degree; and the primordial weights a $C_\ell$ would sum against are fixed nowhere in this construction, which is the datum \S\ref{sec:scope} names as inherited rather than derived.

\emph{What the bridge does not carry across is the measure.} On an Einstein four-metric the Lichnerowicz curvature term splits, and while the Ricci part is proportional to the tensor and shifts the problem by a constant, \emph{the Riemann part is not, because this background is not conformally flat}: the Kretschmann scalar is $48M^2/r^6+24/\alpha^4$, the second term exactly the $\Lambda$-only value $8\Lambda^2/3$, \emph{so the Weyl invariant is $48M^2/r^6$---mass-driven, and divergent as $r\to0$, which is the close of the lift}. \emph{At the forced mass the ratio of the two is $2\alpha^6/27r^6$, exactly one half at the comoving turnaround}---a closed value at the lift's own entry---\emph{then $32$ at half that radius and $5\times10^{11}$ at a hundredth of it}. \emph{So it is nowhere a small correction on the segment the transport traverses, and the layer's cross-section problem carries no counterpart to it}: a tensor exponent on this background is the Regge--Wheeler reduction with that coupling carried through the radial problem, and not an extension of the measure the scalar and vector exponents are computed on\rcpt{P15_the_bridge_is_exact_on_the_spectrum_and_the_transport_is_not_shared_because_the_four_route_carries_a_weyl_coupling_the_layer_has_no_counterpart_for}. \emph{That computation belongs with the tensor sector itself}, which \S\ref{sec:intro} places in the companion dynamics paper~\cite{JanzenDynamics}.

\emph{So the throat's isotropization is not only the standard theorem read on the near-horizon geometry; it is a damping factor computed on the construction's own curve, with no $\Lambda$, $H_0$, $\Omega_m$, mass or epoch in exponent or prefactor}---\emph{the seam is isotropic by identity rather than by tuning}. The guard above applies to this route in the same form and with the same force: $L$ here is the degree on the cosmological layer, and the observable multipole is reached only through the map of \S\ref{sec:largescale}\rcpt{P15_the_angular_label_crosses_the_lift_untouched_and_the_amplitude_does_not_because_the_lift_is_purely_imaginary_conformal_time_so_what_it_adds_is_an_envelope_with_exactly_no_phase}. The map between them is \S\ref{sec:largescale}, not an identity; conflating them would manufacture a prediction the geometry does not make. \emph{And \S\ref{sec:largescale} measures how far from an identity it is, which bounds the degrees over which an exponent in $L$ may be read as one in $\ell$}: the projection is quasi-injective while its width stays inside the mode spacing, which holds at $L=1$ and $L=2$ and fails from $L=3$ up. \emph{So the exponential-in-degree statement above transfers to the sky for the first two degrees---where this route's own figures are computed---and above them becomes a statement about the lowest contributing degree instead}, since a steep envelope carried through a smearing map is dominated by the bottom edge of each band. \emph{The functional form is not preserved there, and that is a limit on how the envelope may be read rather than a correction to it.} \emph{And a second guard belongs to this route in particular, because it is the premise that makes the expansion possible}: carrying $(L,m)$ along the bead needs a harmonic decomposition of the layer at every point of the curve, the lift included---\emph{and the layer there is squashed rather than round}, \S\ref{sec:largescale} measuring its squashing as $\sqrt{-f}/r$. \emph{The decomposition exists and is the same one}, because the squashed layer is a left-invariant metric on the same group manifold: that is what \eqref{eq:squashed-spectrum} above records, and it is why the transport carries $(L,m)$ unmixed. \emph{The guard that remains is narrower and is worth stating as such}: the round value $L(L+2)$ is the $\varepsilon=1$ member and not the generic one, so a damping quoted at $L(L+2)$ is a statement about a horn and not about the lift, and what the lift supplies is the uniform bound. \emph{The isotropization conclusion does not rest on this route in any case}: the near-horizon one needs nothing of the cosmological layer, resting on the throat geometry of Proposition~\ref{prop:throat} and the de~Sitter no-hair theorem alone. \emph{That the two agree in direction, by mechanisms sharing neither a decomposition nor a theorem, is what makes the second worth computing}. \emph{A second guard belongs beside it, and it bounds what the tower damps.} The computation above is a statement about propagating field modes---degrees of freedom carrying a mass on the $\dS_2$ factor, which is what being heavy principal-series means. \emph{The low multipoles of a perturbation about a spherically symmetric background are not such modes}: $\ell=0$ carries the mass and the axial $\ell=1$ carries the angular momentum, the latter a stationary slow-rotation configuration rather than an oscillation, with the radiative content beginning at $\ell\ge2$---the multipole face of the non-radiative skeleton the range paper describes~\cite{JanzenRange}. \emph{So what the throat isotropises is the radiative content, and a conserved charge sitting in a constrained sector is not among the modes this argument is about}\rcpt{P15_the_angular_momentum_sector_is_not_a_propagating_mode}. \emph{This is a bound on the claim and not a claim in the other direction}: that the angular momentum is not removed \emph{by this mechanism} says nothing about whether it is carried off by matter or torqued on the way, which this paper does not settle. \emph{And the geometry the tower is computed on is itself a non-rotating one}, Proposition~\ref{prop:throat} following directly from $f$ at the Nariai mass. \emph{So it is worth measuring how much the conclusion rests on that.} Writing $\lambda$ for the ratio of the $\dS_2$ curvature radius squared to the $S^2$ one, the index generalises to $\nu^2=\tfrac14-\lambda\,\ell(\ell+1)$, with $\lambda=1$ the equal-radii case above. \emph{The monopole is untouched}: $\nu^2=\tfrac14$ for every $\lambda$, so the scale-invariant base does not depend on the radii being equal. \emph{And the damping persists well away from it}: every $\ell\ge1$ remains heavy principal-series for $\lambda>1/4\ell(\ell+1)$, which at $\ell=1$ is $\lambda>1/8$---the $\dS_2$ radius would have to fall below $0.354$ of the $S^2$ radius, a factor near three, before the dipole ceased to decay, with the higher multipoles safer still\rcpt{P15_the_tower_is_robust_to_the_throat_being_squashed}. \emph{What ratio a rotating progenitor would produce is not computed here}, and a rotating near-horizon geometry is generally warped rather than a direct product, so a single ratio may not describe it; what the margin establishes is that the conclusion does not turn on the equal-radii point itself. \emph{The ratio can in fact be computed on the rotating family, and it runs the helpful way.} Kerr--de~Sitter's radial function $\Delta_r=(r^2+a^2)(1-r^2/\alpha^2)-2Mr$ reduces at $a=0$ to $r^2f(r)$, and its degenerate locus $\Delta_r=\Delta_r'=0$ gives $a^2=r^2(\alpha^2-3r^2)/(\alpha^2+r^2)$, which returns $r_{N}=\alpha/\sqrt3$ and the Nariai mass at $a=0$ rather than presupposing them. \emph{The family is bounded}: $a$ reaches $(2-\sqrt3)\alpha$ at $r/\alpha=0.3933$, where $\Delta_r''$ vanishes and the double root becomes triple---the ultracold member. \emph{Along it the ratio begins at unity and rises}: $\lambda=1$ exactly in the static case and $\lambda>1$ monotonically with $a$, so rotation carries the throat away from the $1/8$ threshold rather than toward it\rcpt{P15_rotation_moves_the_throat_away_from_the_damping_threshold}. \emph{That ratio is read at the pole, where the sphere's radius is $r_N$}; the rotating near-horizon sphere is warped through $r_N^2+a^2\cos^2\theta$ and a single ratio does not describe it, so what the calculation establishes is the direction and its size at one well-defined point, with a factor of eight in hand at the outset.

\emph{The warped problem is done directly, and does not need the proxy.} The
near-horizon limit of the degenerate family, taken as a limit rather than posited, is a warped
product and not a direct one: $\rho_0^{2}=r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta$ multiplies the $\dS_2$ and
polar directions alike, and the $\dS_2$ is fibred over the sphere by
$\tilde k=2r_{0}a\Xi/(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2})$, which vanishes only at $a=0$. A massless field still
separates on it, the warp cancelling from the relation between the transverse eigenvalue and the
$\dS_2$ operator because it multiplies both. \emph{For the axisymmetric modes it cancels
altogether}: the transverse problem is $(\Delta_\theta\sin\theta\,Y')'+E\sin\theta\,Y=0$ with
$\Delta_\theta=1+(a/\alpha)^{2}\cos^{2}\theta$---a spheroidal deformation of Legendre in which
$\rho_0$ does not appear at all, so the harmonic label survives the warping rather than being
spoiled by it. \emph{The decay condition generalises to}
$\nu^{2}=\tfrac14-L^{2}E-L^{4}m^{2}\tilde k^{2}$, the last term the rotation's own fibration, which
is non-negative and so can only deepen the damping. \emph{Two exact bounds then settle the family
without a scan}: $L^{2}\ge1$ on the physical branch and $E\ge2$ above the monopole, each with
equality only at $a=0$, so $L^{2}E\ge2$ against the threshold $\tfrac14$. \emph{The monopole keeps
$\nu^{2}=\tfrac14$ exactly on the warped sphere too.} \emph{So the conclusion holds across the whole
family $0\le a<a_{\max}$ with the same factor of eight, and the marginal member is the
non-rotating one}---rotation strictly improves the case rather than threatening
it\rcpt{P15_the_warped_throat_keeps_every_mode_above_the_monopole_decaying}.

\section{Large scales: the flat/discrete decoupling and the low-multipole floor}\label{sec:largescale}

Here the construction makes its sharpest large-scale statement, and the load-bearing piece is established.

\begin{proposition}[The fundamental-observer slice is flat]\label{prop:flat}
In the fundamental-observer frame~\eqref{eq:proper-frame}, a surface of constant $\tau$ has induced three-metric $dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2$ (since $(\partial_\chi r)^2d\chi^2=dr^2$ along the slice), whose Riemann tensor vanishes identically. The constant-$\tau$ slice is exactly flat $\mathbb{R}^3$.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\emph{Argument.} The substitution is immediate from the line element; the curvature of $dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2$ is zero by direct computation (receipts: \texttt{verify\_geometry.py}, anchor~1\rcpt{P15_verify_geometry}). This is the framework paper's own stated property that constant-$\tau$ slices are Euclidean. $\qquad\square$

The decoupling follows. The distance slicing is the flat constant-$\tau$ one---$\Omega_k=0$, no curvature term in the redshift--distance relation---while the \emph{cosmological} layers are the closed $S^3$, labelled by constant $\tilde\tau=\tau+\chi$ and read as a sphere in the de~Sitter presentation (\S\ref{sec:properframe}). \emph{The attribution matters and is stated rather than left to the reader}: on the reassigned chart of \eqref{eq:proper-frame} a constant-$\tilde\tau$ surface carries the induced metric $-f(r)\,\dd\chi^2+r^2\dd\Omega^2$, whose intrinsic Ricci eigenvalues are $(0,1/r^2,1/r^2)$---a flat line times a round $S^2$ of radius $r(\tilde\tau)$, not a three-sphere---which is what the reassignment does and is the same fact as the $45^\circ$ picture of \S\ref{sec:properframe}. \emph{The line's causal character is the congruence's own speed, and it is not the same along the whole curve}: the $E=1$, $k=0$ radial equation gives $(\partial_\chi r)^2=2M/r+r^2/\alpha^2$ identically, so the $\chi$-block coefficient $-f=(\partial_\chi r)^2-1$ is positive where the congruence exceeds unit areal speed and negative where it falls below it, vanishing at the two loci where it is unit. \emph{At the Nariai mass the cubic factorises exactly},
\begin{equation}
-f=\frac{(r-\alpha/\sqrt3)^2\,(r+2\alpha/\sqrt3)}{\alpha^2 r},
\label{eq:chi-block-factored}
\end{equation}
\emph{a double root at $r=+\alpha/\sqrt3$ and a simple one at $r=-2\alpha/\sqrt3$---which are the lap's two seams}, necessarily so, since $-f=0$ and $|\partial_\chi r|=1$ are the same condition. \emph{So the sign of $-f$ is the sign of $(r+2\alpha/\sqrt3)/r$, and the surface is spacelike on both legs and Lorentzian through the whole interior of the lap}---$-1$ exactly at the comoving turnaround, where $\partial_\chi r$ vanishes and the surface is a unit-timelike line times $S^2(A)$. \emph{The flat-line reading is therefore the signature outside the seams}\rcpt{P15_the_layer_is_R_times_S2_on_the_reassigned_chart_and_the_two_presentations_are_two_metrics_on_one_layering_related_by_the_null_reassignment}. The non-synchrony $\tilde\tau=\tau+\chi$ is the decoupling itself: flat distances and a closed $S^3$ of comoving worldlines coexist, the one read on the constant-$\tau$ slicing and the other on the layer the same construction labels by $\tilde\tau$. \emph{And the relation between those two readings is stated exactly, because it is not the one a reader will assume}: they are \emph{not} two coordinate presentations of a single metric, and nothing here claims they are. \emph{The two are reached from one another by the reassignment of the null condition}---the operation that promotes one null bundle of the cosmological horn to the fundamental timelike congruence (\S\ref{sec:properframe})---\emph{so they are two metrics carried on one ontological layering, isometric through that reassignment and not through any change of coordinates}. \emph{What is common to them, and what makes the layering one object, is the foliation itself}: the cosmic layers are the surfaces of constant areal radius, on the de~Sitter horns and inside the lap alike, and it is the growing-$r$ foliation that begins at the branch point where $r$ and $\tilde\tau$ vanish together. \emph{The difference between a flat reading and a curved one is therefore nothing but which bundle is called at rest}, and the lift's purely imaginary conformal time---the Euclidean segment of \S\ref{sec:scope}---\emph{is what keeps that reassignment well defined across the lap}. \emph{One consequence is worth stating so that it is not mistaken for a defect later}: a curvature invariant computed in one presentation and compared with the other registers that the reassignment is not a diffeomorphism, which the construction asserts of it, and is therefore not evidence about where the layer's sphere lives\rcpt{P15_the_layer_is_R_times_S2_on_the_reassigned_chart_and_the_two_presentations_are_two_metrics_on_one_layering_related_by_the_null_reassignment}. \emph{What the de~Sitter presentation displays plainly is the layer outside the seam, before the lap and after it}; inside the lap the seam buries the explicit form, and the sphere's character there is reached by analytic continuation along the bead rather than read off a chart. \emph{The foliation is the constant-$r$ one at every point of the curve, and the layer's SIZE continues across the lap with it; its SHAPE does not}. \emph{That is settled below rather than assumed in either direction}, by carrying the sphere the de~Sitter presentation displays at a horn along the flow and measuring what arrives. \emph{What the construction does supply is a sharp test for it, and the test's own discriminating power is measured rather than assumed.} A round $S^3$ of radius $r$ has scalar curvature $\mathcal R=6/r^2$ and volume $V=2\pi^2r^3$, so the scale-free combination
\begin{equation}
\mathcal R\,V^{2/3}=6(2\pi^2)^{2/3}=43.8232\ldots
\label{eq:shape-invariant}
\end{equation}
carries no $r$ at all---it cancels symbolically---so it is insensitive to the layer's size and reports only its shape. \emph{And it does discriminate}: on a Berger sphere of squashing $\varepsilon$ the same combination is $2\cdot2^{2/3}\pi^{4/3}\varepsilon^{2/3}(4-\varepsilon^2)$, which varies with the squashing and takes the round value only at $\varepsilon=1$. \emph{That point is a strict maximum of the combination and its first derivative there vanishes}, the second being $-77.9$, \emph{so the test's sensitivity to a departure from roundness is second order in the squashing}---sharp enough to discriminate, and weaker against a near miss than a reader would assume of it. \emph{So \eqref{eq:shape-invariant} is the quantity a demonstration of the continuation would have to return, evaluated on a layer metric obtained from the construction rather than written down}---and what remains owed is exactly that: the three-metric of the layer inside the lap carried from the sphere the
